{"id": "b444abcc41c4826d", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T01:04:27.001067+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Complete UNVERIFIED common-prefix policy screen: an explicit full-support dependent measure with epsilon=2^-m, eta=2^-2m and strictly nonsaturating positive amplitudes passes every adjacent cocoercivity sum and every bridge-plus-leaf odd-endpoint sum, yet for every original H_m with m>=65536 one of Q_(0,1024), Q_(1024,0), Q_(3,1027), Q_(1027,3) exceeds1/50 for every anchor and every choice of values. This excludes the specified amplitude law, not all amplitudes of the common-prefix measure.", "evidence": "# Common-prefix characters: strict local slack and a remote-pair obstruction\n\nStatus: **self-contained, unverified joint candidate from the user-directed\ncontinuation after the prior seventeen-round launch**. No solver, parameter\nsweep, installation, fact submission, new launch, or finalization is used.\nThe sole correctness predecessor is accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`, for\nthe necessity and orientation of the original interpolation inequalities.\nEarlier common-prefix findings motivate the construction; they are not proof\npremises. All identities needed below are derived here.\n\nThis specifies one new dependent, horizon-dependent probability law and one\npositive amplitude law with strict local backoff. The latter is impossible:\nfour original remote interpolation inequalities cannot all hold. It does\n**not** exclude every amplitude law for this probability distribution, and\ndoes not establish either resolution of the original minimax problem.\n\n## 1. Complete finite policy and the claim\n\nFix an integer m>=65536. Set\n\n    sigma=1+sqrt(2), delta=1-1/m, c_chr=sigma(1+1/m),\n    a_chr=delta/c_chr, N=2^m, n=N-1,\n    epsilon=1/N, eta=1/N^2,\n    l=1+a_chr, p_chr=1-epsilon, alpha=1-2epsilon,\n    beta=7/10, D_chr=1+l p_chr,\n    u_k=delta c_chr^(k-1), k>=1.\n\nThe actual algorithm uses\n\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_chr c_chr^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.\n\nEquivalently, its original step i is\nh_i=1+a_chr c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n. A leaf has nu_2(i+1)=0\nand step l; a level-k bridge, k>=1, has step 1+u_k. Every subword below\nretains the same enclosing-m parameters. No chronological index, step,\nphysical horizon, or benchmark is changed.\n\nGive sample i its standard m-bit binary label (i_0,...,i_(m-1)). For a\ncharacter index s=(s_0,...,s_(m-1)) in {0,1}^m define\n\n    chi_s(i)=(-1)^(sum_j s_j i_j),\n    B(s)=(-1)^s_0,\n    C_j(s)=(-1)^(s_1+...+s_j), 1<=j<m.\n\nThe map s -> (B,C_1,...,C_(m-1)) is bijective: (-1)^s_1=C_1 and\n(-1)^s_j=C_(j-1)C_j for j>=2. Thus the following specifies EVERY atom.\nWrite mu_1=-7/10 and mu_(-1)=9/10, and for b in {-1,1} put\n\n    A_b(c_1,...,c_(m-1))\n       =[(1+mu_b)/2] 1_(all c_j=1)\n          +[(1-mu_b)/2] 1_(all c_j=-1),\n    P_b(c_1,...,c_(m-1))=product_(j=1)^(m-1)(1+mu_b c_j)/2,\n\n    w_s=[(1+p_chr B(s))/2]\n          [(1-eta) A_(B(s))(C_1(s),...,C_(m-1)(s))\n              +eta P_(B(s))(C_1(s),...,C_(m-1)(s))].       (1)\n\nHere 1_(condition) equals one or zero according to the displayed condition.\nEach conditional factor is a probability law. Their mixture sums to one;\nevery w_s is strictly positive since epsilon,eta>0 and |mu_b|<1.\nThe first component makes ALL prefix signs equal, including the two lowest\nones. The second component gives full support at a stated rate.\n\nDefine the positive odd-to-odd amplitude ratios\n\n    q_1=47/100, q_2=99/100, q_3=21/10,\n    q_4=9/2, q_5=99/10,\n    q_k=(69/100)u_k/D_chr, k>=6.                          (2)\n\nThe amplitudes of EVERY original sample are fixed before the trajectory:\n\n    Gamma_0=1,\n    Gamma_(i+1)=alpha Gamma_i                  at a leaf,\n    Gamma_(i+1)=(q_k/alpha) Gamma_i             at a level-k bridge.\n                                                                    (3)\n\nPrescribe the actual real vectors in R^N\n\n    g_i=(Gamma_i sqrt(w_s) chi_s(i))_s, 0<=i<=n.            (4)\n\nFor any common anchor X_0 and ANY real values f_0,...,f_n, define the original\npositions and residuals\n\n    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i) h_t g_t,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.            (5)\n\nThe claim has two parts.\n\n* Every adjacent cocoercivity sum Q_(i,i+1)+Q_(i+1,i) is strictly negative.\n  Every sum between the two odd endpoints of a bridge and its following\n  leaf is also strictly negative. These sums do not depend on f or X_0.\n* Let K=2^10=1024. At least one of\n\n      Q_(0,K), Q_(K,0), Q_(3,K+3), Q_(K+3,3)              (6)\n\n  is greater than 1/50, for EVERY f and EVERY X_0.\n\nThus (1)--(4) cannot be the gradient transcript of one convex differentiable\n1-smooth function, with or without a minimizer. The first assertion is only\nabout necessary pair sums; it does not assert interpolation from local tests.\nThe second assertion retains four actual ordered inequalities and is a\nuniform, finite-horizon obstruction to this entire specified policy.\n\n## 2. Exact kernel moments and bounds\n\nFor a binary difference d=i xor j, put d_m=0. Inserting the inverse relations\nbetween s and its prefix signs gives\n\n    chi_s(d)=B(s)^d_0 product_(j=1)^(m-1) C_j(s)^(d_j xor d_(j+1)),\n    <g_i,g_j>/(Gamma_i Gamma_j)=E chi_s(i xor j).            (7)\n\nExpectations here are finite sums against (1). Conditional on B=b, every\nC_j has mean mu_b in BOTH components of (1). Consequently\n\n    E B=p_chr=1-epsilon,\n    E C_k=z_chr=-7/10+4epsilon/5,\n    E[B C_k]=w_chr=-7/10-epsilon/10.                       (8)\n\nAt a bridge of level k and its following leaf, the relative character\nsigns at the three ORIGINAL samples are 1,B C_k,C_k. Their correlations\nare w_chr,p_chr,z_chr, respectively. At a leaf, the adjacent correlation is\np_chr. Multiplication by any starting character cancels in all these\ninner products, so these identities hold at every occurrence.\n\nThis law lies outside the previous low-joint-moment chart:\n\n    E C_1 C_2=1-eta+eta(49/100+4epsilon/25)>1/2.            (9)\n\nTo check (9), the common component has product one, and the independent\ncomponent has conditional product mu_B^2. Averaging over B gives\n49/100+4epsilon/25. The final inequality uses eta<=10^(-6).\n\nWe use only the following rational bounds:\n\n    0<epsilon<=10^(-6), 9999/10000<delta<1,\n    12/5<c_chr<2417/1000,\n    1413/1000<l<283/200,\n    12/5<D_chr<483/200,\n    999/1000<alpha<1, alpha>=1-2*10^(-6).                  (10)\n\nFor completeness 707/500<sqrt(2)<99/70 follows by squaring positive\nrationals. Hence c_chr>1207/500>12/5 and, using m>=1024,\nc_chr<(169/70)(1025/1024)<2417/1000. Also\na_chr>(70/169)(1023/1025)>413/1000 and\na_chr<sqrt(2)-1<29/70<83/200, giving the bounds on l.\nThen D_chr>1+(1413/1000)(999999/1000000)>12/5 and D_chr<483/200.\nThe stronger lower bound on delta follows from m>=65536>10000.\nThe bounds on epsilon and alpha follow directly from their definitions.\n\nDefine D_tilde=1+l p_chr/alpha and C_tilde=-z_chr-l w_chr/alpha.\nThe same inequalities give\n\n    D_tilde<4831/2000,\n    C_tilde<1691/1000,\n    z_chr<-699999/1000000,\n    D_tilde/D_chr<=1/alpha<1001/1000,\n    1/alpha<100001/100000.                                (11)\n\nFor the first two, use l/alpha<(283/200)/(1-2*10^(-6)),\n-z_chr<=7/10, and -w_chr<=7/10+10^(-7). The ratio bound follows from\nD_tilde=D_chr/alpha+1-1/alpha<=D_chr/alpha. Each strict numerical comparison\nin (11) is a rational inequality with positive denominators.\n\n## 3. Strict local slack without saturation\n\nAccepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290` states that an actual smooth-convex\ntranscript must obey every Q_ij<=0, with exactly the sign convention (5).\nFor adjacent samples with step 1+u, unit-direction correlation d, and\npositive norm ratio r, adding its two original inequalities gives\n\n    [Q_(i,i+1)+Q_(i+1,i)]/Gamma_i^2\n          =r^2+(u-1)d r-u.                                (12)\n\nAt a leaf u=a_chr, d=p_chr, r=alpha. Substitution yields\n\n    alpha^2+(a_chr-1)p_chr alpha-a_chr\n       =-(1+3a_chr)epsilon+(2+2a_chr)epsilon^2<0.           (13)\n\nNow let u=u_k and q=q_k. The bridge has next norm ratio q/alpha;\nthe following leaf produces norm ratio q from the original odd starting\nsample. Directly expanding the pair sum between those odd endpoints gives\n\n    [Q_(before,after)+Q_(after,before)]/Gamma_before^2\n       =D_tilde q^2+(z_chr u+C_tilde)q-u.                  (14)\n\nThe intervening gradient has norm (q/alpha)Gamma_before and is included:\nX_after-X_before=-(1+u)g_before-l g_middle. Substituting (8) into this\nexact displacement gives (14), not an identification or deletion of the\nmiddle vector.\n\nFor k=1,...,5 define the explicit lower bound\n\n    u_low(k)=(9999/10000)(12/5)^(k-1).\n\nBy (10),(11), (14) is bounded above by\n\n    (4831/2000)q_k^2\n       +[1691/1000-(699999/1000000)u_low(k)]q_k-u_low(k).   (15)\n\nAll five rational expressions (15) are negative. Their negativity can be\nchecked by inserting the five fractions (2), multiplying by the positive\ndenominators, and comparing integers. The accompanying fixed audit records\nthese five comparisons; no optimization or parameter search is involved.\n\nFor the bridge alone at k=1, put r_hi=(100001/100000)q_1. Since u_1<1,\n(12) is at most\n\n    r_hi^2+(700001/1000000)(1-u_low(1))r_hi-u_low(1)<0.     (16)\n\nFor k=2,...,5, where u_k>1, replace only the positive square upwards and\nthe negative product downwards. The resulting upper bound is\n\n    [(100001/100000)q_k]^2\n           -(7/10)(u_low(k)-1)q_k-u_low(k)<0.             (17)\n\nAgain these are four fixed rational comparisons, supplied by the fractions\nin (2). There is no saturation at any of these levels.\n\nFor k>=6, write gamma=69/100, so q=gamma u/D_chr. In (14), (11) gives\n\n    D_tilde q^2+z_chr u q\n       <=[(1001/1000)(69/100)-699/1000]u q<0,\n    C_tilde q-u<[(1691/1000)(69/100)/(12/5)-1]u<0.         (18)\n\nThis proves strictness for every such odd endpoint comparison, including\narbitrarily high bridges of the ORIGINAL horizon. For the bridge itself,\nr=q/alpha<(3/10)u and u>=u_low(6)>2. Since w_chr<=-7/10,\n\n    r^2+(u-1)w_chr r-u\n       <-(4/10)u r+(7/10)r-u<0.                          (19)\n\nEquations (13)--(19) prove all stated local slacks. They do not rely on a\nmaximal adjacent root, nor on either low joint moment being at most 1/2.\n\n## 4. An expanded copy inside the original fixed prefix\n\nLet J_0=() and J_j=(J_(j-1),j,J_(j-1)) be a list of bridge levels. This\nonly indexes odd-to-odd transitions, with both actual steps retained in\n(3) and (14). Set\n\n    T_0=1, T_j=q_j T_(j-1)^2.                             (20)\n\nCounting the transitions before sample 2^j-1 gives\nGamma_(2^j-1)=alpha T_(j-1). The next bridge has level j, so\n\n    Gamma_(2^j)=q_j T_(j-1).                              (21)\n\nFor j=10 write r=Gamma_K with K=1024. We show r>2 using small rational\nlower bounds alone. Formula (2) and (10) give\n\n    q_6>(69/100)(9999/10000)(12/5)^5/(483/200)>4549/200.\n\nSince q_(k+1)/q_k=c_chr>12/5 for k>=6, valid lower bounds for q_6,...,q_10\nare, in order,\n\n    227/10, 109/2, 131, 314, 754.                         (22)\n\nStarting with T_0=1, lower bounds for T_1,...,T_9 are\n\n    47/100, 21869/100000, 10043/100000,\n    4538/100000, 2038/100000, 942/100000,\n    483/100000, 305/100000, 292/100000.                    (23)\n\nFor each entry its predecessor squared, multiplied by the corresponding\nq in (2) or the lower bound (22), is at least that entry. These nine\ninequalities are integer comparisons after clearing denominators. Thus\n\n    r=q_10 T_9>754*(292/100000)>2.                         (24)\n\nThe first three steps after K have levels 0,1,0, exactly as at the start.\nConsequently the first and repeated four-sample amplitude patterns are\n\n    (Gamma_0,Gamma_1,Gamma_2,Gamma_3)=(1,alpha,q_1,alpha q_1),\n    (Gamma_K,Gamma_(K+1),Gamma_(K+2),Gamma_(K+3))\n                        =r(1,alpha,q_1,alpha q_1).        (25)\n\nAll these are actual indices well before n. No terminal padding, restart,\nrescaling of the second subproblem's physical parameters, or new algorithm\nis involved.\n\n## 5. Matched original pairs in the exact reference Gram\n\nIntroduce an AUXILIARY reference probability: B=1 and C_1=...=C_(m-1)=V,\nwhere P(V=1)=3/20 and P(V=-1)=17/20. Its direction vectors repeat\n\n    e_+, e_+, e_-, e_-, ...,\n    e_+=(sqrt(3/20),sqrt(17/20)),\n    e_-=(sqrt(3/20),-sqrt(17/20)),\n    <e_+,e_->=-beta.                                     (26)\n\nIndeed only character bit s_1 can be nonzero under this measure.\nUse exactly the SAME amplitudes (3), steps, values, and sample indices\nas in the finite policy. Only the direction Gram is changed in this\nauxiliary calculation. It is not assumed legal or obtained by a limiting\nargument.\n\nLet Z=X_K-X_0 in this reference transcript. Positive amplitudes and steps\ngive\n\n    Z=-P_+ e_+-P_- e_-, P_+,P_->=0.                       (27)\n\nPut q=q_1, g=alpha q, and\n\n    A_len=l+(1+delta)alpha, B_len=l q,\n    d_3=A_len e_++B_len e_-.\n\nBy the first three actual updates, X_3=X_0-d_3. By (25),\nX_(K+3)=X_K-r d_3, and the two matched gradient pairs are\n(e_+,r e_+) and (g e_-,r g e_-).\n\nWrite R_i^0=Q_(i,K+i)^0+Q_(K+i,i)^0 for i=0,3, where the superscript\nmarks only this reference Gram. Adding the two original ordered residuals\ncancels all values and gives\n\n    R_0^0=(r-1)[(r-1)-<e_+,Z>],\n    R_3^0=(r-1)g[(r-1)(g+<e_-,d_3>)-<e_-,Z>].            (28)\n\nWith the POSITIVE weight beta/g, their sum is EXACTLY\n\n    S^0=R_0^0+(beta/g)R_3^0\n       =(r-1)[(r-1)kappa+(1-beta^2)P_+],                 (29)\n    kappa=1+beta q(alpha+l)-beta^2[l+(1+delta)alpha].\n\nTo check the final term, <e_++beta e_-,e_->=0 and\n<e_++beta e_-,e_+>=1-beta^2. Substituting (27) therefore gives\n-<e_++beta e_-,Z>=(1-beta^2)P_+>=0. This is the specific geometric\ninformation provided by the common-prefix law.\n\nUsing (10), q=47/100, and (1+delta)alpha<2,\n\n    kappa>1+(7/10)(47/100)(999/1000+1413/1000)\n               -(49/100)(283/200+2)\n         =60099/500000>3/25.                             (30)\n\nEquations (24),(27),(29) imply S^0>3/25. This lower bound depends on\nneither anchor nor values. It is a sum of four original ordered residuals,\nnot a chosen objective, a norm to the origin, or a surrogate distance.\n\nThe same exact identity yields a useful narrower interface: in the\ntwo-ray reference, ANY repeated pattern (1,alpha,q,alpha q) with kappa>0\ncannot expand by a factor r>1 after positive steps, regardless of all the\nother amplitudes. With the parameter bounds (10), every q>=2/5 has\n\n    kappa>1+(7/10)(2/5)(999/1000+1413/1000)\n                  -(49/100)(283/200+2)=201/100000>0.       (31)\n\nThe finite full-support statement proved next concerns the definite law\n(2),(3); (31) alone is not a general perturbation theorem for arbitrary\namplitudes or arbitrarily late repeated blocks.\n\n## 6. Finite full support: explicit perturbation bound\n\nThe weight of the B=1 common component in (1) is\n(1-epsilon/2)(1-eta). Conditional on this component the measure is exactly\n(26). Hence for ALL binary labels i,j, the normalized kernels satisfy\n\n    |K_ij-K_ij^0|<=epsilon+2eta=:d_err<=2epsilon.           (32)\n\nThis follows by writing the probability as its displayed reference\ncomponent plus the remaining probability. Every character product is in\n[-1,1], so the difference of expectations is at most twice the remaining\nmass. It is a direct finite probability bound, including high binary\nlabels; no condition number or inverse Gram is used.\n\nHere are deliberately conservative bounds on the fixed prefix through K+3.\nThe largest bridge level there is ten. Since c_chr<5/2,\n\n    u_k<(5/2)^9<2^12, h_i<2^13,\n    q_k<2^12, q_k/alpha<2^13                       (k<=10).\n\nThere are fewer than K+4 updates before any of those samples. Thus, putting\n\n    U=2^(14(K+4)),\n\nwe have Gamma_i<U there, and\n\n    sum_(t<K+3) h_t Gamma_t<(K+4)2^13 U<2^24 U.            (33)\n\nThese loose bounds are not computational estimates; they follow by\nmultiplying the scalar ratio bounds. Their only purpose is to make the\nfinite perturbation cost explicit.\n\nFor any two samples i<j in this prefix, their pair sum has the original\nformula\n\n    R_ij=||g_i-g_j||^2\n             -sum_(t=i)^(j-1) h_t <g_i-g_j,g_t>.          (34)\n\nThe diagonal norm terms are unchanged because both probability measures\nsum to one. Equations (32),(33) give\n\n    |R_ij-R_ij^0|\n       <=2d_err Gamma_i Gamma_j\n           +d_err(Gamma_i+Gamma_j)sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t Gamma_t\n       <2^26 d_err U^2.                                 (35)\n\nUse the SAME positive coefficient beta/g on the finite-policy pair sum,\n\n    S=R_(0,K)+(beta/g)R_(3,K+3).\n\nSince alpha>999/1000 and q=47/100, beta/g<3/2. In particular its total\nweight is less than four, so (35) yields\n\n    |S-S^0|<2^28 d_err U^2\n              <=2^(29-m+28(K+4))=2^(28813-m)<2^(-100).    (36)\n\nThe final inequality holds for every original m>=65536. Although very\nconservative, it is uniform in the original horizon. Combining (30),(36),\n\n    S>3/25-2^(-100)>11/100.                              (37)\n\nIf all four ordered residuals in (6) were at most 1/50, their positive\nweighted sum would be at most\n\n    [2+2beta/g]/50<5/50=1/10,\n\ncontradicting (37). At least one is therefore greater than 1/50, as claimed.\nEvery function value and every common anchor was arbitrary. Positive defects\nhave not been hidden in a vanishing perturbation or discarded as negligible.\n\nOne can also set epsilon=eta=0 in the definitions and take alpha=1. The\nreference and proposed Gram then coincide; (20)--(30) give the obstruction\ndirectly at that boundary, while the leaf pair sums equal zero. The positive\nfinite laws are already excluded by (32)--(37), independently of any\ncontinuity claim at the singular measure.\n\n## 7. Scope, checks, and the remaining policy problem\n\nThe explicit law has positive weights at every finite horizon, depends on\nthe ORIGINAL horizon through epsilon and eta, couples the two lowest\nprefix signs, and uses nonsaturating positive amplitudes. {"id": "d4ccd3fd9ce605f1", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T01:05:31.971380+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C72 pre-compute full primitive interface: all original multipliers retained; optimum rows are forced zero and an explicit one-coordinate lift restores an actual minimizer without changing V. Joint PP/PD identities and exact-B amplification require A_seed>(r+1)^log_2(1+sqrt2). The new length15 discovery targets this stronger threshold, not a proxy improvement. UNVERIFIED; zero solver and verifier calls so far.", "evidence": "# C72: full primitive cone, a lossless optimum lift, and the exact reuse threshold\n\nStatus: self-contained research draft, UNVERIFIED. Sixth launch, first round,\ncumulative round 14. No verifier call is authorized. All new results are proved\njointly here; shared findings are reference evidence, not predecessors.\nAccepted background: b98bb807d514e80d (benchmark definitions),\n0be8b54e0a33381c (square-root mass contraction), and 1252b8823bb5a290\n(full smooth-convex interpolation and its explicit conjugate interpolant).\n\n## 1. The intrinsic property and every original vector\n\nNormalize L=1. For a nonnegative word h of length n let s=sum h_i,\nH_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, and\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                      -s(s+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nCall h primitive if V_h>=0 on every actual smooth convex run. This is exactly\nZhang--Jiang's primitive property: expansion of their difference of squared\npositions cancels x_0 and all mixed gradient terms and leaves V_h. No norm\nof x_0 is being substituted for the original objective distance.\n\nLet e denote the n+1 column of ones; define A_ii=1, A_it=h_t for t<i,\nand A_it=0 for t>i. The formal gradients g_0,...,g_n are independent.\nChoose ALL n(n+1) ordered finite multipliers lambda_ij>=0, i!=j; their\nzero diagonal is conventional. Define L=diag(lambda e)-lambda and\n\n omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,\n D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)).\n\nThen the full sufficient cone is exactly\n\n L^T e=omega,                 M=D+(LA+A^T L^T)/2 >=0.            (1)\n\nIndeed value cancellation in V_h+sum lambda Q is the displayed flow.\nThe position terms have matrix sym(L(A-I)); the squared-difference terms\nhave matrix sym(L)-diag(omega)/2. Adding V's diagonal gives (1), with\n\n                  V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g)>.          (2)\n\nThus (1) implies V_h>=0 because Q_ij<=0. All incoming terminal edges are\npresent. There is no rank, symmetry, child, sparsity, or fixed-remainder\nrestriction on lambda or M. In particular accepted terminal reduction\n1252b8823bb5a290 is NOT used to delete primitive constraints.\n\nAdding optimum indices to this certificate cannot enlarge its feasible\ncone. With x_star=g_star=f_star=0, the x_0 squared coefficient remains\nzero. Its mixed coefficient with g_i is -lambda_(i,star). A PSD matrix with\na zero diagonal entry has that entire row zero (test the corresponding\n2x2 principal minors). Therefore all lambda_(i,star)=0. The sum of finite\nvalue-flow equations is then -sum lambda_(star,i)=0, since the V value\ncoefficients sum to zero. Nonnegativity forces all lambda_(star,i)=0.\nConsequently the full optimum/position column is forced to be identically\nzero; (1) preserves the entire remaining cone, not a guessed reduction.\n\nA unit-slope Huber run started beyond 1+s has g_i=1, Q_ij=0, and V_h=0.\nFor any certificate (1) this gives e^T M e=0 and hence M e=0. This is an\nEXACT equality kernel, never a positive full-space eigenvalue margin. The\nnumerical formulation below uses the full matrix without a kernel quotient.\n\n## 2. Lossless intrinsic primal: explicitly restore a minimizer\n\nFor arbitrary finite sample jets obeying every Q_ij<=0 and the GD recursion,\nthere is a legal smooth convex function with a minimizer and the SAME V_h.\nThis justifies the intrinsic model at the level of actual functions as well\nas the preceding full-certificate column argument.\n\nFix epsilon>0 and append one coordinate. Put\n\n g'_i=(g_i,epsilon), x'_i=(x_i,T-epsilon H_i),\n f'_i=f_i+c-epsilon^2 H_i.\n\nThe GD recursion remains exact, and direct substitution shows Q'_ij=Q_ij:\nthe extra function difference -epsilon^2(H_i-H_j) cancels the extra\nposition term epsilon^2(H_i-H_j). Choose the finite constants\n\n c>=max_i{-f_i+epsilon^2 H_i+(||g_i||^2+epsilon^2)/2},\n epsilon T>=max_i{f_i+c-<g_i,x_i>+(||g_i||^2+epsilon^2)/2}.\n\nAfter adding the optimum jet (0,0,0), these are exactly Q'_(star,i)<=0\nand Q'_(i,star)<=0. The full conjugate interpolation construction in\naccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies an everywhere finite convex\n1-smooth interpolant with this optimum and every displayed jet. Thus it\nis a genuine same-function GD run with an actual minimizer.\n\nThe additional V contribution is epsilon^2 times\n\n 2sum_i h_i(s-H_i)+sum_i h_i(1-h_i)-s(s+1)=0,\n\nbecause 2sum_i h_i(s-H_i)=s^2+sum_i h_i^2. Hence V'_h=V_h exactly.\nThis lift does NOT preserve the original distance or normalized objective,\nand is used ONLY for intrinsic primitive validity. Zero steps and n=0\ncause no exception; identical original queries stay identical after a zero\nstep. All formal original gradients remain in the final cone model.\n\nIt follows that minimizing V_h over the full Gram G>=0, trace(G)=1, all\nordered Q_ij<=0, and a harmless value gauge f_n=0 detects primitive failure\nexactly at the sign level. Every negative feasible V yields a legal failure\nby the lift. Conversely every nonzero-gradient legal failure can be divided\nby its positive total squared gradient to meet trace(G)=1. Zero-gradient\njets cannot have negative V, since all values then agree. A common-gradient\nHuber transcript has V=0, so this minimum is always <=0.\n\nFor positive steps the primal also has strict feasibility: use a diagonal\nquadratic with n+1 distinct curvatures in (0,1) and nonzero initial\ncoordinates, then scale to trace(G)=1. The gradient polynomial columns\nz product_(t<i)(1-h_t z) have distinct degrees 1,...,n+1 and are independent\nat n+1 nonzero distinct nodes (divide by z). Every finite distinct-query\nresidual is strictly negative. This is the same Vandermonde argument as in\naccepted1252b8823bb5a290. Values are bounded after fixing f_n because the\ntwo ordered residuals bound every f_i-f_n and trace(G)=1 bounds every Gram\nentry. Thus no unbounded objective is concealed by the gauge.\n\n## 3. Composition identities, including arbitrary PSD child remainders\n\nThese identities are rederived from the Q definition and coincide with the\nproofs of Zhang--Jiang, arXiv:2410.12395v2, Theorems3.1/3.2. Their exact\nlocal paper, definitions, and both proofs were reread. No external result\nwith unexamined hypotheses is needed for the algebra below.\n\nPrimitive join. Let a,b be primitive, A=1+sum a, C=1+sum b. Let d>0 solve\n\n d(A+C+d)=AC; T=A+C+d, kappa=(T/C)^2.\n\nForm h=(a,1+d,b). Let p be the bridge query and z the terminal query, and\nlet j range over the nonterminal queries in b. Direct expansion gives\n\n V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1)[sum_j b_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)\n                                  +d Q_(p,z)+sum_j b_j Q_(j,z)]. (3)\n\nOne way to audit all coefficients is to call the bracket C_aux; its value\npart is 2sum_j b_j f_j+(d-C)f_p+(2-C-d)f_z, and its gradient part is\n\n sum_j b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2+[(C-d)/2-d^2]||g_p||^2\n                                          +(C+d)||g_z||^2/2.\n\nInsert these in (3), and use (kappa-1)(C-d)=2(A+d), kappa d^2=A^2,\nkappa C^2=T^2. The mixed terms cancel. Since C>d and kappa>1, every\nbracket coefficient is nonnegative. Child certificates with matrices M_a,\nM_b produce the FULL parent remainder M_a+kappa M_b on disjoint original\nchild-gradient sets. They may have arbitrary PSD rank. Its mass is\n\n T=Omega(A,C)=(A+C+sqrt((A+C)^2+4AC))/2.\n\nDominant join. Define, for weights u_i>=0 of mass D=2sum h+1,\n\n J_(h,u)=||x_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2D f_z\n                              -||x_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.\n\nDominance means J>=0 on every legal run; it implies W(h)<=1/D by Q_i*<=0.\nThe empty word has u=1 and J=0. A primitive word is dominant with\nu=(h,s+1), because expanding the last square gives J=V_h exactly.\nFor a primitive left child of mass A, and a dominant right child of mass D\nand weights v, put\n\n mu=2A/D, r=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2, gamma=A+1, beta=1+A/r.\n\nThe parent word is (a,beta,b). Its weights are u=(a,gamma,r v), all\nnonnegative, of total G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2=Dr^2. Its identity is\n\n J_parent=V_a+r^2 J_right-2mu sum_(i>p) v_i Q_(i,p).             (4)\n\nTo verify the equality expand both sides. The value flow uses\nmu D=(sum a)+gamma=2A. The gradient coefficients reduce to\n(sum a)(sum a+1)+mu D+gamma=gamma^2,\nr^2=r+mu, and r^2 beta=mu+gamma r; these cancel every mixed term and\nleave the defining parent square. This proves (4), including empty\nchildren. It proves exactly the ConPD rule in accepted benchmark notation.\n\n## 4. A finite seed threshold that compares the ORIGINAL exact B_n\n\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda), q=1/rho. Suppose an actual\nnonnegative primitive word a has length r_seed and A_seed=1+sum a. Put\nm_seed=r_seed+1. The sufficient target condition is\n\n                       A_seed>m_seed^rho.                      (5)\n\nA gain merely above C_(r_seed) does not imply (5).\nHere are the full amplification argument and the parameter losses.\nFirst Omega(x,y)^q<=x^q+y^q for x,y>=0. For positive arguments use\nx=exp(u-t), y=exp(u+t), t>=0. It becomes J(t)>=0 where\n\n J(t)=q^(-1)log(2cosh(qt))-arsinh(cosh t).\n\nJ(0)=0 and J(t) tends to zero at infinity. Its derivative has the sign\nof qt-alpha(t), where alpha=artanh(sinh(t)/sqrt(1+cosh(t)^2)). Direct\ndifferentiation gives alpha'=cosh(t)/sqrt(1+cosh(t)^2), strictly increasing\nfrom 1/sqrt(2) to1. Also 1/sqrt(2)<q<1: lambda>2 and lambda^3<16 imply\n1>q>3/4>1/sqrt(2). Thus alpha-qt starts by decreasing, has strictly\nincreasing derivative, and tends to infinity; it crosses zero once on\nt>0. Therefore J first increases and then decreases to zero, proving the\ninequality. Zero arguments follow directly from Omega(x,0)=x.\n\nInduction on each primitive binary tree now gives C_k<= (k+1)^rho.\nUnrolling a maximizing dominant tree, its primitive side branches have\nleaf counts l_1,...,l_t summing to n. Since G(A,D)<=D+4A,\n\n B_n<=1+4sum l_i^rho<=1+4n^rho.\n\nBalanced primitive trees give C_(2^k-1)=lambda^k, by the preceding upper\nbound and Omega(A,A)=lambda A. Hence B_(2^k)>=G(lambda^k,1)>=2lambda^k.\nThe number K=limsup B_n/n^rho thus satisfies 2<=K<=4. No phase limit is\nassumed and this is the ORIGINAL exact DP sequence.\n\nTake a fully expanded optimizing dominant tree at length n, with smallest\nmaximizing split as deterministic tie rule. It has exactly n primitive\nempty leaves and one final dominant empty leaf: induction on the unique\nright dominant chain proves this count. Replace every primitive empty\nleaf by a and recompute all physical bridges by (3)-(4). Leave the final\ndominant empty leaf unchanged. The actual resulting word H_n has length\nm_seed n and is dominant. Homogeneity of Omega makes each primitive mass\nexactly A_seed times its former mass.\n\nLet D_n=2sum H_n+1. In an auxiliary scalar evaluation also change the\nterminal dominant mass from1 to A_seed. Homogeneity of G makes that\nauxiliary root A_seed B_n. Accepted0be8b54e0a33381c proves\nsqrt(G(A,d))=(sqrt(d)+sqrt(d+8A))/2 and that this is increasing and\n1-Lipschitz as a function of sqrt(d). Propagating the unchanged terminal\nleaf discrepancy therefore gives\n\n sqrt(A_seed B_n)-(sqrt(A_seed)-1)<=sqrt(D_n)<=sqrt(A_seed B_n). (6)\n\nChoose n_j with B_(n_j)/n_j^rho tending to K. Equation(6) gives\nD_(n_j)/n_j^rho tending to A_seed K, whereas by the SAME global limsup\nlimsup B_(m_seed n_j)/(m_seed n_j)^rho<=K. Consequently\n\n limsup_j B_(m_seed n_j) W_(m_seed n_j)(H_(n_j))\n                           <= m_seed^rho/A_seed <1.            (7)\n\nFor delta=(1-m_seed^rho/A_seed)/2>0, every sufficiently large output\nhorizon in this subsequence has ratio <=1-delta. This is a fixed relative\nall-function upper separation and suffices to refute the original uniform\nasymptotic matching claim, conditional on the actual certified seed.\nThe unused dominant leaf and its O(sqrt(B_n)) mass loss are explicit.\n\n## 5. Before-solve design, calibration signs, and scope\n\nThe new discovery lane searches length15 only, with every coordinate free\nand fixed capacity A_target=(1+eta)lambda^4, initially eta=1/1000. The\nknown Silver word has length15 and exactly lambda^4 capacity, so (5) has\nzero baseline threshold gap. Source order, unequal step magnitudes, and the\nfull all-pair sensitivity guide proposals; no symmetry or tensor class is\nimposed. Length4 is used only for two exact model calibrations.\n\nCalibration expected signs BEFORE any solve:\n* h=(1,2,1,1) has min V=0. Its nonzero lambda entries are 01:2,10:4,\n  21:3,31:3,41:3,14:3,23:8,32:16,34:35,43:48,24:3. Expansion gives\n  M=|g0-g1|^2+4|g2-g3|^2+16|g3-g4|^2. Huber attains0.\n* h=(3,1,1,1) is nonprimitive. Use x=(1,-2,-3/2,-1,-1/2),\n  g=(1,-1/2,-1/2,-1/2,-1/2), f_i=g_i x_i-g_i^2/2. This is the\n  convex1-smooth function with derivative clipped to[-1/2,1]. Direct\n  substitution gives V=-45/4 and sum||g_i||^2=2. The trace-one model\n  therefore has min V<=-45/8. Every actual query and optimum Q<=0.\n* The full exact length15 Silver word has min V=0 by (3), starting with\n  empty leaves. Its full nonnegative array is generated by (3), with\n  identically zero PSD remainder. Its e kernel is not made strict.\n\nThe primal objective is NOT normalized objective error. Only an exactly\nreconstructed nonnegative certificate satisfying (1) and (5), followed by\n(3)-(7), could settle the original negative route. {"id": "6f982a593ecfe624", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T01:29:44.012158+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C74 complete joint UNVERIFIED follow-through: unique full signed-terminal certificate at every exact balanced Silver word; an exact unsaturated-cut defect identity; explicit original-Gram diagonal gaps sufficient for a first-order mass normal under arbitrary full coefficient re-selection. The exact 16-gradient normal completion is OPEN: evaluation44 is only a numerical candidate. First-order normality would still leave curved higher-order and distant primitive gains open. Includes C72's complete model and original-B_n reuse threshold, not a target proof.", "evidence": "# C72: full primitive cone, a lossless optimum lift, and the exact reuse threshold\n\nStatus: self-contained research draft, UNVERIFIED. Sixth launch, first round,\ncumulative round 14. No verifier call is authorized. All new results are proved\njointly here; shared findings are reference evidence, not predecessors.\nAccepted background: b98bb807d514e80d (benchmark definitions),\n0be8b54e0a33381c (square-root mass contraction), and 1252b8823bb5a290\n(full smooth-convex interpolation and its explicit conjugate interpolant).\n\n## 1. The intrinsic property and every original vector\n\nNormalize L=1. For a nonnegative word h of length n let s=sum h_i,\nP_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, and\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                      -s(s+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nCall h primitive if V_h>=0 on every actual smooth convex run. This is exactly\nZhang--Jiang's primitive property: expansion of their difference of squared\npositions cancels x_0 and all mixed gradient terms and leaves V_h. No norm\nof x_0 is being substituted for the original objective distance.\n\nLet e denote the n+1 column of ones; define A_ii=1, A_it=h_t for t<i,\nand A_it=0 for t>i. The formal gradients g_0,...,g_n are independent.\nChoose ALL n(n+1) ordered finite multipliers lambda_ij>=0, i!=j; their\nzero diagonal is conventional. Define L=diag(lambda e)-lambda and\n\n omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,\n D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)).\n\nThen the full sufficient cone is exactly\n\n L^T e=omega,                 M=D+(LA+A^T L^T)/2 >=0.            (1)\n\nIndeed value cancellation in V_h+sum lambda Q is the displayed flow.\nThe position terms have matrix sym(L(A-I)); the squared-difference terms\nhave matrix sym(L)-diag(omega)/2. Adding V's diagonal gives (1), with\n\n                  V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g)>.          (2)\n\nThus (1) implies V_h>=0 because Q_ij<=0. All incoming terminal edges are\npresent. There is no rank, symmetry, child, sparsity, or fixed-remainder\nrestriction on lambda or M. In particular accepted terminal reduction\n1252b8823bb5a290 is NOT used to delete primitive constraints.\n\nAdding optimum indices to this certificate cannot enlarge its feasible\ncone. With x_star=g_star=f_star=0, the x_0 squared coefficient remains\nzero. Its mixed coefficient with g_i is -lambda_(i,star). A PSD matrix with\na zero diagonal entry has that entire row zero (test the corresponding\n2x2 principal minors). Therefore all lambda_(i,star)=0. The sum of finite\nvalue-flow equations is then -sum lambda_(star,i)=0, since the V value\ncoefficients sum to zero. Nonnegativity forces all lambda_(star,i)=0.\nConsequently the full optimum/position column is forced to be identically\nzero; (1) preserves the entire remaining cone, not a guessed reduction.\n\nA unit-slope Huber run started beyond 1+s has g_i=1, Q_ij=0, and V_h=0.\nFor any certificate (1) this gives e^T M e=0 and hence M e=0. This is an\nEXACT equality kernel, never a positive full-space eigenvalue margin. The\nnumerical formulation below uses the full matrix without a kernel quotient.\n\n## 2. Lossless intrinsic primal: explicitly restore a minimizer\n\nFor arbitrary finite sample jets obeying every Q_ij<=0 and the GD recursion,\nthere is a legal smooth convex function with a minimizer and the SAME V_h.\nThis justifies the intrinsic model at the level of actual functions as well\nas the preceding full-certificate column argument.\n\nFix epsilon>0 and append one coordinate. Put\n\n g'_i=(g_i,epsilon), x'_i=(x_i,T-epsilon P_i),\n f'_i=f_i+c-epsilon^2 P_i.\n\nThe GD recursion remains exact, and direct substitution shows Q'_ij=Q_ij:\nthe extra function difference -epsilon^2(P_i-H_j) cancels the extra\nposition term epsilon^2(P_i-H_j). Choose the finite constants\n\n c>=max_i{-f_i+epsilon^2 P_i+(||g_i||^2+epsilon^2)/2},\n epsilon T>=max_i{f_i+c-<g_i,x_i>+(||g_i||^2+epsilon^2)/2}.\n\nAfter adding the optimum jet (0,0,0), these are exactly Q'_(star,i)<=0\nand Q'_(i,star)<=0. The full conjugate interpolation construction in\naccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies an everywhere finite convex\n1-smooth interpolant with this optimum and every displayed jet. Thus it\nis a genuine same-function GD run with an actual minimizer.\n\nThe additional V contribution is epsilon^2 times\n\n 2sum_i h_i(s-P_i)+sum_i h_i(1-h_i)-s(s+1)=0,\n\nbecause 2sum_i h_i(s-P_i)=s^2+sum_i h_i^2. Hence V'_h=V_h exactly.\nThis lift does NOT preserve the original distance or normalized objective,\nand is used ONLY for intrinsic primitive validity. Zero steps and n=0\ncause no exception; identical original queries stay identical after a zero\nstep. All formal original gradients remain in the final cone model.\n\nIt follows that minimizing V_h over the full Gram G_prim>=0, trace(G_prim)=1, all\nordered Q_ij<=0, and a harmless value gauge f_n=0 detects primitive failure\nexactly at the sign level. Every negative feasible V yields a legal failure\nby the lift. Conversely every nonzero-gradient legal failure can be divided\nby its positive total squared gradient to meet trace(G_prim)=1. Zero-gradient\njets cannot have negative V, since all values then agree. A common-gradient\nHuber transcript has V=0, so this minimum is always <=0.\n\nFor positive steps the primal also has strict feasibility: use a diagonal\nquadratic with n+1 distinct curvatures in (0,1) and nonzero initial\ncoordinates, then scale to trace(G_prim)=1. The gradient polynomial columns\nz product_(t<i)(1-h_t z) have distinct degrees 1,...,n+1 and are independent\nat n+1 nonzero distinct nodes (divide by z). Every finite distinct-query\nresidual is strictly negative. This is the same Vandermonde argument as in\naccepted1252b8823bb5a290. Values are bounded after fixing f_n because the\ntwo ordered residuals bound every f_i-f_n and trace(G_prim)=1 bounds every Gram\nentry. Thus no unbounded objective is concealed by the gauge.\n\n## 3. Composition identities, including arbitrary PSD child remainders\n\nThese identities are rederived from the Q definition and coincide with the\nproofs of Zhang--Jiang, arXiv:2410.12395v2, Theorems3.1/3.2. Their exact\nlocal paper, definitions, and both proofs were reread. No external result\nwith unexamined hypotheses is needed for the algebra below.\n\nPrimitive join. Let a,b be primitive, A=1+sum a, C=1+sum b. Let d>0 solve\n\n d(A+C+d)=AC; T=A+C+d, kappa=(T/C)^2.\n\nForm h=(a,1+d,b). Let p be the bridge query and z the terminal query, and\nlet j range over the nonterminal queries in b. Direct expansion gives\n\n V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1)[sum_j b_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)\n                                  +d Q_(p,z)+sum_j b_j Q_(j,z)]. (3)\n\nOne way to audit all coefficients is to call the bracket C_aux; its value\npart is 2sum_j b_j f_j+(d-C)f_p+(2-C-d)f_z, and its gradient part is\n\n sum_j b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2+[(C-d)/2-d^2]||g_p||^2\n                                          +(C+d)||g_z||^2/2.\n\nInsert these in (3), and use (kappa-1)(C-d)=2(A+d), kappa d^2=A^2,\nkappa C^2=T^2. The mixed terms cancel. Since C>d and kappa>1, every\nbracket coefficient is nonnegative. Child certificates with matrices M_a,\nM_b produce the FULL parent remainder M_a+kappa M_b on disjoint original\nchild-gradient sets. They may have arbitrary PSD rank. Its mass is\n\n T=Omega(A,C)=(A+C+sqrt((A+C)^2+4AC))/2.\n\nDominant join. Define, for weights u_i>=0 of mass D=2sum h+1,\n\n J_(h,u)=||x_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2D f_z\n                              -||x_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.\n\nDominance means J>=0 on every legal run; it implies W(h)<=1/D by Q_i*<=0.\nThe empty word has u=1 and J=0. A primitive word is dominant with\nu=(h,s+1), because expanding the last square gives J=V_h exactly.\nFor a primitive left child of mass A, and a dominant right child of mass D\nand weights v, put\n\n mu=2A/D, r=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2, gamma=A+1, beta=1+A/r.\n\nThe parent word is (a,beta,b). Its weights are u=(a,gamma,r v), all\nnonnegative, of total G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2=Dr^2. Its identity is\n\n J_parent=V_a+r^2 J_right-2mu sum_(i>p) v_i Q_(i,p).             (4)\n\nTo verify the equality expand both sides. The value flow uses\nmu D=(sum a)+gamma=2A. The gradient coefficients reduce to\n(sum a)(sum a+1)+mu D+gamma=gamma^2,\nr^2=r+mu, and r^2 beta=mu+gamma r; these cancel every mixed term and\nleave the defining parent square. This proves (4), including empty\nchildren. It proves exactly the ConPD rule in accepted benchmark notation.\n\n## 4. A finite seed threshold that compares the ORIGINAL exact B_n\n\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda), q=1/rho. Suppose an actual\nnonnegative primitive word a has length r_seed and A_seed=1+sum a. Put\nm_seed=r_seed+1. The sufficient target condition is\n\n                       A_seed>m_seed^rho.                      (5)\n\nA gain merely above C_(r_seed) does not imply (5).\nHere are the full amplification argument and the parameter losses.\nFirst Omega(x,y)^q<=x^q+y^q for x,y>=0. For positive arguments use\nx=exp(u-t), y=exp(u+t), t>=0. It becomes J(t)>=0 where\n\n J(t)=q^(-1)log(2cosh(qt))-arsinh(cosh t).\n\nJ(0)=0 and J(t) tends to zero at infinity. Its derivative has the sign\nof qt-alpha(t), where alpha=artanh(sinh(t)/sqrt(1+cosh(t)^2)). Direct\ndifferentiation gives alpha'=cosh(t)/sqrt(1+cosh(t)^2), strictly increasing\nfrom 1/sqrt(2) to1. Also 1/sqrt(2)<q<1: lambda>2 and lambda^3<16 imply\n1>q>3/4>1/sqrt(2). Thus alpha-qt starts by decreasing, has strictly\nincreasing derivative, and tends to infinity; it crosses zero once on\nt>0. Therefore J first increases and then decreases to zero, proving the\ninequality. Zero arguments follow directly from Omega(x,0)=x.\n\nInduction on each primitive binary tree now gives C_k<= (k+1)^rho.\nUnrolling a maximizing dominant tree, its primitive side branches have\nleaf counts l_1,...,l_t summing to n. Since G(A,D)<=D+4A,\n\n B_n<=1+4sum l_i^rho<=1+4n^rho.\n\nBalanced primitive trees give C_(2^k-1)=lambda^k, by the preceding upper\nbound and Omega(A,A)=lambda A. Hence B_(2^k)>=G(lambda^k,1)>=2lambda^k.\nThe number K=limsup B_n/n^rho thus satisfies 2<=K<=4. No phase limit is\nassumed and this is the ORIGINAL exact DP sequence.\n\nTake a fully expanded optimizing dominant tree at length n, with smallest\nmaximizing split as deterministic tie rule. It has exactly n primitive\nempty leaves and one final dominant empty leaf: induction on the unique\nright dominant chain proves this count. Replace every primitive empty\nleaf by a and recompute all physical bridges by (3)-(4). Leave the final\ndominant empty leaf unchanged. The actual resulting word Lift_n has length\nm_seed n and is dominant. Homogeneity of Omega makes each primitive mass\nexactly A_seed times its former mass.\n\nLet D_n=2sum Lift_n+1. In an auxiliary scalar evaluation also change the\nterminal dominant mass from1 to A_seed. Homogeneity of G makes that\nauxiliary root A_seed B_n. Propagating the unchanged terminal\nleaf discrepancy therefore gives\n\n sqrt(A_seed B_n)-(sqrt(A_seed)-1)<=sqrt(D_n)<=sqrt(A_seed B_n). (6)\n\nChoose n_j with B_(n_j)/n_j^rho tending to K. Equation(6) gives\nD_(n_j)/n_j^rho tending to A_seed K, whereas by the SAME global limsup\nlimsup B_(m_seed n_j)/(m_seed n_j)^rho<=K. Consequently\n\n limsup_j B_(m_seed n_j) W_(m_seed n_j)(Lift_(n_j))\n                           <= m_seed^rho/A_seed <1.            (7)\n\nFor delta=(1-m_seed^rho/A_seed)/2>0, every sufficiently large output\nhorizon in this subsequence has ratio <=1-delta. This is a fixed relative\nall-function upper separation and suffices to refute the original uniform\nasymptotic matching claim, conditional on the actual certified seed.\nThe unused dominant leaf and its O(sqrt(B_n)) mass loss are explicit.\n\n## 5. The allowance is at most72 evaluations,120 measured\nbuild-plus-solve seconds,n<=15,40 distinct nonbaseline words. No old budget\nis renewed and no verifier call is made.\n\n## 6. C74: a full-array source rigidity lemma and its local scope\n\nThis appendix is part of the C72 joint draft. It is UNVERIFIED. It is not a\nproof that an improved primitive seed is impossible. The numerical normal\nat the end is not an exact PSD certificate.\n\nDefine the balanced primitive words Sil_0=(), Sil_(m+1)=(Sil_m,1+lambda^(m-1),Sil_m),\nwhere lambda=1+sqrt(2). Thus Sil_m has length2^m-1 and capacity lambda^m.\nThe complete array Lambda_m is obtained from (3) starting with the empty\narray, and has identically zero full remainder. It is the UNIQUE full\ncertificate even if all incoming-terminal coefficients are allowed to be\nsigned, while every other coefficient is nonnegative. Here and below\n\"certificate\" includes cancellation of all values and PSD on all original\ngradients. The optimum columns are already forced zero by Section1.\n\nHere is a full proof rather than an assumption of a unique optimizer.\nAt a root write h=(a,1+d,b), A=1+sum a, C=1+sum b, T=A+C+d, dT=AC,\nkappa=(T/C)^2, eta=d/C. Let p be the bridge query, z the final query,\nP={queries through p}, and B={queries after p through z}.\nThere are two legal equality runs. A far-out unit-slope Huber ray has all\ngradients1 and all Q=V=0. The clipped derivative f'(x)=min(1,max(x,-eta)),\nwith f(0)=0 and initial x_0=A, has gradients1 on P and -eta on B. If\nR_i^a is the remaining left step sum before p and R_j^b the remaining\nright sum before z, its finite data are\n\n x_i=1+R_i^a, f_i=R_i^a+1/2       (i in P),\n x_j=-eta(1+R_j^b), f_j=eta^2(R_j^b+1/2) (j in B).\n\nIts same-group residuals vanish and its cross residuals are exactly\n\n Q_(i,j)=-(1+eta)eta R_j^b, Q_(j,i)=-(1+eta)R_i^a.             (8)\n\nMoreover f_i=g_i x_i-g_i^2/2 throughout, so telescoping gives\nV=A^2-T^2 eta^2=0. Every incoming-terminal Q is zero. Therefore even with\nsigned incoming-terminal multipliers, evaluating any supplied PSD\ncertificate on these runs gives zero, and every nonterminal negative-Q\nedge has zero coefficient. Positivity of all source steps leaves only\ncross edges i->z (i in P) and j->p (j in B).\n\nWrite their coefficients nu_i and mu_j respectively. Both equality\ngradient vectors lie in the PSD kernel; they span the block indicators\nof P and B. The full mixed form, before using those kernels, is\n\n -sum_(i in P) nu_i <g_i,sum_(j in B,j<z)b_j g_j+g_z>\n                        +d sum_(j in B)mu_j <g_p,g_j>.\n\nRows i<p of the matrix applied to the B indicator force C nu_i=0.\nRows in B applied to the P indicator then force\nmu_j=nu_p b_j/d for j<z and mu_z=nu_p/d. Summing value-flow equations\nthrough P gives sum mu-nu_p=2(A+d). Hence\n\n nu_p=d(kappa-1), mu_j=(kappa-1)b_j, mu_z=kappa-1.              (9)\n\nIn particular every full mixed coefficient is zero. Formula(3) then\nsplits the supplied full matrix as M_a+kappa M_b, on disjoint original\ngradient blocks, where the prefix array is unchanged and the tail array is\n\n lambda^b_ij=lambda_ij/kappa\n                 -(1-1/kappa)b_i 1_(i<z,j=z).\n\nBoth blocks are PSD. The prefix array is ordinary; the tail may have\nsigned terminal coefficients but all other signs remain nonnegative.\nThus induction may be applied inside both balanced children. The base\nempty word has the zero array. For the one-step base, writing u=sqrt(2),\nvalue cancellation gives lambda_10=lambda_01+2u, while the constant\nkernel forces lambda_01=2; its matrix is (2-u^2)|g0-g1|^2=0.\nThe induction with (9) uniquely reconstructs Lambda_m, and forces all\nfull child and parent matrices to be zero. This proves the stated rigidity.\nIt is rigidity at this exact word, not a neighborhood or global theorem.\n\n## 7. Exact continuation identity away from a saturated cut\n\nFor ANY positive a,b and bridge1+d with 0<d<C=1+sum b, put\nA=1+sum a, T=A+C+d, and now define\n\n kappa=1+2(A+d)/(C-d),\n R=(A+d)(AC-dT)/(C-d).\n\nNo primitive assumption and no saturation equation is used. Direct\ncoefficient comparison, retaining every value and gradient, yields\n\n V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1)C_aux\n                              +R(|g_p|^2-|g_z|^2),             (10)\n\nwhere C_aux is the same bracket in(3). The values match because\n(kappa-1)(C-d)=2(A+d). The unmatched squared-gradient coefficients are\nA^2-kappa d^2 and kappa C^2-T^2; their sum is zero and the first equals\nR. This verifies all terms using the explicit expansion in Section3.\n\nRecursively use(10) on the fixed complete binary tree near Sil_m. Every\nactual coefficient in the resulting array Lambda(h) is nonnegative,\nsince d>0,C>d,kappa>1 persist. Its two child capacities are\nA_v=lambda^(m-d_v-1), its bridge gap is A_v/lambda, and w_v=lambda^(2r_v).\nFor an arbitrary first variation u of ALL the physical steps,\n\n dR_v[u]=sqrt(2) A_v [sum_(steps in left or right child)u_i\n                                             -2lambda u_(p(v))]. (12)\n\nTo check this derivative, the factor AC-dT vanishes at the source, and\n(A+d)/(C-d)=lambda. Its derivative is\n(C-d)dA+(A-d)dC-(T+d)dd, giving(12). No derivative of w_v contributes\nbecause R_v=0 at the source.\n\n## 8. A concrete exact normal-completion interface\n\nConsider any full finite jet (G_prim,f) for the actual source Sil_m, where G_prim is\nthe Gram matrix of its2^m original gradients, satisfying\n\n G_prim>=0, Q_ij<=0 for EVERY ordered finite pair,\n Q_ij=0 on EVERY positive entry of Lambda_m.                    (13)\n\nAll quantities Q here use the source word and the independent f_i;\na common value shift may set the last value to zero. Suppose for some\nc_tan>0 its diagonals further satisfy the explicit2^m-1 equations\n\n G_(p(v),p(v))-G_(z(v),z(v))\n             = c_tan lambda^(2d_v-m-2r_v)/(2^(d_v+1)sqrt(2)).       (14)\n\nThen the envelope sensitivity of the COMPLETE canonical array is exactly\n\n                  S(G_prim,f)=-c_tan(1,...,1).                         (15)\n\nProof: differentiate (2) along the explicitly defined array Lambda(h)\nin(11). Every derivative of a multiplier is supported where Lambda_m>0,\nso its contribution vanishes on(13). By(12), put\n t_v=w_v sqrt(2)A_v(G_pp-G_zz). Equation(14) says\n t_v=c_tan lambda^(d_v-1)/2^(d_v+1).\nFor the step at a node of depth d the resulting sensitivity is\nsum_(proper ancestors v)t_v-2lambda t_node. The geometric sum is\nc_tan[(lambda/2)^d-1], because lambda/2=1+1/(2lambda).\nThe second term is c_tan(lambda/2)^d, so their difference is -c_tan, proving(15).\nThis yields the sensitivity without assuming favorable cross-gradient\nterms: the ENTIRE matrix and all-Q legality remain obligations in(13).\n\nIf a solution of(13)-(14) is actually proved, every sequence of fully\ncertifiable words h_l tending to Sil_m obeys\n\n limsup_l [sum(h_l)-sum(Sil_m)]/||h_l-Sil_m|| <=0,                 (16)\n\nwith zero differences omitted. Thus a C^1 primitive-certificate path out\nof this source cannot have a positive first-order capacity derivative.\nHere is a proof that retains arbitrary re-selection of all coefficients.\nA fixed sufficiently small-curvature quadratic, run with nearby positive\nwords, has every finite Q strictly negative, uniformly in a small\nneighborhood: choose theta so all theta h_i<1 and use x_0=1. Its iterates\nare positive and distinct; Q_ij=-theta(1-theta)(x_i-x_j)^2/2.\nEvaluating a certificate on this actual run bounds the sum of ALL its\nnonnegative coefficients by a common constant. Hence along any sequence\nof nearby certificates, compactness gives convergent subsequences.\nEvery limit is a full source certificate and therefore equals Lambda_m\nby Section6. Thus every selected array tends to Lambda_m.\n\nEvaluate the nearby PSD remainder on the fixed G_prim from(13). At the\nsource, V=0 by the zero remainder and complementarity. The unchanged\nsource part is sum lambda_l Q(source)<=0: positive source coefficients\nmultiply zero, and all other coefficients are nonnegative. The change\nwith h therefore bounds the nonnegative remainder above by\n\n S(G_prim,f) dot(h_l-Sil_m)+o(||h_l-Sil_m||).\n\nThis follows directly from the polynomial V and Q expressions, the\nconvergence lambda_l->Lambda_m, and their uniform boundedness. Equation(15)\nnow gives(16). This uses no derivative or pseudoinverse of the selected\nlambda_l and no strict full-space remainder margin.\n\nCrucial scope: even(16) would NOT prohibit a positive capacity gain of\nsecond or higher order along a curved path, nor a distant seed. It does\nnot prove C_(2^m-1) is globally optimal over primitive schedules. No such\nnormal is asserted to exist merely because its finite SDP appears feasible.\nFor an explicit logical scope check, the set {(x,y):y<=x^2} has tangent\ncone y<=0 at(0,0), but contains(t,t^2) with positive y for every t!=0.\nIndeed for convergent normalized directions the inequality gives the\ny-component<=0, whereas t^2/sqrt(t^2+t^4) tends to zero. Thus a first-order\nnormal alone cannot exclude higher-order objective gains in a nonconvex\nfeasible set. This example is not asserted to be a GD schedule.\n\nThe all-depth algebra makes the missing construction specific. In a parent\nnormal of strength c_tan, the required left/right child normal strengths are\nc_tan_left=(lambda/2)c_tan and c_tan_right=c_tan/(2lambda). The root also needs\nG_pp-G_zz=c_tan/(2sqrt(2)lambda^m). Cross-child Q and full PSD must be realized\nsimultaneously at those exact scales. Independent child scaling without\nthis original-space completion proves nothing. At length15 this is one\n16-gradient Gram completion with fixed diagonal differences, not an\nasymptotic benchmark comparison or a surrogate finite objective gain.\n\n## 9. Quantitative b/u/e and load estimates remain OPEN; no verifier acceptance or whole-kernel result.", "evidence": "# Positive off-diagonal entries and exact chronological decimation\n\nStatus: **unverified proof draft**, sixth launch, first continuation round.\nNo kernel evaluation, optimizer, installation, or fact submission was used.\nThe uniform positive-semidefinite conclusion and the original minimax Goal\nremain open. This document proves some of the actual coefficient claims;\nit does not assume the remaining thirteen-band estimates.\n\n## 1. Definitions and the coefficient that is being estimated\n\nFix an integer m>=128 and keep epsilon=1/m fixed throughout. Set\n\n lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,\n d_k=1+a*c^k  (k>=0), H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j).\n\nThe extension of d_k past m-1 is only a coefficient device. The actual\nqueried word is H_m with its last d_0 removed. Write h_i for its steps,\ns_i=sum_(r<i)h_r, and s'=s_i+h_i when discussing a single step.\n\nOn the infinite original ruler word define B_00=1 and\n\n B_(i+1),k=(1-h_i/d_k)B_ik+(h_i/d_(k-1))B_i,(k-1),\n\nwith a zero second term at k=0. For each occupied state k, h_i<=d_k:\nthe first occurrence of d_j kills state j, and all smaller states have\nalready died. This also proves B_ik>=0, sum_k B_ik=1, and\nB_ik=0 for i>=2^k or k>i.\n\nFor arbitrary formal vectors y_k put g_i=sum_k v_ik*y_k, where\nv_ik=B_ik/d_k. The row-finite symmetric matrix E is defined by\n\n sum_(k,l)E_kl<y_k,y_l>\n =sum_i [2s_(i+1)h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>\n       +s_(i+1)||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2].   (1)\n\nEach coefficient is a finite sum: a term containing state k occurs by\nthe step immediately before query2^k. In particular E_kl=0 for k<l\nand l>2^k. The m-principal block is the actual finite residual kernel.\nThe coefficient convention in (1) means that an off-diagonal entry\nappears twice in the quadratic form.\n\n## 2. Two elementary bounds for the actual chronological word\n\nFirst, 2<c<3 and 1<d_0<2. Also\n\n                         d_(k+1)>2d_k  (k>=2).            (2)\n\nIndeed d_(k+1)-2d_k=(c-2)a*c^k-1. At k=2 the quantity\nbefore subtracting1 is (1-epsilon)c(c-2), and\n\n c(c-2)=1+2lambda(lambda-1)epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2\n        >1+6epsilon.\n\nConsequently (1-epsilon)c(c-2)>1+5epsilon-6epsilon^2>1.\nThe expression increases with k, proving (2).\n\nSecond, every original physical step satisfies\n\n                             h_i<s_i+2.                  (3)\n\nLet S_j=sum H_j and C_j=S_j+2-d_j. Then C_0=2-d_0>0.\nThe recurrences S_(j+1)=2S_j+d_j and\nd_(j+1)=c*d_j-(c-1) give the exact identity\n\n C_(j+1)=2C_j+(3-c)(d_j-1)>0.\n\nThe prefix before the FIRST d_j is H_j, so (3) holds there. Every later\noccurrence has a larger elapsed clock and the same step size. This proves\n(3) at every position, without changing the enclosing epsilon.\n\n## 3. Nonnegative off-diagonal entries, term by term\n\nFor a fixed step h=h_i, write v_k=v_ik and v_(-1)=0. The scaled birth\nrecurrence is\n\n v'_k=v_k+(h/d_k)(v_(k-1)-v_k).\n\nFor k<l, expansion of that step's contribution to E_kl gives exactly\n\n C_kl*v_k*v_l\n +[s'h^2(d_l-1)/(d_k*d_l)]v_(k-1)*v_l\n +[s'h^2(d_k-1)/(d_k*d_l)]v_k*v_(l-1)\n +[s'h^2/(d_k*d_l)]v_(k-1)*v_(l-1),                     (4)\n\nwhere\n\n C_kl=h[2s'+1-h-s'h(1/d_k+1/d_l-1/(d_k*d_l))].           (5)\n\nTo check (4), the first term of (1) contributes\ns'h(v'_k*v_l+v_k*v'_l), the second contributes\ns'(v'_k-v_k)(v'_l-v_l), and the third contributes\nh(1-h)v_k*v_l. Expanding these three expressions gives (4)-(5).\n\nThe last three coefficients in (4) are nonnegative. Suppose k>=2.\nIf v_k*v_l=0, the possibly signed coefficient C_kl costs nothing.\nOtherwise state k is occupied, so h<=d_k. Equation (2) gives\nd_l>=d_(k+1)>2d_k and hence\n\n h(1/d_k+1/d_l-1/(d_k*d_l))<3/2.\n\nIt follows from (3) that\n\n C_kl>h(s'/2+1-h)=(h/2)(s_i+2-h)>0.                     (6)\n\nThus every physical step contributes a nonnegative amount to E_kl\nwhen 2<=k<l. This proof keeps the signed terms of the original step\nuntil they combine into C_kl; it does not take their absolute values.\n\nFor k=0,1, support leaves only E_01 and E_12. The first two steps,\nwhose unscaled state vectors are successively V_0,V_1,V_2, give\n\n E_01=(d_0-1)/d_1>0,\n E_12=(d_0+d_1)(d_1-1)/(d_1*d_2)>0.\n\nThese calculations and (4)-(6) prove\n\n                    E_kl>=0 for every k!=l.              (7)\n\nIn particular the actual second-neighbor weights obey e_0=e_1=0 and\n\n                    e_k=E_(k,k+2)>0 for k>=2.             (8)\n\nFor strictness, just before the first d_k both B_ik and B_i,(k+1)\nare positive when k>=2. For B_ik, use the path that takes a birth on\neach of its first k steps and then stays. All those birth probabilities\nare positive, and every subsequent step before the first d_k is smaller\nthan d_k. For B_i,(k+1), take the first k+1 births and then stay; this\nis possible because k+1<=2^k-1, and all later pre-d_k steps are smaller\nthan d_(k+1). At the d_k step, the third term in (4) with l=k+2 is\nstrictly positive: s'>0, d_k>1, v_k>0, v_(k+1)>0. This proves (8).\n\nNo uniform lower bound such as e_k>=1/15 is proved by this strictness\nargument: the displayed positive path probabilities have not been\nestimated uniformly.\n\n## 4. Exact consequences for the unresolved row margins\n\nFor all nonnegative indices define\n\n A_i=E_(i,i+1), e_i=E_(i,i+2),\n b_i=A_i-2(e_(i-1)+e_i),\n u_i=E_ii-A_(i-1)-A_i+e_(i-2)+e_i\n                         -sum_(|l-i|>=3)|E_il|,\n\nwith negative subscripts equal to zero. Let F_i=sum_l E_il be the\nfinite unweighted row sum, and ellhat_i=sum_l E_il*d_l the constant\nload obtained by polarizing (1) against the constant gradient.\n\nEquation (7) removes all absolute values from the far off-diagonal\nsum. Therefore the exact full-row identity is\n\n                     u_i=2E_ii+2(e_(i-2)+e_i)-F_i.         (9)\n\nIn particular a proof of u_i does not need to identify alternating\nsigns of individual far entries. It still needs signed cancellation\nbetween the diagonal, the neighboring entries, and the row sum.\nNonnegative entries do not imply either b_i>=2/15 or u_i>=0.\n\nThis does not discard a terminal coefficient. In the inherited finite\ndecomposition, the triple starting at m-2 remains the square\ne_(m-2)||y_(m-2)+2y_(m-1)||^2. Its coefficient now has the sign in (8).\nThe last diagonal remains\n\n u_(m-1)+sum_(l>=m+2)E_(m-1,l)+b_(m-1)+e_(m-1).\n\nNeither its required quantitative lower bound nor the completed\nconstant-column remainder follows from (7) alone.\n\n## 5. A scalar constant-load formula with the signed difference retained\n\nFor this section all sums run over the infinite coefficient word, but\nevery state-specific sum is finite. Set\n\n mu_ik=h_i B_ik/d_k,\n L_k=sum_(r=0)^k d_r,\n M_k=sum_i h_i*mu_ik,\n N_k=sum_i s_(i+1)*h_i*mu_ik,\n\nand put M_(-1)=N_(-1)=0. The flow equation is\nB_(i+1),k-B_ik=mu_i,(k-1)-mu_ik. Summing it proves\nsum_i mu_ik=1 for each transient state. Weighting it by s_(i+1)\nand summing by parts gives\n\n sum_i s_(i+1)(mu_ik-mu_i,(k-1))=sum_i h_i B_ik=d_k.\n\nStarting with a zero predecessor moment shows\nsum_i s_(i+1)mu_ik=L_k. Thus M_k and N_k are respectively the mean\nexit step and the mean product of exit clock and exit step for state k.\n\nPolarizing (1) against the constant gradient gives\n\n ellhat_k=sum_i h_i[(s_i+1)v_ik+s_(i+1)v_(i+1),k].         (10)\n\nFor example, the first term in (1) contributes\ns'h(v_ik+v_(i+1),k), its increment-square term contributes zero,\nand its last term contributes h(1-h)v_ik. Their sum is (10).\nSubstitute the birth recurrence into (10), then use the two occupation\nidentities just proved. The result is\n\n             ellhat_k=2L_k+1-M_k-(N_k-N_(k-1))/d_k.        (11)\n\nIn detail, before the recurrence contribution the sum is\n2L_k+1-M_k. The remaining term is\nsum_i s'h_i^2(v_i,(k-1)-v_ik)/d_k\n=(N_(k-1)-N_k)/d_k. This checks its sign and its denominator.\n\nFormula (11) is an identity, not a new condition replacing the load\ninequalities. It identifies the chronological mixed moment that must\nbe estimated. Bounding N_k and N_(k-1) separately loses the signed\nquantity needed for the desired 2d_k<=ellhat_k<=11d_k/4.\n\n## 6. An exact reduction that distinguishes the original lower-level order\n\nPut t=d_0, ell=c-1, gamma=ell-t, and\n\n Z(z)=c*z/(1+ell*z),\n A(z)=(1-t*z)(1+ell*z)=1+gamma*z-t*ell*z^2.\n\nThese are new scalar functions, unrelated to the adjacent entries A_i.\nThey are well-defined at every nonnegative reciprocal physical node.\nThe affine-geometric node relation gives\n\n (1-t*z)(1-d_(j+1)*z)=A(z)(1-d_j*Z(z)),\n Z(1/d_(j+1))=1/d_j.                                    (12)\n\nWrite the original infinite step word in consecutive pairs. The exact\npair at position r is (t,d_(nu_2(r+1)+1)), where nu_2 is the exponent\nof2 in a positive integer. After removing every t and lowering the\nremaining level by1, the compressed word is the same original ruler\nword, with the SAME epsilon. For its first r steps write\nP_r(z)=product_(i<r)(1-h_i*z) and s_r=sum_(i<r)h_i. Then\n\n P_(2r)(z)=A(z)^r P_r(Z(z)),\n P_(2r+1)(z)=(1-t*z)A(z)^r P_r(Z(z)),\n s_(2r)=c*s_r-gamma*r,\n s_(2r+1)=c*s_r-gamma*r+t.                               (13)\n\nAll four identities follow by multiplying or summing the individual\npairs in (12). This is a chronological identity, not an identity of\nthe full multiset alone. The foil that exchanges the third and fourth\nsteps destroys the displayed consecutive-pair description.\n\nThe Newton factors also transform exactly:\n\n phi_(k+1)(z)=(1-t*z)(1+ell*z)^k phi_k(Z(z)),              (14)\n\nwhere phi_k(z)=product_(j<k)(1-d_j*z). At epsilon=0, interpreted only\nas a coefficient limit, t=ell=sqrt(2), gamma=0, and A(z)=1-2z^2.\nAt every actual epsilon>0 the linear term gamma*z and the clock defect\n-gamma*r in (13) must both be retained. In fact\n\n gamma=lambda*epsilon+2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]>0.\n\nEquations (12)-(14) do not yet establish a bound for E or the mixed\nmoments in (11). They give an exact original-order transformation in\nwhich those estimates can be pursued without replacing an embedded\nblock's parameters by epsilon=1/j.\n\n## 7. Scope and remaining work\n\nThe new proved-in-draft part of the stated interface is e_i>=0 for\nevery required index. The stronger entrywise result (7) and the\nidentities (9), (11), and (13) have explicit proofs above.\n\nThe unresolved claims remain b_i>=2/15, u_i>=0, the quantitative\ne_i>=1/15 and u_i>=1/16 at the required terminal indices, and the\ntwo-sided constant-load bounds. This document does not prove the full\nkernel positive semidefinite, and it is not a counterexample to those\nremaining actual-word claims. Even their proof would only concern the\nspecified reciprocal-level temporal quotient, not arbitrary legal GD.\n\nThe existing same-multiset foil is still a useful warning: its first\nfew steps also satisfy h_i<s_i+2, and entrywise off-diagonal positivity\nalone does not distinguish its negative diagonal-margin behavior.\nThus (7) must not be promoted into a substitute for the original-order\nestimates. The exact pair identity (13), unlike that coarse envelope,\ndoes distinguish the original word.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max2_chronological_kernel_positive_entries.md", "plan_id": "max2_chronological_local_squares", "attempt_type": "direct", "status": "partial", "subgoal": "Prove actual original-word thirteen-band scalar inequalities uniformly m>=128", "solved_parts": ["e_i>=0 uniformly at every required index", "All off-diagonal coefficients >=0, termwise proof using actual clock and d_(k+1)>2d_k", "Signed exit-moment identity for ellhat", "Exact original-pair/Mobius decimation at SAME enclosing epsilon"], "key_stuck_points": ["b_i>=2/15", "u_i>=0 and terminal >=1/16", "Quantitative terminal e_i>=1/15", "2d_i<=ellhat_i<=11d_i/4"], "counterexample_search_for_stuck_subgoal": {"performed": true, "result": "not_refuted", "summary": "The same-level swapped foil still satisfies the coarse clock envelope and off-diagonal positivity, so these alone cannot imply u or full PSD.
