{"id": "54a10537d332fc3e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T01:37:17.290022+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Complete UNVERIFIED all-history obstruction for the assigned common-prefix/full-support law: for every original H_m, m>=1024, every legal positive-amplitude realization (with arbitrary unmatched histories, anchors and values) has total chronological mass <800R and V_chr <=512(3/5)^((n+1)/8)+2^32/(n+1)^2, where R is TRUE initial distance. Hence B_n V_chr tends0 uniformly. Finite epsilon is retained exactly as four coarse rays; only eta=(n+1)^-2 is perturbed after proving the mass bound. No amplitude policy for this exact law can repair the target deficit.", "evidence": "# The fixed common-prefix law fails for every positive amplitude history\n\nStatus: **complete joint candidate, UNVERIFIED**. This continues the eighth\nmax4 assignment. No solver, parameter sweep, installation, or fact submission\nis used. The only correctness predecessors are accepted facts\n`1252b8823bb5a290` (original smooth-convex interpolation) and\n`b98bb807d514e80d` (the exact benchmark and its elementary bound). All other\narguments used here are supplied within this document. In particular the\nearlier named-amplitude obstruction is not a predecessor.\n\nThe amplitudes below are arbitrary positive numbers. They may depend on the\nentire known chronological history, the enclosing horizon, and the full word.\nNo two copies of a subword are assumed to have matching amplitude profiles.\n\n## 1. Statement, unchanged word, and every probability atom\n\nFix an integer m >= 1024. Define\n\n    sigma = 1+sqrt(2), delta = 1-1/m,\n    c_chr = sigma(1+1/m), a_chr = delta/c_chr,\n    N = 2^m, n = N-1, epsilon = 1/N, eta = 1/N^2,\n    l = 1+a_chr, k = 1+delta.\n\nThe actual word is H_chr,0 empty and\n\n    H_chr,j+1 = (H_chr,j, 1+a_chr*c_chr^j, H_chr,j), 0 <= j < m.\n\nThus h_i = 1+a_chr*c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)), 0 <= i < n, where nu_2(q)\nis the exponent of 2 dividing the positive integer q. Every subword retains\nthe same enclosing-m parameters. There are n physical steps and N samples.\n\nGive sample i its standard m-bit binary label (i_0,...,i_(m-1)). For\ns in {0,1}^m define\n\n    chi_s(i) = (-1)^(sum_j s_j i_j),\n    B(s) = (-1)^s_0,\n    C_j(s) = (-1)^(s_1+...+s_j), 1 <= j < m.\n\nThese are real signs. The map from s to (B,C_1,...,C_(m-1)) is bijective:\n(-1)^s_1=C_1 and (-1)^s_j=C_(j-1)C_j for j>=2. Put\n\n    mu_+ = -7/10, mu_- = 9/10, p_chr = 1-epsilon,\n\n    A_b(c_1,...,c_(m-1))\n      = (1+mu_b)/2 * 1_(all c_j=1)\n          + (1-mu_b)/2 * 1_(all c_j=-1),\n    P_b(c_1,...,c_(m-1)) = product_j (1+mu_b*c_j)/2,\n\n    w_s = (1+p_chr*B(s))/2\n             * [(1-eta) A_(B(s))(C_1(s),...,C_(m-1)(s))\n                     + eta P_(B(s))(C_1(s),...,C_(m-1)(s))].       (1)\n\nEach conditional law sums to one. Every atom is strictly positive because\nepsilon,eta>0 and both conditional means lie strictly between -1 and 1.\nThis is exactly the assigned finite probability law.\n\nFor ANY positive amplitudes Gamma_0,...,Gamma_n, prescribe\n\n    g_i = (Gamma_i*sqrt(w_s)*chi_s(i))_s, 0 <= i <= n.              (2)\n\nSuppose these are the actual gradients of ONE finite-dimensional convex\ndifferentiable 1-smooth function F with a minimizer, along its actual GD:\n\n    X_(i+1) = X_i-h_i*g_i, g_i = grad F(X_i), 0 <= i <= n,\n\nwhere only i<n is used in the recursion. The same assertion covers any\nEuclidean realization of the Gram matrix in (2). The anchor, function, and\nvalues are unrestricted apart from these requirements. Define\n\n    R = dist(X_0,argmin F), F_star = min F,\n    V_chr = 2(F(X_n)-F_star)/R^2,\n    T_path = sum_(i<n) h_i*Gamma_i.\n\nThen R>0, and the claimed bounds are\n\n    T_path < 800 R,                                         (3)\n    ||X_i-X_star|| <= 129 R for every i,                     (4)\n    Gamma_n <= R[(3/5)^(N/8)+2^23/N^2],                     (5)\n    V_chr <= 512(3/5)^(N/8)+2^32/N^2.                       (6)\n\nHere X_star is a true closest minimizer. Since the exact original benchmark\nobeys B_n <= 1+4*n^p, p=log_2(3), by fact `b98bb807d514e80d`,\n\n    B_n V_chr <= (1+4*n^p)[512(3/5)^((n+1)/8)\n                                      +2^32/(n+1)^2] -> 0.     (7)\n\nThe bounds are uniform over ALL positive amplitudes and all actual legal\nrealizations of (2). They concern this fixed witness class, not unrestricted\nW_n(h). Failure on these original dyadic words prevents this class from\nestablishing the all-schedule matching lower bound. It does not decide the\nproject's minimax question.\n\nFor L-smooth functions, take F=f/L and g_i=grad f(X_i)/L. The same proof then\nuses the required normalization 2(f(X_n)-f_star)/(L R^2).\n\n## 2. Necessary original inequalities and short-step consequences\n\nThe set of minimizers is nonempty and closed. A bounded minimizing sequence\nfor distance from X_0 has a convergent subsequence, so a closest minimizer\nX_star exists. Positive Gamma_0 implies X_0 is not a minimizer, hence R>0.\n\nWrite f_i=F(X_i)-F_star. Accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290` supplies all\noriginal ordered inequalities, including the optimum:\n\n    Q_ij = f_i-f_j + <g_i,X_j-X_i> + ||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0.\n\nAdding the two orientations gives\n\n    ||g_i-g_j||^2 <= <g_i-g_j,X_i-X_j>.                         (8)\n\nIn particular <g_i,X_i-X_star> >= ||g_i||^2. Norms in (2) are exactly\nGamma_i. Smoothness and grad F(X_star)=0 also give\nGamma_i <= ||X_i-X_star||. For a physical step 0<h<=2, (8) gives\n\n    ||X_(i+1)-X_star||^2\n       <= ||X_i-X_star||^2-h(2-h)||g_i||^2,\n    ||g_(i+1)|| <= ||g_i||.                                  (9)\n\nFor the second assertion, put d=g_(i+1)-g_i. Then\n-h<d,g_i> >= ||d||^2, and expansion of ||g_i+d||^2 gives an increment\nat most (1-2/h)||d||^2 <= 0. These facts will only be applied to the\nexplicit short physical steps l,k,l, all strictly between 0 and 2.\n\nWe will use the following rational bounds:\n\n    7/5 < l < 3/2, 1 < k < 2, delta*c_chr < 5/2,\n    epsilon < 1/65536, eta < 1/65536,\n    p_chr >= 999/1000.                                      (10)\n\nFor example sqrt(2)<99/70 by squaring, so\nc_chr<(169/70)(1025/1024)<49/20. Also delta>99/100, giving\ndelta/c_chr>(99/100)/(49/20)>2/5; c_chr>2 gives the upper bound on l.\nFinally delta*c_chr=sigma(1-1/m^2)<sigma<5/2. The bounds on epsilon,eta\nfollow from m>=1024. No decimal approximation is needed.\n\n## 3. A uniform movement bound before taking any small-mixture estimate\n\nSelect in (1) the component B=1 and all prefix signs common. Its mass is\n\n    lambda = (1-epsilon/2)(1-eta), omega = 1-lambda.\n\nConditional on it, the only variable sign is C_1, with mean -7/10; all\ncharacter bits except s_1 are zero. Thus its unit sample directions are\ntwo unit vectors a_0,b_0, in the exact period a_0,a_0,b_0,b_0, with\n<a_0,b_0>=-beta_0, beta_0=7/10. The remaining measure has mass omega.\nIn a direct-sum Euclidean realization of the SAME Gram matrix,\n\n    g_i = Gamma_i (sqrt(lambda)*u_i, sqrt(omega)*z_i),\n    u_i in {a_0,b_0}, ||z_i||=1.                             (11)\n\nSplitting a probability mixture proves this representation directly. An\nisometry of gradient spans, extended to the initial displacement and any\northogonal component, preserves every scalar product and the initial\ndistance. Thus it is legitimate for every realization in the statement.\n\nFix a block start t=4r, 0<=r<N/4. Before t put\n\n    P_a = sum_(j<t,u_j=a_0) h_j*Gamma_j,\n    P_b = sum_(j<t,u_j=b_0) h_j*Gamma_j, T=P_a+P_b.\n\nTranslate X_star to zero. If X_0=(x^c,x^r), then\n\n    X_t = (x^c-sqrt(lambda)(P_a*a_0+P_b*b_0),\n                         x^r-sqrt(omega)*W), ||W||<=T.\n\nDefine normalized coarse coordinates\n\n    A_x = <a_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)-P_a+beta_0*P_b,\n    B_x = <b_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)-P_b+beta_0*P_a.\n\nThe first two steps in the block have sizes l,k and both coarse directions\na_0. Their total amplitude-weighted mass is\n\n    D_a = l*Gamma_t+k*Gamma_(t+1)>0.\n\nTheir displacement is (sqrt(lambda)*D_a*a_0,sqrt(omega)*Z), ||Z||<=D_a.\nBoth are short steps. The two distance inequalities in (9), added or\napplied successively, therefore give\n\n    lambda*D_a^2\n       <= 2 lambda*D_a*A_x\n            +2 sqrt(omega)(R+sqrt(omega)*T)*D_a.\n\nWith e=omega/lambda and r_0=sqrt(omega)*R/lambda, division gives\n\n    D_a <= 2 A_x+2(r_0+eT).                                 (12)\n\nAt sample t+2 the coarse gradient direction is b_0. Its optimum\ninequality <g_(t+2),X_(t+2)> >= ||g_(t+2)||^2 > 0 implies, after\nbounding the remainder component by its norm,\n\n    B_x+(beta_0+e)D_a+r_0+eT >= 0.\n\nSubstitution of (12), with positive coefficient beta_0+e, yields\n\n    B_x+2(beta_0+e)A_x+[1+2(beta_0+e)](r_0+eT) >= 0.          (13)\n\nHere omega<=epsilon/2+eta<1/65536, so\n\n    e<1/1000, 1/sqrt(lambda)<1001/1000,\n    sqrt(omega)/lambda<1/255.\n\nExpanding (13) gives a bound on the nonnegative masses:\n\n    c_a P_a+c_b P_b\n       <= <b_0+2(beta_0+e)a_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)\n                           +[1+2(beta_0+e)]r_0,\n\n    c_a = beta_0+(1-2beta_0)e-2e^2,\n    c_b = 1-2beta_0^2-(1+4beta_0)e-2e^2.                    (14)\n\nBoth coefficients exceed 1/64: their lower bounds at 0<=e<=1/1000\nare 699598/1000000 and 16198/1000000. Also\n\n    ||b_0+2(beta_0+e)a_0||^2 = 1+4e(beta_0+e)\n                                      < (1002/1000)^2,\n    1+2(beta_0+e)<5/2.\n\nThe right side of (14) is less than\n[(1002/1000)(1001/1000)+(5/2)/255]R < 2R. Therefore\n\n    T < 128 R at EVERY block start.                         (15)\n\nThis bound has been derived with arbitrary amplitudes still present; no\nbounded-amplitude hypothesis or conditioning assumption has been imposed.\nAt a block start ||X_t||<=R+T<=129R. Its three short steps l,k,l preserve\nthis bound by (9). These blocks cover every sample 0,...,n, proving (4).\nEvery Gamma_i<=129R as well. At the last block the mass added after its\nstart is at most (2l+k)129R<5*129R. Thus\n\n    T_path < (128+645)R < 800R,\n    K_path := R+T_path < 1024R.                             (16)\n\nIn particular every gradient norm and every subinterval's total mass is\nbounded by K_path. This estimate precedes the perturbation argument below.\n\n## 4. Keep the finite epsilon component exactly: four coarse directions\n\nNow split (1) in a DIFFERENT way: keep the whole common-prefix component,\nof mass 1-eta, and regard only the conditional-product component of mass\neta as the remainder. In the common component write C_j=V for every j.\nThe four unit directions for indices modulo 4 are\n\n    a_e=1, a_o=B, b_e=V, b_o=B V                              (17)\n\nin the finite real probability space of (B,V). Their correlations are\n\n    p = E B = 1-epsilon,\n    z = E V = -7/10+4epsilon/5,\n    w = E B V = -7/10-epsilon/10,\n\n    <a_e,a_o>=<b_e,b_o>=p,\n    <a_o,b_o>=<a_e,b_e>=z,\n    <a_e,b_o>=<a_o,b_e>=w.                                 (18)\n\nThese formulas average the stated conditional means in (1). In particular\nfinite epsilon has NOT been replaced by zero. Put beta=-z. We have\n\n    69/100 <= beta <= 7/10, p>0, p-z*w>0,\n    1-k*z^2 >= 1-2(7/10)^2 = 1/50.                         (19)\n\nThe positivity p-zw follows already from p>=999/1000,\n|z|<=7/10 and |w|<701/1000.\n\nLet v_i denote the coarse direction (17) at sample i, and let u_i denote\nits unit direction under the FULL measure (1). Splitting the probability\nmixture gives, for ALL original i,j,\n\n    |<u_i,u_j>-<v_i,v_j>| <= 2 eta.                         (20)\n\nFor a pair with amplitudes A,B and intervening total mass T_ij, replacing\nall its directions by their coarse versions changes the cocoercivity\nexpression <g_i-g_j,X_i-X_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2 by at most\n\n    2 eta(A+B)T_ij +4 eta A B\n           <= 4 eta K_path(A+B).                           (21)\n\nThe first term comes from the actual displacement sum, one scalar product\nat a time; the second comes from the cross term in the squared gradient\ndifference. Individual squared norms are unchanged. The final inequality\nuses (16) and 2AB<=K_path(A+B). Thus (21) accounts explicitly for the\nwhole amplitude-weighted trajectory, rather than merely the Gram entries.\n\n## 5. Every consecutive odd-b return can grow only by O(eta K_path)\n\nLet j=4r+3 and suppose j+4<=n. These are consecutive odd-b samples.\nTheir four intervening steps and directions are\n\n    j:     H on b_o, amplitude B_0=Gamma_j,\n    j+1:   l on a_e, amplitude A_0=Gamma_(j+1),\n    j+2:   k on a_o, amplitude C_0=Gamma_(j+2),\n    j+3:   l on b_e, amplitude E_0=Gamma_(j+3),\n    j+4:            b_o, amplitude D_0=Gamma_(j+4).\n\nThe physical H may be arbitrarily large. No amplitude ratios are imposed.\nTemporarily set a=a_o, b=b_o, tau=<a,b>=z=-beta, and\n\n    Z = -H B_0 b-l A_0 a_e,\n    X = <b,Z>, Y=<a-tau*b,Z>.\n\nBy (18),(19),\n\n    Y = -l A_0(p-z*w) <= 0.                                (22)\n\nThe ORIGINAL pair between j and j+2, together with (21), therefore gives\n\n    C_0^2+B_0^2-2tau B_0 C_0\n       <= (tau C_0-B_0)X+C_0 Y+e_0(C_0+B_0)\n       <= (tau C_0-B_0)X+e_0(C_0+B_0),                     (23)\n\nwhere e_0=4 eta K_path. Suppose Delta=D_0-B_0>0. The ORIGINAL pair\nbetween j and j+4 similarly gives\n\n    Delta[X-k C_0 tau-l E_0 p]\n       >= Delta^2-e_0(D_0+B_0).\n\nConsequently\n\n    X >= Delta+k C_0 tau+l E_0 p-e_0(D_0+B_0)/Delta.         (24)\n\nSubstitute (24) into (23). Its coefficient tau C_0-B_0 is negative.\nPutting M=B_0+beta C_0>0 gives exactly\n\n    (1-k*tau^2)C_0^2 + tau(k-2)B_0 C_0+B_0^2\n           +M Delta+M l E_0 p\n       <= e_0(C_0+B_0)+M e_0(D_0+B_0)/Delta.                (25)\n\nEvery discarded term on the left is nonnegative: tau<0, k<2, p>0.\nIt follows from (19) that the left side is at least\n\n    (C_0^2+B_0^2)/100 + M Delta.\n\nOn the right, D_0+B_0=Delta+2B_0, so it is at most\n\n    e_0[(1+beta)C_0+2B_0] + (2e_0/Delta)B_0 M\n       <= 4e_0 M + (3e_0/Delta)(C_0^2+B_0^2).             (26)\n\nHere beta>=69/100 proves the first bound, and\nB_0 M <= (3/2)(B_0^2+C_0^2) proves the second.\nIf Delta>=1024e_0, (26) is at most\n\n    M Delta/256 + (3/1024)(C_0^2+B_0^2),\n\nstrictly less than the lower bound for (25). This is a contradiction.\nThus every such return obeys\n\n    Gamma_(j+4) <= Gamma_j + d_amp,\n    d_amp = 4096 eta K_path <= 2^22 eta R.                  (27)\n\nThis uses two pairs of ORIGINAL samples and their actual displacement\nsums. The profiles on either side need not match in any way. If eta=0,\nthe same argument with zero error directly rules out Delta>0, so the\nexact four-ray boundary has nonincreasing odd-b amplitudes.\n\n## 6. Every other return contracts by 3/5, for the full finite measure\n\nFor any original bridge of level q>=1 and its following leaf, let the\nthree amplitudes be A,M,D and the two steps 1+u,l. The relative character\nsigns of the three samples are 1,B C_q,C_q. This follows because adding\none to an odd binary label with q trailing ones flips bits 0,...,q; the\nfollowing leaf flips bit 0 once more. Therefore the FULL law, including\nits conditional-product part, gives correlations w,p,z in that order.\nBoth mixture components have the same individual conditional prefix mean.\n\nThe two original inequalities between the endpoints give the exact sum\n\n    0 >= -u A^2+D^2+(u-1)z A D+l M(pD-wA).                 (28)\n\nThe following leaf is short, so (9) gives M>=D. Since p>0 and w<0,\npD-wA>0. Hence the ratio q_amp=D/A satisfies\n\n    F_u(q_amp) <= 0,\n    F_u(q) = (1+lp)q^2+[(u-1)z-lw]q-u.                    (29)\n\nThis is a necessary inequality with arbitrary adjacent slack. Its positive\nleading coefficient and negative constant term give exactly one positive\nroot; F_u(q)<=0 for positive q places q below that root.\n\nAt level 1, u=delta<1. Bounds (10),(18) give\n\n    1+lp >= 11993/5000,\n    (u-1)z-lw >= (7/10)l >= 49/50,\n    F_delta(1/2) >= 1793/20000 > 0.\n\nThus every low bridge followed by its leaf has gain less than 1/2.\n\nAt level 2, u=delta*c_chr lies between 1 and 5/2. Since z>=-7/10,\nw<=-7/10 and l>7/5,\n\n    (u-1)z-lw >= (7/10)(1+l-u) >= -7/100,\n    F_u(6/5) >= 108748/125000 > 0.\n\nThus every level-2 bridge followed by its leaf has gain less than 6/5.\nIf the H in section 5 has level 2, its return is exactly the concatenation\nof this compound step and a level-1 compound step. Therefore\n\n    Gamma_(j+4) <= (3/5) Gamma_j at every level-2 return.    (30)\n\nNo value, anchor, copy profile, or equality assumption was used in this\ncalculation. The even samples and their physical leaves are retained.\n\n## 7. The exact block count, terminal objective, and benchmark\n\nThere are K=N/4 odd-b samples. Put B_r=Gamma_(4r+3), 0<=r<K.\nFor 0<=r<K-1 the bridge at index 4r+3 has level\n\n    nu_2(4r+4)=2+nu_2(r+1).\n\nIt has level 2 precisely when r is even. Since K is even, (27),(30) give\n\n    B_(2s+1) <= theta B_(2s), theta=3/5,\n    B_(2s+2) <= B_(2s+1)+d_amp,\n\nwhenever the indicated indices exist. The final return is a contraction.\nIteration of these scalar inequalities, keeping the actual K, yields\n\n    B_(K-1) <= theta^(K/2) B_0\n                   + [theta/(1-theta)] d_amp\n             = theta^(N/8) B_0+(3/2)d_amp.                 (31)\n\nThe first three steps are l,k,l<2, so B_0=Gamma_3<=Gamma_0<=R.\nEquations (16),(27),(31) prove (5), since\n(3/2)*2^22 < 2^23 and eta=N^-2. The terminal sample is exactly\nn=4(K-1)+3. Neither a discarded suffix nor a free extra terminal sample\nis present.\n\nConvexity at X_n gives\n\n    F(X_n)-F_star <= <g_n,X_n-X_star>\n                     <= 129R Gamma_n.\n\nAfter division by the TRUE distance squared, this gives\nV_chr<=258[(3/5)^(N/8)+2^23/N^2], which implies (6).\nThere is no replacement of distance by an inverse-Gram anchor norm.\n\nFor the original B_n, accepted `b98bb807d514e80d` gives (7). Its first\nterm tends to zero because a geometric exponential in N dominates N^p;\nits second does because p=log_2(3)<2. Both limits hold uniformly over the\nentire amplitude/anchor/function class. The horizon remains n=2^m-1.\n\n## 8. The fixed finite class is nonempty\n\nThis is a nonvacuity construction, not a competitive lower witness. It also\nshows that the obstruction does not mean the Gram itself is illegal.\nFor the SAME full-support weights (1) and the SAME actual word, put\n\n    H_max=max_i h_i,\n    kappa_chr=min(1,(min_i h_i-1)/4)>0,\n    q_decay=kappa_chr/[8(H_max+kappa_chr+1)],\n    Gamma_i=q_decay^i, f_i=(kappa_chr+1/2)Gamma_i^2.\n\nAll N characters are present and have positive weight, so the character\nmatrix is invertible. Indeed\nsum_s chi_s(i)chi_s(j)=N for i=j and 0 otherwise, by pairing the sign\nof a differing bit. Hence the Gram K_ij=<g_i,g_j> is positive definite.\nDefine\n\n    b_i=(kappa_chr+1)Gamma_i^2+sum_(t<i)h_t K_ti,\n    X_0=sum_i (K^(-1)b)_i g_i,\n    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t.\n\nThese finite formulas fix one anchor before GD is run. They give\n<X_i,g_i>=(kappa_chr+1)Gamma_i^2. For i<j direct expansion gives\n\n    Q_ij=(kappa_chr+1-h_i)Gamma_i^2-kappa_chr Gamma_j^2\n                          -sum_(i<t<j)h_t K_it-K_ij,\n    Q_ji=(kappa_chr+1)Gamma_j^2-kappa_chr Gamma_i^2\n                          +(h_i-1)K_ij+sum_(i<t<j)h_t K_jt.\n\nUsing |K_it|<=Gamma_i Gamma_t, q_decay<1/2 and h_i-1>=4kappa_chr,\nthe first residual divided by Gamma_i^2 is at most\n\n    -3kappa_chr+H_max*q_decay/(1-q_decay)+q_decay\n       <= -3kappa_chr+2(H_max+1)q_decay\n       <= -11kappa_chr/4 < 0.\n\nThe second is at most\n\n    -kappa_chr+(kappa_chr+1)q_decay^2\n                             +H_max*q_decay/(1-q_decay)\n       <= -kappa_chr+(2H_max+kappa_chr+1)q_decay\n       <= -3kappa_chr/4 < 0.\n\nFor the optimum jet (0,0,0), Q_(i,star)=0 and\nQ_(star,i)=-kappa_chr Gamma_i^2<0. Thus ALL original ordered pairs,\nincluding the optimum and terminal, have been checked.\n\nFor an explicit one-function realization, let\n\n    C_poly=conv(0,g_0,...,g_n),\n    F_poly(X)=max_(v in C_poly) [<X,v>-||v||^2/2].\n\nThe unique maximizer is the nearest-point projection P_(C_poly)(X).\nIts normal condition is <X-P_(C_poly)(X),v-P_(C_poly)(X)><=0 for every\nv in C_poly. Adding this condition at X and Y proves that the projection\nis 1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing expressions bounds the\nfirst-order remainder for F_poly between 0 and ||X-Y||^2, so F_poly is\ndifferentiable with gradient P_(C_poly), convex, finite, and 1-smooth.\n\nAt each prescribed sample the displayed residuals say\n<X_i-g_i,g_j-g_i>=Q_ji<=0, and\n<X_i-g_i,-g_i>=-kappa_chr Gamma_i^2<0. Thus the normal condition holds\non every vertex and their convex hull: grad F_poly(X_i)=g_i and\nF_poly(X_i)=f_i. The fixed function therefore follows the stated GD.\nIt is nonnegative and minimized at zero. Its full minimizer set is\n{Z:<Z,g_i><=0 for all i}, by the same normal condition at the zero vertex.\nThe true distance to this polar cone is positive and is the R used in\n(3)--(7); no claim equates it with ||X_0||.\n\n## 9. Scope and unresolved changes\n\nThe exclusion covers EVERY positive amplitude choice for (1),(2), including\nunbounded, history-dependent, nonsaturating, and nonmatching choices, all\nanchors and values that actually interpolate, and every m>=1024. The separate initial mass proof pays the full\nepsilon/2+eta remainder before bounding ANY amplitudes.\n\nThe same derivation also covers the exact eta=0 boundary, and epsilon=0\nwhen considered with the same physical word: zero perturbation yields\nnonincreasing returns directly. It does not assume positive definiteness\nfor the upper bound. Positive definiteness is used only for the separate\nfull-support nonvacuity example.\n\nDifferent measures and different binary labels remain outside. In\nparticular the coercive margins 1-2*(7/10)^2 and 1-k*z^2 are essential;\na geometry at or beyond absolute ray correlation 1/sqrt(2) is not covered\nby this proof. The returned\nshort-step theorems cannot be applied to this whole word. Every short-step\nfact actually used is derived above from accepted interpolation. This\nremains the already audited finite interpolation regime; no additional\nunread body of external theory is a premise.\n\nThe result is a full scoped OBSTRUCTION CANDIDATE, not an accepted fact.\nThe current assignment prohibits fact_submit. No solver,\nparameter sweep, installation, or fact submission is used. The only\ncorrectness predecessors are accepted facts\n`1252b8823bb5a290` (original smooth-convex interpolation) and\n`b98bb807d514e80d` (the exact benchmark and its elementary bound). All other\narguments used here are supplied within this document. In particular the\nearlier named-amplitude obstruction is not a predecessor.\n\nThis document reproduces every needed calculation in one joint proof.\nThe earlier unverified draft is awareness, not a correctness predecessor.\nThe amplitudes below, at queried samples only, are arbitrary positive numbers.\nThey may depend on the entire known chronological history, the enclosing\nhorizon, and the full word.\nNo two copies of a subword are assumed to have matching amplitude profiles.\n\n## 1. Statement, unchanged word, and every probability atom\n\nFix an integer m >= 1024. Define\n\n    sigma = 1+sqrt(2), delta = 1-1/m,\n    c_chr = sigma(1+1/m), a_chr = delta/c_chr,\n    N = 2^m, n = N-1, epsilon = 1/N, eta = 1/N^2,\n    l = 1+a_chr, k = 1+delta.\n\nThe actual word is H_chr,0 empty and\n\n    H_chr,j+1 = (H_chr,j, 1+a_chr*c_chr^j, H_chr,j), 0 <= j < m.\n\nThus h_i = 1+a_chr*c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)), 0 <= i < n, where nu_2(q)\nis the exponent of 2 dividing the positive integer q. Every subword retains\nthe same enclosing-m parameters. There are n physical steps and N samples.\n\nGive sample i its standard m-bit binary label (i_0,...,i_(m-1)). For\ns in {0,1}^m define\n\n    chi_s(i) = (-1)^(sum_j s_j i_j),\n    B(s) = (-1)^s_0,\n    C_j(s) = (-1)^(s_1+...+s_j), 1 <= j < m.\n\nThese are real signs. The map from s to (B,C_1,...,C_(m-1)) is bijective:\n(-1)^s_1=C_1 and (-1)^s_j=C_(j-1)C_j for j>=2. Put\n\n    mu_+ = -7/10, mu_- = 9/10, p_chr = 1-epsilon,\n\n    A_b(c_1,...,c_(m-1))\n      = (1+mu_b)/2 * 1_(all c_j=1)\n          + (1-mu_b)/2 * 1_(all c_j=-1),\n    P_b(c_1,...,c_(m-1)) = product_j (1+mu_b*c_j)/2,\n\n    w_s = (1+p_chr*B(s))/2\n             * [(1-eta) A_(B(s))(C_1(s),...,C_(m-1)(s))\n                     + eta P_(B(s))(C_1(s),...,C_(m-1)(s))].       (1)\n\nEach conditional law sums to one. Every atom is strictly positive because\nepsilon,eta>0 and both conditional means lie strictly between -1 and 1.\nThis is exactly the assigned finite probability law.\n\nFor ANY positive amplitudes Gamma_0,...,Gamma_(n-1), prescribe only\n\n    g_i = (Gamma_i*sqrt(w_s)*chi_s(i))_s, 0 <= i < n.               (2)\n\nThere is NO character, norm, span, or sign constraint on the actual\nterminal gradient g_n=grad F(X_n).\n\nSuppose these are the actual gradients of ONE finite-dimensional convex\ndifferentiable 1-smooth function F with a minimizer, along its actual GD:\n\n    X_(i+1) = X_i-h_i*g_i, 0 <= i < n,\n    g_i = grad F(X_i), 0 <= i <= n.\n\nThe same assertion covers any\nEuclidean realization of the Gram matrix in (2). The anchor, function, and\nvalues are unrestricted apart from these requirements. Define\n\n    R = dist(X_0,argmin F), F_star = min F,\n    V_chr = 2(F(X_n)-F_star)/R^2,\n    T_path = sum_(i<n) h_i*Gamma_i.\n\nThen R>0, and the claimed bounds are\n\n    T_path < 800 R,                                         (3)\n    ||X_i-X_star|| <= 129 R for every i,                     (4)\n    ||g_n|| <= R[2(3/5)^(N/8)+2^24/N^2],                   (5)\n    V_chr <= 1024(3/5)^(N/8)+2^33/N^2.                     (6)\n\nHere X_star is a true closest minimizer. Since the exact original benchmark\nobeys B_n <= 1+4*n^p, p=log_2(3), by fact `b98bb807d514e80d`,\n\n    B_n V_chr <= (1+4*n^p)[1024(3/5)^((n+1)/8)\n                                      +2^33/(n+1)^2] -> 0.     (7)\n\nThe bounds are uniform over ALL positive amplitudes and all actual legal\nrealizations of (2). They concern this fixed witness class, not unrestricted\nW_n(h). Failure on these original dyadic words prevents this class from\nestablishing the all-schedule matching lower bound. It does not decide the\nproject's minimax question.\n\nFor L-smooth functions, take F=f/L and g_i=grad f(X_i)/L. The same proof then\nuses the required normalization 2(f(X_n)-f_star)/(L R^2).\n\n## 2. Necessary original inequalities and short-step consequences\n\nThe set of minimizers is nonempty and closed. A bounded minimizing sequence\nfor distance from X_0 has a convergent subsequence, so a closest minimizer\nX_star exists. Positive Gamma_0 implies X_0 is not a minimizer, hence R>0.\n\nWrite f_i=F(X_i)-F_star. Accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290` supplies all\noriginal ordered inequalities, including the optimum:\n\n    Q_ij = f_i-f_j + <g_i,X_j-X_i> + ||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0.\n\nAdding the two orientations gives\n\n    ||g_i-g_j||^2 <= <g_i-g_j,X_i-X_j>.                         (8)\n\nIn particular <g_i,X_i-X_star> >= ||g_i||^2. At queried samples i<n,\nthe norms in (2) are exactly Gamma_i. The terminal norm is ||g_n||.\nSmoothness and grad F(X_star)=0 also give Gamma_i <= ||X_i-X_star|| for\ni<n. For a physical step 0<h<=2, (8) gives\n\n    ||X_(i+1)-X_star||^2\n       <= ||X_i-X_star||^2-h(2-h)||g_i||^2,\n    ||g_(i+1)|| <= ||g_i||.                                  (9)\n\nFor the second assertion, put d=g_(i+1)-g_i. Then\n-h<d,g_i> >= ||d||^2, and expansion of ||g_i+d||^2 gives an increment\nat most (1-2/h)||d||^2 <= 0. These facts will only be applied to the\nexplicit short physical steps l,k,l, all strictly between 0 and 2.\n\nWe will use the following rational bounds:\n\n    7/5 < l < 3/2, 1 < k < 2, delta*c_chr < 5/2,\n    epsilon < 1/65536, eta < 1/65536,\n    p_chr >= 999/1000.                                      (10)\n\nFor example sqrt(2)<99/70 by squaring, so\nc_chr<(169/70)(1025/1024)<49/20. Also delta>99/100, giving\ndelta/c_chr>(99/100)/(49/20)>2/5; c_chr>2 gives the upper bound on l.\nFinally delta*c_chr=sigma(1-1/m^2)<sigma<5/2. The bounds on epsilon,eta\nfollow from m>=1024. No decimal approximation is needed.\n\n## 3. A uniform movement bound before taking any small-mixture estimate\n\nSelect in (1) the component B=1 and all prefix signs common. Its mass is\n\n    lambda = (1-epsilon/2)(1-eta), omega = 1-lambda.\n\nConditional on it, the only variable sign is C_1, with mean -7/10; all\ncharacter bits except s_1 are zero. Thus its unit sample directions are\ntwo unit vectors a_0,b_0, in the exact period a_0,a_0,b_0,b_0, with\n<a_0,b_0>=-beta_0, beta_0=7/10. The remaining measure has mass omega.\nIn a direct-sum Euclidean realization of the SAME Gram matrix,\n\n    g_i = Gamma_i (sqrt(lambda)*u_i, sqrt(omega)*z_i), i<n,\n    u_i in {a_0,b_0}, ||z_i||=1.                             (11)\n\nSplitting a probability mixture proves this representation directly. An\nisometry of gradient spans, extended to the initial displacement and any\northogonal component, preserves every scalar product and the initial\ndistance. Thus it is legitimate for every realization in the statement.\n\nFix a block start t=4r, 0<=r<N/4. Before t put\n\n    P_a = sum_(j<t,u_j=a_0) h_j*Gamma_j,\n    P_b = sum_(j<t,u_j=b_0) h_j*Gamma_j, T=P_a+P_b.\n\nTranslate X_star to zero. If X_0=(x^c,x^r), then\n\n    X_t = (x^c-sqrt(lambda)(P_a*a_0+P_b*b_0),\n                         x^r-sqrt(omega)*W), ||W||<=T.\n\nDefine normalized coarse coordinates\n\n    A_x = <a_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)-P_a+beta_0*P_b,\n    B_x = <b_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)-P_b+beta_0*P_a.\n\nThe first two steps in the block have sizes l,k and both coarse directions\na_0. Their total amplitude-weighted mass is\n\n    D_a = l*Gamma_t+k*Gamma_(t+1)>0.\n\nTheir displacement is (sqrt(lambda)*D_a*a_0,sqrt(omega)*Z), ||Z||<=D_a.\nBoth are short steps. The two distance inequalities in (9), added or\napplied successively, therefore give\n\n    lambda*D_a^2\n       <= 2 lambda*D_a*A_x\n            +2 sqrt(omega)(R+sqrt(omega)*T)*D_a.\n\nWith e=omega/lambda and r_0=sqrt(omega)*R/lambda, division gives\n\n    D_a <= 2 A_x+2(r_0+eT).                                 (12)\n\nAt sample t+2 the coarse gradient direction is b_0. Its optimum\ninequality <g_(t+2),X_(t+2)> >= ||g_(t+2)||^2 > 0 implies, after\nbounding the remainder component by its norm,\n\n    B_x+(beta_0+e)D_a+r_0+eT >= 0.\n\nSubstitution of (12), with positive coefficient beta_0+e, yields\n\n    B_x+2(beta_0+e)A_x+[1+2(beta_0+e)](r_0+eT) >= 0.          (13)\n\nHere omega<=epsilon/2+eta<1/65536, so\n\n    e<1/1000, 1/sqrt(lambda)<1001/1000,\n    sqrt(omega)/lambda<1/255.\n\nExpanding (13) gives a bound on the nonnegative masses:\n\n    c_a P_a+c_b P_b\n       <= <b_0+2(beta_0+e)a_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)\n                           +[1+2(beta_0+e)]r_0,\n\n    c_a = beta_0+(1-2beta_0)e-2e^2,\n    c_b = 1-2beta_0^2-(1+4beta_0)e-2e^2.                    (14)\n\nBoth coefficients exceed 1/64: their lower bounds at 0<=e<=1/1000\nare 699598/1000000 and 16198/1000000. Also\n\n    ||b_0+2(beta_0+e)a_0||^2 = 1+4e(beta_0+e)\n                                      < (1002/1000)^2,\n    1+2(beta_0+e)<5/2.\n\nThe right side of (14) is less than\n[(1002/1000)(1001/1000)+(5/2)/255]R < 2R. Therefore\n\n    T < 128 R at EVERY block start.                         (15)\n\nThis bound has been derived with arbitrary amplitudes still present; no\nbounded-amplitude hypothesis or conditioning assumption has been imposed.\nAt a block start ||X_t||<=R+T<=129R. Its three short steps l,k,l preserve\nthis bound by (9). These blocks cover every sample 0,...,n, proving (4).\nEvery queried Gamma_i<=129R as well; smoothness also bounds ||g_n||.\nAt the last block the mass added after its start is at most\n(2l+k)129R<5*129R. Thus\n\n    T_path < (128+645)R < 800R,\n    K_path := R+T_path < 1024R.                             (16)\n\nIn particular every gradient norm and every subinterval's total mass is\nbounded by K_path. This estimate precedes the perturbation argument below.\n\n## 4. Keep the finite epsilon component exactly: four coarse directions\n\nNow split (1) in a DIFFERENT way: keep the whole common-prefix component,\nof mass 1-eta, and regard only the conditional-product component of mass\neta as the remainder. In the common component write C_j=V for every j.\nThe four unit directions for indices modulo 4 are\n\n    a_e=1, a_o=B, b_e=V, b_o=B V                              (17)\n\nin the finite real probability space of (B,V). Their correlations are\n\n    p = E B = 1-epsilon,\n    z = E V = -7/10+4epsilon/5,\n    w = E B V = -7/10-epsilon/10,\n\n    <a_e,a_o>=<b_e,b_o>=p,\n    <a_o,b_o>=<a_e,b_e>=z,\n    <a_e,b_o>=<a_o,b_e>=w.                                 (18)\n\nThese formulas average the stated conditional means in (1). In particular\nfinite epsilon has NOT been replaced by zero. Put beta=-z. We have\n\n    69/100 <= beta <= 7/10, p>0, p-z*w>0,\n    1-k*z^2 >= 1-2(7/10)^2 = 1/50.                         (19)\n\nThe positivity p-zw follows already from p>=999/1000,\n|z|<=7/10 and |w|<701/1000.\n\nLet v_i denote the coarse direction (17) at sample i, and let u_i denote\nits unit direction under the FULL measure (1). Splitting the probability\nmixture gives, for ALL original queried i,j<n,\n\n    |<u_i,u_j>-<v_i,v_j>| <= 2 eta.                         (20)\n\nFor a pair with amplitudes A,B and intervening total mass T_ij, replacing\nall its directions by their coarse versions changes the cocoercivity\nexpression <g_i-g_j,X_i-X_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2 by at most\n\n    2 eta(A+B)T_ij +4 eta A B\n           <= 4 eta K_path(A+B).                           (21)\n\nThe first term comes from the actual displacement sum, one scalar product\nat a time; the second comes from the cross term in the squared gradient\ndifference. Individual squared norms are unchanged. The final inequality\nuses (16) and 2AB<=K_path(A+B). Thus (21) accounts explicitly for the\nwhole amplitude-weighted trajectory, rather than merely the Gram entries.\n\n## 5. Every consecutive odd-b return can grow only by O(eta K_path)\n\nLet j=4r+3 and suppose j+4<n. These are consecutive odd-b samples.\nTheir four intervening steps and directions are\n\n    j:     H on b_o, amplitude B_0=Gamma_j,\n    j+1:   l on a_e, amplitude A_0=Gamma_(j+1),\n    j+2:   k on a_o, amplitude C_0=Gamma_(j+2),\n    j+3:   l on b_e, amplitude E_0=Gamma_(j+3),\n    j+4:            b_o, amplitude D_0=Gamma_(j+4).\n\nThe physical H may be arbitrarily large. No amplitude ratios are imposed.\nTemporarily set a=a_o, b=b_o, tau=<a,b>=z=-beta, and\n\n    Z = -H B_0 b-l A_0 a_e,\n    X = <b,Z>, Y=<a-tau*b,Z>.\n\nBy (18),(19),\n\n    Y = -l A_0(p-z*w) <= 0.                                (22)\n\nThe ORIGINAL pair between j and j+2, together with (21), therefore gives\n\n    C_0^2+B_0^2-2tau B_0 C_0\n       <= (tau C_0-B_0)X+C_0 Y+e_0(C_0+B_0)\n       <= (tau C_0-B_0)X+e_0(C_0+B_0),                     (23)\n\nwhere e_0=4 eta K_path. Suppose Delta=D_0-B_0>0. The ORIGINAL pair\nbetween j and j+4 similarly gives\n\n    Delta[X-k C_0 tau-l E_0 p]\n       >= Delta^2-e_0(D_0+B_0).\n\nConsequently\n\n    X >= Delta+k C_0 tau+l E_0 p-e_0(D_0+B_0)/Delta.         (24)\n\nSubstitute (24) into (23). Its coefficient tau C_0-B_0 is negative.\nPutting M=B_0+beta C_0>0 gives exactly\n\n    (1-k*tau^2)C_0^2 + tau(k-2)B_0 C_0+B_0^2\n           +M Delta+M l E_0 p\n       <= e_0(C_0+B_0)+M e_0(D_0+B_0)/Delta.                (25)\n\nEvery discarded term on the left is nonnegative: tau<0, k<2, p>0.\nIt follows from (19) that the left side is at least\n\n    (C_0^2+B_0^2)/100 + M Delta.\n\nOn the right, D_0+B_0=Delta+2B_0, so it is at most\n\n    e_0[(1+beta)C_0+2B_0] + (2e_0/Delta)B_0 M\n       <= 4e_0 M + (3e_0/Delta)(C_0^2+B_0^2).             (26)\n\nHere beta>=69/100 proves the first bound, and\nB_0 M <= (3/2)(B_0^2+C_0^2) proves the second.\nIf Delta>=1024e_0, (26) is at most\n\n    M Delta/256 + (3/1024)(C_0^2+B_0^2),\n\nstrictly less than the lower bound for (25). This is a contradiction.\nThus every such return obeys\n\n    Gamma_(j+4) <= Gamma_j + d_amp,\n    d_amp = 4096 eta K_path <= 2^22 eta R.                  (27)\n\nThis uses two pairs of ORIGINAL samples and their actual displacement\nsums. The profiles on either side need not match in any way. If eta=0,\nthe same argument with zero error directly rules out Delta>0, so the\nexact four-ray boundary has nonincreasing odd-b amplitudes.\n\n## 6. Every other return contracts by 3/5, for the full finite measure\n\nFor any original bridge of level q>=1 and its following leaf, provided\nboth endpoints are queried, let the three amplitudes be A,M,D and the\ntwo steps 1+u,l. The relative character\nsigns of the three samples are 1,B C_q,C_q. This follows because adding\none to an odd binary label with q trailing ones flips bits 0,...,q; the\nfollowing leaf flips bit 0 once more. Therefore the FULL law, including\nits conditional-product part, gives correlations w,p,z in that order.\nBoth mixture components have the same individual conditional prefix mean.\n\nThe two original inequalities between the endpoints give the exact sum\n\n    0 >= -u A^2+D^2+(u-1)z A D+l M(pD-wA).                 (28)\n\nThe following leaf is short, so (9) gives M>=D. Since p>0 and w<0,\npD-wA>0. Hence the ratio q_amp=D/A satisfies\n\n    F_u(q_amp) <= 0,\n    F_u(q) = (1+lp)q^2+[(u-1)z-lw]q-u.                    (29)\n\nThis is a necessary inequality with arbitrary adjacent slack. Its positive\nleading coefficient and negative constant term give exactly one positive\nroot; F_u(q)<=0 for positive q places q below that root.\n\nAt level 1, u=delta<1. Bounds (10),(18) give\n\n    1+lp >= 11993/5000,\n    (u-1)z-lw >= (7/10)l >= 49/50,\n    F_delta(1/2) >= 1793/20000 > 0.\n\nThus every low bridge followed by its leaf has gain less than 1/2.\n\nAt level 2, u=delta*c_chr lies between 1 and 5/2. Since z>=-7/10,\nw<=-7/10 and l>7/5,\n\n    (u-1)z-lw >= (7/10)(1+l-u) >= -7/100,\n    F_u(6/5) >= 108748/125000 > 0.\n\nThus every level-2 bridge followed by its leaf has gain less than 6/5.\nIf the H in section 5 has level 2 and j+4<n, its return is exactly the concatenation\nof this compound step and a level-1 compound step. Therefore\n\n    Gamma_(j+4) <= (3/5) Gamma_j at every level-2 return.    (30)\n\nNo value, anchor, copy profile, or equality assumption was used in this\ncalculation. The even samples and their physical leaves are retained.\n\n## 7. Stop the character argument before the free terminal\n\nPut K=N/4, which is even. The QUERIED odd-b samples are\n\n    B_r = Gamma_(4r+3), 0 <= r <= K-2.\n\nThe missing next sample, at r=K-1, is exactly n and has NO prescribed\ncharacter direction. All returns used here end at or before n-4.\nFor 0<=r<=K-3, the actual bridge at index 4r+3 has level\n\n    nu_2(4r+4)=2+nu_2(r+1).\n\nIt has level 2 exactly when r is even. Equations (27),(30), proved above\nusing queried original pairs only, give\n\n    B_(2s+1) <= theta B_(2s), theta=3/5,\n    B_(2s+2) <= B_(2s+1)+d_amp,\n\nwhenever the displayed queried indices exist. Thus for E_s=B_(2s),\n\n    E_(s+1) <= theta E_s+d_amp.\n\nThere are K/2-1 such updates up to the final QUERIED odd-b sample n-4.\nExplicit induction gives, including the empty sum when s=0,\n\n    E_s <= theta^s B_0+d_amp sum_(q=0)^(s-1) theta^q.\n\nThe formula holds at s=0. Multiplication by theta and addition of d_amp\nchanges the sum to sum_(q=0)^s theta^q, proving its next instance. Hence\n\n    Gamma_(n-4) <= theta^(N/8-1) B_0+(5/2)d_amp.             (31)\n\nThe first three physical steps are l,k,l<2, so B_0=Gamma_3<=Gamma_0<=R.\nThe last FOUR physical steps, at indices n-4,...,n-1, are exactly\n\n    1+delta*c_chr, l, k, l.\n\nIndeed n-4=N-5 has nu_2((n-4)+1)=nu_2(N-4)=2;\nnu_2(N-3)=0, nu_2(N-2)=1, and nu_2(N-1)=0. The first two steps form a\nlevel-2 bridge plus leaf with queried endpoints n-4 and n-2. Applying\n(29) at these queried endpoints gives\n\n    Gamma_(n-2) <= (6/5) Gamma_(n-4).\n\nThe remaining actual steps k and l are both less than 2. Applying (9),\nwith no directional constraint on the actual terminal gradient g_n, gives\n\n    ||g_n|| <= Gamma_(n-1) <= Gamma_(n-2)\n             <= (6/5) Gamma_(n-4)\n             <= 2 theta^(N/8) R+3 d_amp.                   (32)\n\nHere (6/5)/theta=2. Since d_amp<=2^22 eta R, eta=N^-2 and\n3*2^22<2^24, this proves (5). No extra sample or step is introduced.\n\nThe movement proof in section 3 uses the optimum inequality only at each\nblock's sample t+2; in the final block this is n-1, still queried. It uses\nthe actual short final leaf only through distance monotonicity. Therefore\n||X_n-X_star||<=129R remains valid without prescribing g_n. Convexity gives\n\n    F(X_n)-F_star <= <g_n,X_n-X_star> <= 129R ||g_n||.\n\nAfter division by the TRUE distance squared,\n\n    V_chr <= 516 theta^(N/8)+258*2^24/N^2\n           <= 1024 theta^(N/8)+2^33/N^2.\n\nThe last comparison uses 516<1024 and 258<512. The original exact benchmark\nis not replaced by a mass surrogate: accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives\nB_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3)<2. A geometric exponential in N dominates N^p,\nand N^(p-2) tends to zero, proving (7) uniformly over the entire queried-\namplitude/anchor/function class. The horizon remains n=2^m-1.\n\nThe argument also covers the maximal prefix interpolant. For any legal\nqueried jets including an optimum, accepted 1252b8823bb5a290 constructs\na convex 1-smooth interpolant with that minimizer and with maximal terminal\nvalue. This is a realizing function in the present class, so the argument\nuses its TRUE closest-minimizer distance as R. Thus maximal terminal\ncompletion cannot evade (6). A reduced-feasible terminal value is at most\nthat maximal interpolant's value by the accepted fact. If the supplied\noptimum lies within distance 1 of X_0, then R<=1, so (6) also bounds twice\nthat reduced terminal value in the normalized finite formulation. This\nobservation imposes neither an incoming formal terminal inequality nor\na terminal character direction.\n\nFor clarity, every character-specific pair used in the proof has queried\nendpoints: the mass argument uses the optimum and t+2<=n-1; the return\nargument uses (j,j+2) and (j,j+4) with j+4<=n-4; and the final compound\npair is (n-4,n-2). The only terminal norm step is the actual short-step\ninequality for the realizing function. When starting from reduced formal\njets, that function is obtained before applying this inequality; the\nformal terminal gradient is never equated with its actual gradient.\n\nThe short-step hypothesis cannot be silently dropped: for F(x)=x^2/2,\na step h>2 from x!=0 produces gradient (1-h)x, whose norm exceeds |x|.\nHere both remaining physical steps k,l are strictly below 2, as proved\nin (10). This is a scope check, not a counterexample to the assertion.\n\n## 8. The fixed finite class is nonempty\n\nThis is a nonvacuity construction, not a competitive lower witness. It also\nshows that the obstruction does not mean the Gram itself is illegal.\nFor the SAME full-support weights (1) and the SAME actual word, this\nexample additionally prescribes the terminal character gradient. Define\nGamma_i below for every 0<=i<=n, and put\n\n    H_max=max_i h_i,\n    kappa_chr=min(1,(min_i h_i-1)/4)>0,\n    q_decay=kappa_chr/[8(H_max+kappa_chr+1)],\n    Gamma_i=q_decay^i, f_i=(kappa_chr+1/2)Gamma_i^2.\n\nIn this example set g_i=(Gamma_i*sqrt(w_s)*chi_s(i))_s for ALL 0<=i<=n.\nAll N characters are present and have positive weight, so the character\nmatrix is invertible. Indeed\nsum_s chi_s(i)chi_s(j)=N for i=j and 0 otherwise, by pairing the sign\nof a differing bit. Hence the Gram K_ij=<g_i,g_j> is positive definite.\nDefine\n\n    b_i=(kappa_chr+1)Gamma_i^2+sum_(t<i)h_t K_ti,\n    X_0=sum_i (K^(-1)b)_i g_i,\n    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t.\n\nThese finite formulas fix one anchor before GD is run. They give\n<X_i,g_i>=(kappa_chr+1)Gamma_i^2. For i<j direct expansion gives\n\n    Q_ij=(kappa_chr+1-h_i)Gamma_i^2-kappa_chr Gamma_j^2\n                          -sum_(i<t<j)h_t K_it-K_ij,\n    Q_ji=(kappa_chr+1)Gamma_j^2-kappa_chr Gamma_i^2\n                          +(h_i-1)K_ij+sum_(i<t<j)h_t K_jt.\n\nUsing |K_it|<=Gamma_i Gamma_t, q_decay<1/2 and h_i-1>=4kappa_chr,\nthe first residual divided by Gamma_i^2 is at most\n\n    -3kappa_chr+H_max*q_decay/(1-q_decay)+q_decay\n       <= -3kappa_chr+2(H_max+1)q_decay\n       <= -11kappa_chr/4 < 0.\n\nThe second is at most\n\n    -kappa_chr+(kappa_chr+1)q_decay^2\n                             +H_max*q_decay/(1-q_decay)\n       <= -kappa_chr+(2H_max+kappa_chr+1)q_decay\n       <= -3kappa_chr/4 < 0.\n\nFor the optimum jet (0,0,0), Q_(i,star)=0 and\nQ_(star,i)=-kappa_chr Gamma_i^2<0. Thus ALL original ordered pairs,\nincluding the optimum and terminal, have been checked.\n\nFor an explicit one-function realization, let\n\n    C_poly=conv(0,g_0,...,g_n),\n    F_poly(X)=max_(v in C_poly) [<X,v>-||v||^2/2].\n\nThe unique maximizer is the nearest-point projection P_(C_poly)(X).\nIts normal condition is <X-P_(C_poly)(X),v-P_(C_poly)(X)><=0 for every\nv in C_poly. Adding this condition at X and Y proves that the projection\nis 1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing expressions bounds the\nfirst-order remainder for F_poly between 0 and ||X-Y||^2, so F_poly is\ndifferentiable with gradient P_(C_poly), convex, finite, and 1-smooth.\n\nAt each prescribed sample the displayed residuals say\n<X_i-g_i,g_j-g_i>=Q_ji<=0, and\n<X_i-g_i,-g_i>=-kappa_chr Gamma_i^2<0. Thus the normal condition holds\non every vertex and their convex hull: grad F_poly(X_i)=g_i and\nF_poly(X_i)=f_i. The fixed function therefore follows the stated GD.\nIt is nonnegative and minimized at zero. Its full minimizer set is\n{Z:<Z,g_i><=0 for all i}, by the same normal condition at the zero vertex.\nThe true distance to this polar cone is positive and is the R used in\n(3)--(7); no claim equates it with ||X_0||.\n\n## 9. Scope and unresolved changes\n\nThe exclusion covers EVERY positive QUERIED amplitude choice for (1),(2),\nincluding unbounded, history-dependent, nonsaturating, and nonmatching choices, all\nanchors and values that actually interpolate, every terminal completion,\nand every m>=1024. It keeps\nthe B=-1 mass exactly in the return argument and pays only the product\nmixture eta=N^-2 there. The separate initial mass proof pays the full\nepsilon/2+eta remainder before bounding ANY amplitudes.\n\nThe same derivation also covers the exact eta=0 boundary, and epsilon=0\nwhen considered with the same physical word: zero perturbation yields\nnonincreasing returns directly. It does not assume positive definiteness\nfor the upper bound. Positive definiteness is used only for the separate\nfull-support nonvacuity example.\n\nDifferent measures and different binary labels remain outside. In\nparticular the coercive margins 1-2*(7/10)^2 and 1-k*z^2 are essential;\na geometry at or beyond absolute ray correlation 1/sqrt(2) is not covered\nby this proof. Nor does a larger arbitrary full-support component come\nwith a vanishing exact-B_n comparison: the estimate retains its eta term.\nThe terminal gradient is completely free. All realizing functions and\nthe maximal prefix interpolant from accepted 1252b8823bb5a290 are covered.\nNo claim is made about arbitrary schedules or the accepted unrestricted\nfinite H6 witness.\n\nMatlas and web searches for long-step cone/length bounds and gradient-norm\nmonotonicity did not supply an applicable external theorem. The returned\nshort-step theorems cannot be applied to this whole word. Every short-step\nfact actually used is derived above from accepted interpolation. This\nremains the already audited finite interpolation regime; no additional\nunread body of external theory is a premise.\n\nThe result is a full scoped PREFIX OBSTRUCTION CANDIDATE, not an accepted\nfact. {"id": "ff8f8f21b222a81b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T01:49:56.975239+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max2: complete draft uniform TIME localization and stable eighteen-node inverse for the remaining thirteen-band problem. Every early-prefix contribution is <10^-10 once six birth levels away; for required rows24<=i<=m-1 only nodes i-11..i+6 remain after exact extinction, with inverse-column l1<6. This resolves the low-node inverse-instability obstacle but NOT the signed late K/T estimates. Also derive a sharp original-order clock envelope excluding the known swap foil, and retain a decimation multiplier that lies on both sides of1 at actual nodes.", "evidence": "# Positive off-diagonal entries and exact chronological decimation\n\nStatus: **unverified proof draft**, sixth launch, first continuation round.\nNo kernel evaluation, optimizer, installation, or fact submission was used.\nThe uniform positive-semidefinite conclusion and the original minimax Goal\nremain open. This document proves some of the actual coefficient claims;\nit does not assume the remaining thirteen-band estimates.\n\n## 1. Definitions and the coefficient that is being estimated\n\nFix an integer m>=128 and keep epsilon=1/m fixed throughout. Set\n\n lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,\n d_k=1+a*c^k  (k>=0), H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j).\n\nThe extension of d_k past m-1 is only a coefficient device. The actual\nqueried word is H_m with its last d_0 removed. Write h_i for its steps,\ns_i=sum_(r<i)h_r, and s'=s_i+h_i when discussing a single step.\n\nOn the infinite original ruler word define B_00=1 and\n\n B_(i+1),k=(1-h_i/d_k)B_ik+(h_i/d_(k-1))B_i,(k-1),\n\nwith a zero second term at k=0. For each occupied state k, h_i<=d_k:\nthe first occurrence of d_j kills state j, and all smaller states have\nalready died. This also proves B_ik>=0, sum_k B_ik=1, and\nB_ik=0 for i>=2^k or k>i.\n\nFor arbitrary formal vectors y_k put g_i=sum_k v_ik*y_k, where\nv_ik=B_ik/d_k. The row-finite symmetric matrix E is defined by\n\n sum_(k,l)E_kl<y_k,y_l>\n =sum_i [2s_(i+1)h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>\n       +s_(i+1)||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2].   (1)\n\nEach coefficient is a finite sum: a term containing state k occurs by\nthe step immediately before query2^k. In particular E_kl=0 for k<l\nand l>2^k. The m-principal block is the actual finite residual kernel.\nThe coefficient convention in (1) means that an off-diagonal entry\nappears twice in the quadratic form.\n\n## 2. Two elementary bounds for the actual chronological word\n\nFor the elementary parameter bounds used below, lambda>12/5 and\nlambda<29/12 give 12/5<c<39/16. The upper comparison follows from\n(29/12)(129/128)=3741/1536<39/16. Consequently\na>(127/128)/(39/16)=127/312>2/5. Moreover\n\n d_0=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],\n d_2=2+sqrt(2)-lambda*epsilon^2,\n\nso 7/5<d_0<3/2 and d_2<7/2; for the first lower bound use\nsqrt(2)>141/100 and 2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]<5/768.\nIn particular 2<c<3 and 1<d_0<2. Also\n\n                         d_(k+1)>2d_k  (k>=2).            (2)\n\nIndeed d_(k+1)-2d_k=(c-2)a*c^k-1. At k=2 the quantity\nbefore subtracting1 is (1-epsilon)c(c-2), and\n\n c(c-2)=1+2lambda(lambda-1)epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2\n        >1+6epsilon.\n\nConsequently (1-epsilon)c(c-2)>1+5epsilon-6epsilon^2>1.\nThe expression increases with k, proving (2).\n\nSecond, every original physical step satisfies\n\n                             h_i<s_i+2.                  (3)\n\nLet S_j=sum H_j and C_j=S_j+2-d_j. Then C_0=2-d_0>0.\nThe recurrences S_(j+1)=2S_j+d_j and\nd_(j+1)=c*d_j-(c-1) give the exact identity\n\n C_(j+1)=2C_j+(3-c)(d_j-1)>0.\n\nThe prefix before the FIRST d_j is H_j, so (3) holds there. Every later\noccurrence has a larger elapsed clock and the same step size. This proves\n(3) at every position, without changing the enclosing epsilon.\n\nThere is a sharper envelope that does exclude the recorded swap foil.\nPut rho=c-2 and gamma=rho-a=c-1-d_0>0. The exact word-sum formula gives\n\n rho(S_j+1)+1-d_j=gamma*2^j.\n\nThus every original occurrence h_i=d_j obeys\n\n rho(s_i+1)+1-h_i=gamma*2^j+rho(s_i-S_j)>=0.              (3a)\n\nIf the third and fourth steps are exchanged, the first d_2 has\ns_i=d_0+d_1 instead of S_2=2d_0+d_1. Its slack in (3a) becomes\n4gamma-rho*d_0<13/128-14/25<0. Here rho>2/5, d_0>7/5, and\ngamma<(13/4)epsilon, as proved explicitly in Section7 below. This\ndistinguishes the original chronology from that particular foil.\nEquation (3a) alone is not asserted to prove b_i or u_i.\n\n## 3. Nonnegative off-diagonal entries, term by term\n\nFor a fixed step h=h_i, write v_k=v_ik and v_(-1)=0. The scaled birth\nrecurrence is\n\n v'_k=v_k+(h/d_k)(v_(k-1)-v_k).\n\nFor k<l, expansion of that step's contribution to E_kl gives exactly\n\n C_kl*v_k*v_l\n +[s'h^2(d_l-1)/(d_k*d_l)]v_(k-1)*v_l\n +[s'h^2(d_k-1)/(d_k*d_l)]v_k*v_(l-1)\n +[s'h^2/(d_k*d_l)]v_(k-1)*v_(l-1),                     (4)\n\nwhere\n\n C_kl=h[2s'+1-h-s'h(1/d_k+1/d_l-1/(d_k*d_l))].           (5)\n\nTo check (4), the first term of (1) contributes\ns'h(v'_k*v_l+v_k*v'_l), the second contributes\ns'(v'_k-v_k)(v'_l-v_l), and the third contributes\nh(1-h)v_k*v_l. Expanding these three expressions gives (4)-(5).\n\nThe last three coefficients in (4) are nonnegative. Suppose k>=2.\nIf v_k*v_l=0, the possibly signed coefficient C_kl costs nothing.\nOtherwise state k is occupied, so h<=d_k. Equation (2) gives\nd_l>=d_(k+1)>2d_k and hence\n\n h(1/d_k+1/d_l-1/(d_k*d_l))<3/2.\n\nIt follows from (3) that\n\n C_kl>h(s'/2+1-h)=(h/2)(s_i+2-h)>0.                     (6)\n\nThus every physical step contributes a nonnegative amount to E_kl\nwhen 2<=k<l. This proof keeps the signed terms of the original step\nuntil they combine into C_kl; it does not take their absolute values.\n\nFor k=0,1, support leaves only E_01 and E_12. The first two steps,\nwhose unscaled state vectors are successively V_0,V_1,V_2, give\n\n E_01=(d_0-1)/d_1>0,\n E_12=(d_0+d_1)(d_1-1)/(d_1*d_2)>0.\n\nThese calculations and (4)-(6) prove\n\n                    E_kl>=0 for every k!=l.              (7)\n\nIn particular the actual second-neighbor weights obey e_0=e_1=0 and\n\n                    e_k=E_(k,k+2)>0 for k>=2.             (8)\n\nFor strictness, just before the first d_k both B_ik and B_i,(k+1)\nare positive when k>=2. For B_ik, use the path that takes a birth on\neach of its first k steps and then stays. All those birth probabilities\nare positive, and every subsequent step before the first d_k is smaller\nthan d_k. For B_i,(k+1), take the first k+1 births and then stay; this\nis possible because k+1<=2^k-1, and all later pre-d_k steps are smaller\nthan d_(k+1). At the d_k step, the third term in (4) with l=k+2 is\nstrictly positive: s'>0, d_k>1, v_k>0, v_(k+1)>0. This proves (8).\n\nNo uniform lower bound such as e_k>=1/15 is proved by this strictness\nargument: the displayed positive path probabilities have not been\nestimated uniformly.\n\n## 4. Exact consequences for the unresolved row margins\n\nFor all nonnegative indices define\n\n A_i=E_(i,i+1), e_i=E_(i,i+2),\n b_i=A_i-2(e_(i-1)+e_i),\n u_i=E_ii-A_(i-1)-A_i+e_(i-2)+e_i\n                         -sum_(|l-i|>=3)|E_il|,\n\nwith negative subscripts equal to zero. Let F_i=sum_l E_il be the\nfinite unweighted row sum, and ellhat_i=sum_l E_il*d_l the constant\nload obtained by polarizing (1) against the constant gradient.\n\nEquation (7) removes all absolute values from the far off-diagonal\nsum. Therefore the exact full-row identity is\n\n                     u_i=2E_ii+2(e_(i-2)+e_i)-F_i.         (9)\n\nIn particular a proof of u_i does not need to identify alternating\nsigns of individual far entries. It still needs signed cancellation\nbetween the diagonal, the neighboring entries, and the row sum.\nNonnegative entries do not imply either b_i>=2/15 or u_i>=0.\n\nThis does not discard a terminal coefficient. In the inherited finite\ndecomposition, the triple starting at m-2 remains the square\ne_(m-2)||y_(m-2)+2y_(m-1)||^2. Its coefficient now has the sign in (8).\nThe last diagonal remains\n\n u_(m-1)+sum_(l>=m+2)E_(m-1,l)+b_(m-1)+e_(m-1).\n\nNeither its required quantitative lower bound nor the completed\nconstant-column remainder follows from (7) alone.\n\n## 5. A scalar constant-load formula with the signed difference retained\n\nFor this section all sums run over the infinite coefficient word, but\nevery state-specific sum is finite. Set\n\n mu_ik=h_i B_ik/d_k,\n L_k=sum_(r=0)^k d_r,\n M_k=sum_i h_i*mu_ik,\n N_k=sum_i s_(i+1)*h_i*mu_ik,\n\nand put M_(-1)=N_(-1)=0. The flow equation is\nB_(i+1),k-B_ik=mu_i,(k-1)-mu_ik. Summing it proves\nsum_i mu_ik=1 for each transient state. Weighting it by s_(i+1)\nand summing by parts gives\n\n sum_i s_(i+1)(mu_ik-mu_i,(k-1))=sum_i h_i B_ik=d_k.\n\nStarting with a zero predecessor moment shows\nsum_i s_(i+1)mu_ik=L_k. Thus M_k and N_k are respectively the mean\nexit step and the mean product of exit clock and exit step for state k.\n\nPolarizing (1) against the constant gradient gives\n\n ellhat_k=sum_i h_i[(s_i+1)v_ik+s_(i+1)v_(i+1),k].         (10)\n\nFor example, the first term in (1) contributes\ns'h(v_ik+v_(i+1),k), its increment-square term contributes zero,\nand its last term contributes h(1-h)v_ik. Their sum is (10).\nSubstitute the birth recurrence into (10), then use the two occupation\nidentities just proved. The result is\n\n             ellhat_k=2L_k+1-M_k-(N_k-N_(k-1))/d_k.        (11)\n\nIn detail, before the recurrence contribution the sum is\n2L_k+1-M_k. The remaining term is\nsum_i s'h_i^2(v_i,(k-1)-v_ik)/d_k\n=(N_(k-1)-N_k)/d_k. This checks its sign and its denominator.\n\nFormula (11) is an identity, not a new condition replacing the load\ninequalities. It identifies the chronological mixed moment that must\nbe estimated. Bounding N_k and N_(k-1) separately loses the signed\nquantity needed for the desired 2d_k<=ellhat_k<=11d_k/4.\n\n## 6. An exact reduction that distinguishes the original lower-level order\n\nPut t=d_0, ell=c-1, gamma=ell-t, and\n\n Z(z)=c*z/(1+ell*z),\n A(z)=(1-t*z)(1+ell*z)=1+gamma*z-t*ell*z^2.\n\nThese are new scalar functions, unrelated to the adjacent entries A_i.\nThey are well-defined at every nonnegative reciprocal physical node.\nThe affine-geometric node relation gives\n\n (1-t*z)(1-d_(j+1)*z)=A(z)(1-d_j*Z(z)),\n Z(1/d_(j+1))=1/d_j.                                    (12)\n\nWrite the original infinite step word in consecutive pairs. The exact\npair at position r is (t,d_(nu_2(r+1)+1)), where nu_2 is the exponent\nof2 in a positive integer. After removing every t and lowering the\nremaining level by1, the compressed word is the same original ruler\nword, with the SAME epsilon. For its first r steps write\nP_r(z)=product_(i<r)(1-h_i*z) and s_r=sum_(i<r)h_i. Then\n\n P_(2r)(z)=A(z)^r P_r(Z(z)),\n P_(2r+1)(z)=(1-t*z)A(z)^r P_r(Z(z)),\n s_(2r)=c*s_r-gamma*r,\n s_(2r+1)=c*s_r-gamma*r+t.                               (13)\n\nAll four identities follow by multiplying or summing the individual\npairs in (12). This is a chronological identity, not an identity of\nthe full multiset alone. The foil that exchanges the third and fourth\nsteps destroys the displayed consecutive-pair description.\n\nThe Newton factors also transform exactly:\n\n phi_(k+1)(z)=(1-t*z)(1+ell*z)^k phi_k(Z(z)),              (14)\n\nwhere phi_k(z)=product_(j<k)(1-d_j*z). At epsilon=0, interpreted only\nas a coefficient limit, t=ell=sqrt(2), gamma=0, and A(z)=1-2z^2.\nAt every actual epsilon>0 the linear term gamma*z and the clock defect\n-gamma*r in (13) must both be retained. In fact\n\n gamma=lambda*epsilon+2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]>0.\n\nEquations (12)-(14) do not yet establish a bound for E or the mixed\nmoments in (11). They give an exact original-order transformation in\nwhich those estimates can be pursued without replacing an embedded\nblock's parameters by epsilon=1/j.\n\n## 7. The decimation multiplier changes sign around one\n\nIt is false that the multiplier A at all required physical nodes can\nbe discarded by a uniform inequality A<=1, or by a uniform inequality\nA>=1. Both failures occur at every actual m>=128, without using a\nstandalone-depth parameter in an embedded block.\n\nFor z=1/d_j,\n\n             A(1/d_j)-1=(gamma*d_j-t*ell)/d_j^2.           (15)\n\nWe have 7/5<t<3/2, t<ell<3/2, and\n\n 2epsilon<gamma\n   <(lambda+2/lambda)epsilon\n   =(3sqrt(2)-1)epsilon<(13/4)epsilon.                    (16)\n\nAt j=2, d_2<7/2, so\ngamma*d_2<91/1024<49/25<t*ell. Therefore A(1/d_2)<1.\nAt the actual highest level j=m-1,\n\n gamma*d_(m-1)>(4/5)2^(m-1)/m>9/4>t*ell.\n\nHere a>2/5 and c>2 give the first bound. For the second, 2^(m-1)/m\nis increasing for m>=2, and even 2^10/128=8 already exceeds45/16.\nThis proves A(1/d_(m-1))>1. Both indices are in the original residual\ncoordinate set. These are counterexamples only to discarding the\ndecimation factor in one direction, not to the desired band bounds.\n\nThere is nevertheless a useful uniform estimate. For k>=8 define\n\n X_k=(65/16)epsilon*(2/c)^k+(225/32)*(2/c^2)^k.\n\nThen, for every 0<=z<=1/d_k and every integer 0<=r<=2^(k-1),\n\n 0<A(z),\n r|A(z)-1|<=X_k<1/80,\n |A(z)^r-1|<=(80/79)X_k<1/79,\n 0<=gamma*r/d_k<=(65/16)epsilon*(2/c)^k.                 (17)\n\nFor r=0 all expressions with r vanish. For positive r, (16),\nt*ell<9/4, a>2/5, and d_k>a*c^k give\n\n r|A(z)-1|\n <=2^(k-1)[gamma/d_k+t*ell/d_k^2]\n <(65/16)epsilon*(2/c)^k+(225/32)*(2/c^2)^k.\n\nAlso c>12/5, so 2/c<5/6 and 2/c^2<3/8. For k>=8, the exact rational\ncomparisons (5/6)^8<1/4 and (3/8)^8<1/2500 show\n\n X_k<65/8192+9/3200<1/80.\n\nPositivity follows from z<=1/d_k<1/t. With delta=|A(z)-1|, if A(z)>=1\nthe binomial expansion gives\n\n A(z)^r-1<=sum_(j>=1)(r*delta)^j<=X_k/(1-X_k).\n\nIf A(z)<1, 1-A(z)^r<=r*delta<=X_k. These prove the third estimate in\n(17); the last follows from the linear part of the same calculation.\n\nThus the multiplier and clock defects are uniformly small far up the\nactual spectrum, despite the change of sign in (15). Their precise\nrates include BOTH epsilon*(2/c)^k and (2/c^2)^k. This does not yet\ncontrol E: converting pointwise spectral estimates to a birth coefficient\nrequires retaining the signed divided differences, not taking a large\nabsolute bound on the entire inverse Newton matrix.\n\n## 8. An early-time bound for the entire nearby band\n\nThis localization is in PHYSICAL TIME, in addition to the earlier\nlocalization in matrix-column distance. It controls the part of a near\nentry accumulated before the low reciprocal columns die.\n\nFix j>=2 and let E^early_kl be the contribution to (1) of the first\n2^j steps, namely the prefix (H_j,d_j). If k,l>=j+6, then\n\n                         |E^early_kl|<10^(-10).           (18)\n\nHere is a uniform proof. Write T=sum(H_j,d_j). The exact word sum is\n\n sum H_j=a*c^j/(c-2)+(1-a/(c-2))*2^j-1.\n\nBecause 0<a/(c-2)<1 and 2<c, this sum is less than c^j-1.\nConsequently T<(1+1/a)d_j<(7/2)d_j. All steps in the prefix are at\nmost d_j, and d_j>3.\n\nFor an integer r>=0 define\n\n p_r=min{1,(7/2)^r/[r!*2^(r(r-1)/2)]}.\n\nAt each query of this prefix, the probability of state j+r is at most\np_r. To prove this, condition on a birth path's first arrival at j.\nReaching j+r requires r subsequent births on increasing distinct\nsteps. Each specified set has probability at most the product of its\nsteps divided by d_j...d_(j+r-1); all stay factors are at most one.\nThe sum of those step products is at most T^r/r!, and (2) bounds the\ndenominator below by d_j^r*2^(r(r-1)/2). This proof applies at either\nend of every step and for every arrival time.\n\nThe sequence p_r decreases for r>=3. Thus, for k>=j+6,\n\n 0<=v_ik,v_(i+1),k<=p_6/d_k,\n |v_(i+1),k-v_ik|<=h_i[(5/2)p_5+p_6]/d_k^2.\n\nFor the second line, apply the birth recurrence and use\nd_k/d_(k-1)<c<5/2. Define Q_6=(5/2)p_5+p_6. The exact fractions give\n\n p_5=16807/3932160<1/230,\n p_6=117649/1509949440<1/12500,\n Q_6<1/92+1/12500<11/1000.\n\nThe first and last terms of (1), polarized for a general k,l, are at\nmost [T^2+2d_j*T]p_6^2/(d_k*d_l) in absolute value. This uses\n2sum_i s_(i+1)h_i=T^2+sum_i h_i^2 and\nsum_i h_i^2<=d_j*T. The increment-square term is at most\nT^2*d_j*Q_6^2/(d_k^2*d_l^2), since\nsum_i s_(i+1)h_i^2<=T^2*d_j. These statements also apply when k=l.\n\nNow d_k,d_l>2^6*d_j. Only eighteen reciprocal nodes remain in the late contribution\n\nFor this section fix an ACTUAL required row 24<=i<=m-1, put j=i-12,\nand consider l=i+r with -6<=r<=6. Extended indices l>=m are only the\nsame-epsilon coefficient device, as in Section1. All contributing\nphysical steps are still through the first d_i and are in the original\nqueried word.\n\nDefine R_kb=(d_k/d_b)phi_k(1/d_b) for b>=k, and zero for b<k.\nIts triangular inverse D has entries\n\n D_bk=product_(0<=r<=k,r!=b)(1-d_r/d_b)^(-1),  0<=b<=k,\n D_bk=0 for b>k.                                         (19)\n\nOne direct check uses Lagrange interpolation at the distinct nodes\n1/d_0,...,1/d_k. Comparing highest-degree coefficients shows that the\nweights reciprocal to the products of node differences annihilate\npolynomials of degree below k and recover the leading coefficient\nat degree k. The resulting\ninverse of R is exactly (19).\n\nWriting P_n(z)=product_(t<n)(1-h_t*z), the corresponding scalar\ncoefficient identity is\n\n                  v_nk=sum_(b=0)^k D_bk*P_n(1/d_b)/d_b.  (20)\n\nIndeed P_n(z)=sum_k B_nk phi_k(z), by the birth recurrence. Evaluation\nat the first k+1 reciprocal nodes kills every phi_r with r>k, and\ninversion by (19) gives (20).\n\nFor every query n>=2^j and every b<=j, P_n(1/d_b)=0 exactly; the same\nis true at the following query. Thus all such nodes disappear from\nthe late part of (1). Define M^late_ab to be the quadratic coefficient\nof the normalized modal columns P_n(1/d_a)/d_a and P_n(1/d_b)/d_b,\nusing only physical steps 2^j<=n<2^i, with their ORIGINAL elapsed\nclocks s_n. Then (20) gives the exact identity\n\n E_il=E^early_il\n          +sum_(a=j+1)^i sum_(b=j+1)^l D_ai*M^late_ab*D_bl. (21)\n\nFor l<i every term vanishes already by query2^l; for l>=i the first\ncolumn vanishes by query2^i. Consequently the displayed final cutoff\nis correct in both cases and adds no physical step beyond the original\ntrajectory. Only the eighteen nodes i-11,...,i+6 can occur in (21).\n\nCrucially, the remaining inverse columns have a uniform absolute sum.\nFor each k in [i-6,i+6],\n\n                         sum_(b=j+1)^k |D_bk|<6.          (22)\n\nTo prove this, put q_c=1/c and P^q_s=product_(r=1)^s(1-q_c^r),\nwith P^q_0=1. Factoring each difference in (19) gives\n\n |D_bk|=q_c^((k-b)(k-b+1)/2)\n             *(d_b/(d_b-1))^k/(P^q_b P^q_(k-b)).\n\nSince q_c<5/12, the elementary inequality\nproduct_r(1-x_r)>=1-sum_r x_r for 0<=x_r<=1 gives\nP^q_s>=1-q_c-q_c^2>59/144>2/5 for s>=1.\nAlso P^q_1>7/12. For b>=i-11 and k<=i+6,\n\n k/(a*c^b)<(5/2)(i+6)/2^(i-11)<=75/8192<1/100.\n\nThe middle expression decreases with i>=24. Hence\n(d_b/(d_b-1))^k<=exp(k/(a*c^b))<exp(1/100).\nSumming with r=k-b and then extending the positive sum to all r>=0,\n\n sum_b |D_bk|\n <(5/2)exp(1/100)\n       *[1+5/7+(5/2)*sum_(r>=2)(5/12)^(r(r+1)/2)]\n <=(5/2)exp(1/100)[1+5/7+625/3206]\n <5 exp(1/100)<500/99<6.\n\nFor the second line, ratios of successive terms starting at r=2\nare at most(5/12)^3, so their sum is at most125/1603. For the last\nline, exp(x)<=1/(1-x) for 0<=x<1 follows by comparing its power\nseries with the geometric series. This proves (22).\n\nFor example, if an analytic approximation changes every normalized\nlate modal coefficient in this eighteen-node window by at most eta,\nthen (21)-(22) change each reconstructed near entry by at most36eta.\nDiscarding the early part costs an additional10^(-10), by (18).\nThis is a genuine uniform coefficient-stability estimate; no small\nfixed-index inverse coefficient is included in its norm.\n\nThe pointwise decimation bound (17) is only needed while a reciprocal\ncolumn is alive. Multiplying that bound by an unnecessarily longer\nhorizon would lose the extinction information.\n\nThis leaves a specific analytic step: control the signed late K/T\ncoefficients under (12)-(14), with the original clock, and bound the\nresulting finite window uniformly. Neither (21) nor (22) asserts those\nremaining estimates or the signs of b_i and u_i.\n\n## 10. Scope and remaining work\n\nThe new proved-in-draft part of the stated interface is e_i>=0 for\nevery required index. The stronger entrywise result (7), the identities\n(9), (11), and (13), the uniform decimation estimates (17), and the\ntime localization and stable finite inverse (18)-(22) have explicit\nproofs above.\n\nThe unresolved claims remain b_i>=2/15, u_i>=0, the quantitative\ne_i>=1/15 and u_i>=1/16 at the required terminal indices, and the\ntwo-sided constant-load bounds.
