There is no new seed, quantitative improving phase\ncertificate, optimizer call, phase scan, installation or verifier call.\n\nThe accepted background used here is fact 0be8b54e0a33381c: its exact\nbenchmark recurrences, the actual Huber lower bound for every nonnegative\nword, and the square-root contraction of the dominant join. Its full proof\nwas read. All new certificate algebra is reproduced here; C72 is not used\nas a verified predecessor. The explicitly identified external scalar phase\ntheorems below are matched literature inputs to this joint candidate, not\nnew facts in the project graph.\n\n## 1. Source matching, indexing, and objective normalization\n\nUse the exact S_circle and S_bullet recurrences of fact 0be8b54e0a33381c.\nWrite\n\n    P_n=1+S_circle(n),  B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1,  U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2),  rho=log_2(lambda).\n\nThus P_n is also the C_n of the accepted project glossary; it is not the\nprefix sum P_j used in the single-cut witness. Define the homogeneous joins\n\n    Omega(a,c)=(a+c+sqrt((a+c)^2+4ac))/2,\n    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.\n\nDirect substitution of the accepted phi and psi gives\n\n    P_0=B_0=1,\n    P_n=max_(k+l+1=n) Omega(P_k,P_l),\n    B_n=max_(k+l+1=n) G(P_k,B_l).                         (1)\n\nFor a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) denotes the\nsupremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite\nEuclidean dimension, every convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz\ngradient, every minimizer x_star and x_0!=x_star, under\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Smoothness L>0 is fixed.\n\nThe primary source is Yu Liu, Kang Chen, Rujun Jiang and Tianyu Wang,\n*Optimal Recursive Composition and Dyadic Phase Laws for Gradient Descent\nwith Predetermined Stepsizes*, arXiv:2609.11788v1 (2026),\nhttps://arxiv.org/html/2609.11788v1 . Its HTML/PDF are in downloads, and\nthe extracted text was read. Two Matlas queries returned unrelated papers;\nthis source is web-grounded, NOT represented as a Matlas hit. The requested\nlocal SOURCE_PHASE_AMPLIFICATION_20260927.md was absent.\n\nPut hats on the source's scalar quantities to avoid its overloaded W:\n\n    source N = our n+1,\n    source K = Omega, source A(u,w) = G(u,w),\n    source rho = our lambda, source p = our rho,\n    source Uhat_N = P_(N-1), source What_N = B_(N-1).       (2)\n\nHere is an induction, rather than a transfer of certificate classes.\nProposition 2.5 has Uhat_1=1 and\nUhat_N=max_(1<=a<N) Omega(Uhat_a,Uhat_(N-a)). With k=a-1 and l=N-a-1,\nthis is the first recursion in (1). Source equations (34)-(37) have\neta^F_1=1, What_N=1/eta^F_N, and\nWhat_N=max_(1<=a<N) G(Uhat_a,What_(N-a)). The same k,l substitution gives\nthe second recursion in (1), including its initial value. This proves (2).\n\nSource equation (35) bounds the gap by eta^F_N*(L/2)||x_0-x_star||^2.\nConsequently eta^F_(n+1)=1/B_n is the project's normalized coefficient W\nfor those benchmark schedules. If R denotes gap/(L||x_0-x_star||^2),\nour W=2R and source eta=2R. The source What_N is a reciprocal denominator,\nnot our worst-case functional W_n(h). No proximal or gradient-norm metric\nis imported. The arbitrary primitive seed below is never asserted to be\ns-composable.\n\nThe minimal external input for the main limit calculation is Theorem 6.3:\nthere is a positive Lipschitz 1-periodic Psi_F such that, with\n\n    C^F_N=N^rho/B_(N-1),\n    eps_N=C^F_N-Psi_F(log_2 N),\n\nthe supremum of |eps_N| over 2^j<=N<=2^(j+1) tends to zero. Theorem 6.5\nadds nonconstancy. For the separate baseline-gap observation in section 7\nwe also use Theorem 5.5 and the enclosure in Theorem 6.5:\n\n    P_(N-1)=N^rho Phi(log_2 N),  phi_min=min Phi in (0,1),\n    max Phi=Phi(0)=1,\n    c_star<min Psi_F<max Psi_F<=c_star/phi_min.             (3)\n\nFor section 9, source Lemma 6.4 also supplies the support inequality\nG(a,d)^q_pow<=K_sup*a^q_pow+d^q_pow for a,d>=0, where q_pow=1/rho,\nK_sup=max_(0<=x<=1) 2^q_pow*(1-x^(2q_pow))/(1-x)^q_pow,\nthe continuous endpoint values are 2^q_pow and 0, and c_star=K_sup^(-rho).\nFor all other sections, only positivity of c_star and (3) are used.\nAll profiles are\nfunctions on R/Z, so arguments are understood modulo one. The proofs of\nTheorem 6.3, Appendix D.2, D.3-D.9, and the relevant symmetric-phase\nproofs were read. The uniformity follows there from adjacent-value control,\ncompactness and dense fixed-ray limits; the strict inequalities in (3)\ncome from support-deficit concentration. They do not provide an explicit\namplitude or an oriented comparison for the phases needed below.\n\n## 2. Full original-space seed condition\n\nNormalize L=1, translate a selected minimizer to zero, and subtract f_star\nso that its value is also zero. For a finite\nnonnegative word h of length r, let s=sum h_i, A=1+s, and retain every\nformal gradient g_0,...,g_r, including gradients at zero steps. Put\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)\n          +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_r||^2.\n\nEvery actual smooth convex trajectory has Q_ij<=0. We call the seed\nprimitive when V_h>=0 on every such trajectory in every finite dimension.\nA concrete sufficient certificate retains ALL ordered finite multipliers\nLambda_ij>=0, i!=j, and Lambda_ii=0. Let e be the all-ones column, and set\n\n    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,\n    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,\n    D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)).\n\nThe feasibility conditions are\n\n    L_flow^T e=omega,\n    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0.            (4)\n\nIndeed substituting the GD equations and collecting function coefficients\nand gradient inner products gives the exact identity\n\n    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.\n\nTo audit the matrix formula, the position terms of sum Lambda Q contribute\nsym(L_flow(T-I)); the squared-difference terms contribute\nsym(L_flow)-diag(omega)/2. Adding V_h's diagonal yields (4).\nThus (4) proves the required actual primitive inequality. It has no support,\nrank, symmetry, quotient, or terminal-edge restriction.\n\nAdding optimum multipliers cannot supply a missing positive x_0 diagonal:\nthat diagonal is zero. Its mixed coefficient with g_i is\n-Lambda_(i,star), so PSD forces each Lambda_(i,star)=0. Summing the finite\nfunction-flow equations then forces sum_i Lambda_(star,i)=0, and their\nnonnegativity makes all these multipliers zero. Therefore the original\nformal space (x_0,g_0,...,g_r) is retained, with its x_0 row zero.\nNo equality between formal gradients at duplicated query positions is\nimposed. For the implication used here, sufficiency of (4) is enough;\nno unproved dual attainment is invoked.\n\n## 3. Complete primitive and dominant composition identities\n\nPrimitive join: let a,b be primitive, with capacities A=1+sum a and\nC=1+sum b. Choose d>0 by d(A+C+d)=AC, put T_mass=A+C+d and\nkappa=(T_mass/C)^2, and use the physical word h=(a,1+d,b).\nLet p be its bridge query, z its terminal query, and j range over the\nnonterminal queries of b. Direct expansion gives\n\n    V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1) E_PP,\n    E_PP=sum_j b_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)+d Q_(p,z)\n                                      +sum_j b_j Q_(j,z).       (5)\n\nFor a coefficient audit, the value part of E_PP is\n2sum b_j f_j+(d-C)f_p+(2-C-d)f_z. Its gradient part is\n\n    sum_j b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2\n       +[(C-d)/2-d^2]||g_p||^2+(C+d)||g_z||^2/2.\n\nThe remaining coefficient identities are\n(kappa-1)(C-d)=2(A+d), kappa d^2=A^2, and kappa C^2=T_mass^2.\nThey follow from d T_mass=AC. Also 0<d<C and kappa>1, so all\nadded multipliers are nonnegative. If the child certificates have arbitrary\nPSD remainders, the full parent remainder is their original-coordinate sum\nM_a+kappa M_b, embedded on the actual child-gradient indices. The resulting\ncapacity is exactly Omega(A,C).\nThe physical bridge obeys 1+d=phi(A-1,C-1), as required by (1).\n\nDominant join: for a word h with terminal z, put D=2sum h+1. For\nnonnegative weights u_i summing to D define\n\n    J_(h,u)=||x_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2D f_z\n                                  -||x_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.    (6)\n\nThe empty word has u=1 and J=0. A primitive word is dominant with\nu=(h,s+1), since direct expansion gives J=V_h. In general J>=0 implies\n2f_z<=||x_0||^2/D on every actual trajectory.\n\nLet a be a primitive left child of capacity A and b a dominant right child\nwith mass D and weights v. Define\n\n    mu=2A/D,  zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2,\n    gamma=A+1,  beta=1+A/zeta.\n\nUse the physical parent word (a,beta,b), and weights u=(a,gamma,zeta v).\nTheir mass is G(A,D)=D zeta^2=2A+zeta D=2sum(parent)+1. With p the\nbridge query, all right-child queries i>p, including its terminal, give\n\n    J_parent=V_a+zeta^2 J_right\n                         -2mu sum_(i>p)v_i Q_(i,p).             (7)\n\nThe physical bridge is exactly beta=psi(A-1,(D-1)/2); multiply\nzeta^2-zeta=2A/D by D/zeta to check this against the radical in psi.\n\nThis is an equality on the full original formal space. Function\ncoefficients use mu D=2A. Expanding all squares and the GD bridge uses\n\n    (A-1)A+mu D+gamma=gamma^2,\n    zeta^2=zeta+mu,  zeta^2 beta=mu+gamma zeta;\n\nthese cancel the mixed terms and give (7). The child remainder is pulled\nback from the actual right-child starting position x_p-beta g_p; hence the\noriginal x_0 and all inherited gradients remain in the certificate.\nAll extra interpolation coefficients are nonnegative. Induction on the\nactual tree supplies a full PSD objective certificate: divide (6), after\ninserting its nonnegative interpolation representation, by D. Its PSD\nremainder is the retained final square plus the embedded child remainders,\nall divided by D. This is an all-function objective bound, not a bound on\na quadratic, projection or temporal subclass.\n\n## 4. The exact physical substitution and the untouched dominant leaf\n\nFix an ACTUAL seed a satisfying section 2, of length r=m-1 and capacity\nA=1+sum a>=1. For each n choose a fully expanded maximizing dominant tree\nfor B_n in (1), for example the smallest maximizing split at each node.\nThe unique dominant right spine ends at one dominant empty leaf. Every\nother leaf is primitive: there are exactly n of them, by induction on (1).\n\nReplace each primitive empty leaf by the actual word a, retaining every\noriginal node, and recompute each physical bridge by (5) or (7). Leave the\nlast dominant empty leaf empty, with mass 1. Call the resulting word H_n\nand its true mass D_n=2sum H_n+1. There are n old bridge steps and nr\ninserted seed steps. Thus\n\n    length(H_n)=mn,  W_(mn)(H_n)<=1/D_n.                       (8)\n\nEvery primitive branch capacity is multiplied by A, by homogeneity of\nOmega. If the last dominant leaf were ALSO changed from mass 1 to A in\nan auxiliary scalar evaluation, homogeneity of G would make the root\nA B_n. This last change is scalar only, not an additional physical block.\nThe square-root join is\n\n    sqrt(G(a,d))=(sqrt(d)+sqrt(d+8a))/2.\n\nFact 0be8b54e0a33381c proves that it is increasing and 1-Lipschitz in\nsqrt(d). Propagating the final-leaf difference along the whole spine gives\n\n    sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1)<=sqrt(D_n)<=sqrt(A B_n).            (9)\n\nWriting c_A=sqrt(A)-1 and ell_n=c_A/sqrt(A B_n), this implies\n\n    (1-ell_n)^2<=D_n/(A B_n)<=1,\n    0<=A B_n-D_n<=2c_A sqrt(A B_n)-c_A^2.                    (10)\n\nThe lower square-root bound is positive because B_n>=1. The accepted\nbound B_n>=2n+1 already makes ell_n tend to zero; the phase theorem gives\nthe sharper ell_n=O(n^(-rho/2)). The bound is independent of tree depth,\nsplit ties and multiplier choices.\n\nIn fact (8) is equality. The actual nonnegative word H_n has sum\n(D_n-1)/2. The one-dimensional Huber function in fact 0be8b54e0a33381c,\nstarted at x_0=1 with threshold 1/D_n and minimizer 0, attains normalized\nerror 1/D_n on that entire word. Its initial distance is genuinely 1.\nTogether with the full upper certificate this proves, within the joint\ncandidate,\n\n    W_(mn)(H_n)=1/D_n.                                      (11)\n\nRestoring any L>0 by multiplying the normalized objective by L leaves\nthe specified h_i/L GD updates and the ratio 2gap/(L distance^2) unchanged.\n\n## 5. Exact phase comparison, errors, and attaining sequences\n\nLet X=n+1 and Y=mn+1=mX-(m-1). Source matching and the definition of eps\ngive the exact scalar ratio\n\n    B_(mn)/B_n=(Y/X)^rho\n        [Psi_F(log_2 X)+eps_X]/[Psi_F(log_2 Y)+eps_Y].         (12)\n\nThus the normalized actual objective satisfies\n\n    B_(mn) W_(mn)(H_n)\n      =(1/A)(Y/X)^rho\n        [Psi_F(log_2 X)+eps_X]/[Psi_F(log_2 Y)+eps_Y]\n        *[A B_n/D_n].                                      (13)\n\nThe last factor lies in [1,(1-ell_n)^(-2)]. Formula (13) retains all\nthree errors: finite-index phase errors eps, Y=mX-(m-1) rather than mX,\nand the untouched terminal leaf. If L_Psi is a Lipschitz constant, then\n\n    dist(log_2 Y,log_2 X+log_2 m)\n        <=(m-1)/(Y log 2),\n\nusing -log(1-u)<=u/(1-u). Since min Psi_F>0, the denominator in (12)\nstays bounded away from zero for large n. Uniform eps convergence,\nthis phase displacement, and (10) therefore make (13) converge uniformly\nin its moving input phase to\n\n    R_m,A(t)=(m^rho/A) Psi_F(t)/Psi_F(t+log_2 m).              (14)\n\nMore explicitly, fix ANY t in [0,1) and set\n\n    X_j=floor(2^(j+t)),  n_j=X_j-1,  output horizon=m n_j.\n\nThen dist(log_2 X_j,t)<=1/(X_j log 2), and (13) tends to R_m,A(t).\nThis constructs the required inputs, including at a minimizer of (14).\nIt does not assume that a convenient dyadic ray happens to minimize it.\n\nDefine the now-identified scalar threshold\n\n    tau_m=m^rho min_(t in R/Z) Psi_F(t)/Psi_F(t+log_2 m).      (15)\n\nThe minimum is attained. Compactness gives the reverse inequality for\nany sequence n->infinity, and the displayed n_j attains it. Therefore\n\n    liminf_(n->infinity) B_(mn) W_(mn)(H_n)=tau_m/A.           (16)\n\nConsequently A>tau_m is NECESSARY AND SUFFICIENT for a fixed relative\nimprovement along some infinite subsequence of THIS actual substitution.\nFor sufficiency take delta=(1-tau_m/A)/2 and sufficiently large j in the\nconstructed minimizing-phase sequence. For necessity, a fixed improvement\nwould force the left side of (16) below 1. This is stronger than merely a\nsufficient comparison of certificates because of the Huber equality (11).\nIt does not characterize other possible uses of the same seed.\n\n## 6. What nonconstancy does and does not prove\n\nFor m=2^k, the phase shift is zero, so tau_m=m^rho. For every integer\nm>=2 that is not a power of two, log_2 m is irrational: if it equalled\na/b with integers a,b>0, m^b=2^a would force m to be a power of two.\nIf Psi_F(t)>=Psi_F(t+log_2 m) for all t, integrate the continuous\nnonnegative difference around the circle. Its integral is zero, so equality\nholds everywhere. Iterating the irrational rotation and using continuity\nwould then make Psi_F constant. This contradicts Theorem 6.5. Thus\n\n    tau_m<m^rho for every non-dyadic integer m.              (17)\n\nThis is a qualitative lowering, not the quantified threshold requested\nas TASK output (1). It supplies neither a rational interval inside the\ngap nor the sign of a comparison at a specified pair of rays.\n\nA concrete caution: fix a nonzero irrational shift alpha and any\n0<a_0<1. Let psi_e(t)=K(1+e cos(2pi(t-t_0))), with K>0 and\n\n    0<e<(1-a_0)/(1+a_0).\n\nIt is positive, Lipschitz, periodic and nonconstant, but every ratio\npsi_e(t)/psi_e(t+alpha)>a_0. Moreover at t=t_0 that ratio is strictly\ngreater than 1. Hence no prescribed quantitative lowering or specified\nimproving input phase follows from those qualitative properties alone.\nIf desired, this example also meets the numerical-free enclosure in (3):\ntake K=c_star*(1+1/phi_min)/2 and make e smaller than\nmin{1-c_star/K,c_star/(phi_min K)-1}. Those bounds are positive.\nThis is NOT a counterexample to the Bellman recurrence or to the real\nsource phase. It identifies exactly the missing Bellman information.\n\n## 7. Comparison with the primitive baseline\n\nThere is an unconditional lower comparison within this same scalar model:\n\n    tau_m>=P_(m-1).                                        (18)\n\nTo prove it, use a recursively generated maximizing primitive word of\nlength m-1 as the seed. Such a word is produced from empty leaves using\n(5), and has capacity P_(m-1) by (1). Its substituted word is itself an\nordinary recursive dominant word of length mn, so D_n<=B_(mn) by (1).\nEquation (9) with A=P_(m-1), followed by a sequence for any phase t, gives\nm^rho Psi_F(t)/Psi_F(t+log_2 m)>=P_(m-1). Minimize over t.\nThus a seed capacity strictly above the primitive baseline is necessary;\nstrictly above the power envelope is sufficient; their difference cannot\nbe silently discarded.\n\nOne can further prove the existence of nondyadic lengths with a strictly\npositive intervening gap, although these source inputs do not identify\nsuch a length quantitatively. Let\n\n    Gamma=(min Psi_F)/(max Psi_F).\n\nBy (3), Gamma>phi_min, and (15) gives tau_m>=m^rho Gamma. Choose t_star\nwith Phi(t_star)=phi_min. Integers\nm_j=floor(2^(j+t_star)) approach this phase. Since phi_min<1=Phi(0),\nthese integers are nondyadic for large j. Continuity gives, eventually,\n\n    P_(m_j-1)/m_j^rho=Phi(log_2 m_j)\n                  <(phi_min+Gamma)/2<Gamma,\n    tau_(m_j)-P_(m_j-1)\n                  >(Gamma-phi_min)m_j^rho/2>0.               (19)\n\nSo the threshold of THIS operation is not universally identical to the\nfinite primitive baseline. This does not construct an actual primitive\nseed in the intervening capacity interval, nor decide whether tau_3 or\ntau_5 strictly exceeds its baseline. Those are separate scalar questions.\n\n## 8. Concrete missing estimate and a fully specified next seed interface\n\nFor each fixed positive integer b define the exact ray value\n\n    Z_b=lim_(j->infinity) lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1)\n       =b^rho/Psi_F(log_2 b).                               (20)\n\nExistence and the equality follow directly from the matched phase law.\nConsider the explicit scalar estimate\n\n    E5:    Z_15 <= (773/100) Z_3.                            (21)\n\nIt has NOT been proved here and could fail on these specific rays. It is\na concrete missing comparison, rather than an unnamed phase infimum. If\nit holds, then ANY actual primitive seed of length 4 and capacity\nA>773/100, satisfying (4), gives fixed asymptotic improvement by (13) on\n\n    n_j=3*2^j-1,  output horizon=5n_j=15*2^j-5.\n\nIndeed the output leaf count is 15*2^j-4; the bounded offset 4 disappears\nby (12), and the exact limiting ratio is Z_15/(A Z_3). The last dominant\nleaf remains unchanged throughout.\n\nThe target constant is strictly between the exact finite baseline and\nthe former sufficient power threshold, with an explicit baseline gap:\n\n    P_4 < 77/10 < 773/100 < 5^rho,\n    773/100-P_4 > 3/100.                                   (22)\n\nHere is finite exact arithmetic establishing (22), independently of an\nasymptotic phase scan. Write q_3=Omega(1,lambda). Then\n\n    4<q_3<201/50,  P_3=lambda^2,\n    P_4=Omega(lambda,q_3).\n\nFor the upper bound on q_3, its defining polynomial at 201/50 is greater\nthan 181/8750>0 after replacing sqrt(2) by 99/70; the larger root is q_3.\nAt 4 its value is 7-5sqrt(2)<0. Also\nOmega(1,201/50)<29/5<lambda^2, proving P_3 from the two available splits.\nUsing lambda<169/70 and lambda^2<204/35 gives\n\n    Omega(1,lambda^2)<38/5,\n    Omega(lambda,4)>38/5,\n    Omega(lambda,201/50)<77/10.\n\nFor these three claims, the relevant quadratic evaluations at 38/5,\n38/5,77/10 are respectively >6/175, < -12/25, and >71/1750.\nThis proves both the maximizing P_4 split and P_4<77/10.\nFor the last power comparison, exact integer arithmetic gives\n\n    lambda^70=a_70+b_70 sqrt(2),\n    a_70=311363698964240484013304163,\n    b_70=220167382952941249990598278,\n    5a_70+7b_70-5*2^89=3140077278340482752898201>0,\n    5^89*100^70>773^70.\n\nSince sqrt(2)>7/5, these imply rho>89/70 and\n5^rho>5^(89/70)>773/100. They are a few fixed integer/rational checks,\nnot a parameter scan; their displayed identities are the proof premises.\n\nThe conditional next seed problem is exactly: find h_0,...,h_3>=0 with\ns=sum h_i>673/100, twenty nonnegative ordered Lambda_ij on i,j=0,...,4,\nand the full five-by-five PSD matrix (4), with omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_3,2s)\nand D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i),-s(s+2)). Retain the zero x_0 row, all five\nformal gradients and every incoming terminal multiplier. No product,\nsparsity, reflection, tensor or fixed-remainder restriction is added.\nThis is a concrete feasibility specification, not a claim of feasibility,\nan allocated search, or a consequence already obtained from E5.\n\nFor the smallest nondyadic scalar multiplier m=3, the analogous explicit\ntest is Z_9<=(101/25)Z_3. It would use the same input n_j and output\n9*2^j-3. Here P_2<201/50<101/25<3^rho, the last inequality following\nfrom rho>89/70 and 3^89*25^70>101^70. This is also unproved. Length four\nis specified above as the next seed interface beyond the previously\nstudied small-length obstructions; no unverified small-length obstruction\nis used as a premise of this proof.\n\n## 9. Direct attempts at (21), and the precise remaining obstacle\n\nFirst attempt: use the source's reciprocal sandwich. On fixed rays it\ngives 2P_(b-1)<=Z_b<=lambda P_(b-1). These separate upper/lower bounds\ndo not orient the two objective phases and retain the factor lambda/2>1.\nThe stronger enclosure (3) still gives only\nZ_15/Z_3<5^rho/phi_min, which exceeds the desired power threshold. It\ntherefore cannot prove (21).\n\nSecond attempt: use the homogeneous support barrier at the first pivot.\nDefine the symmetric continuum capacity\nP_cont(x)=x^rho Phi(log_2 x), and d_star=1/c_star. A prospective\none-pivot upper bound based only on the global continuation cap is\n\n    sup_(0<=a<=x) G(P_cont(a),d_star*(x-a)^rho),\n    P_cont(0)=0.\n\nIt equals d_star*x^rho: the support inequality bounds it above by this\nquantity, but a=0 attains it because G(0,d)=d. Thus this relaxation\ncannot detect any strict phase penalty. Deleting a=0 does not repair it,\nsince the same value is approached as a tends to zero. One must charge\nthe total cost of a string of small pivots before the first macroscopic\none, as the source's concentration proof does qualitatively, or retain a\nsharper phase-dependent continuation bound. A finite prefix of dyadic\nray values supplies lower approximations; without a certified remainder\nit supplies no upper enclosure for the numerator Z_15.\n\nThe cosine family in section 6 explicitly falsifies a proposed shortcut\nfrom nonconstancy/continuity/enclosure to a predetermined gap or favorable\nray. It does not reject a phase-refinement program that uses more of (1).\n\nThe remaining scalar task is (21), an alternative explicitly quantified\nphase pair with its own attaining integers, or a rigorous obstruction for\nthe proposed pair. Separately, no actual seed satisfying the resulting\nfull certificate and capacity requirement is known. The two original pair sums at(j,j+2),(j,j+4), with M=B-z C, yield [1-kz²]C²+z(k-2)BC+B²+M Delta+MlEp+lAC(p-zw)<=e0(C+B)+M e0(D+B)/Delta, where e0=4eta(R+T_path). Since A>=C/r_leaf, the strengthened coefficient follows. Put L=l/r_leaf,d=gamma epsilon/2. Its positive zero is beta_R=[k d+sqrt((k+L)(1+Lp)+L²d²)]/[(k+L)rho]. For mass, split lambda=rho(1-eta),e=(1-lambda)/lambda,d_mass=(l+k)/(l+1+(1-l)e). The queried optimum at t+1 gives D_a<=d_mass(A_x+sqrt(1-lambda)R/lambda+eT). Combining the optimum at t+2 gives c_a P_a+c_b P_b<=O(R), where c_a=(beta+e)[d_mass(1-e)-1] and c_b=1-e-d_mass(beta+e)². Even at c_b=0 this bounds P_a, and the same t+2 optimum bounds P_b using P_a at the NEXT actual block. The actual final level2 bridge handles the last block. Thus beta_M=sqrt((1-e)/d_mass)-e is a closed sufficient mass boundary tending2^(-1/4), not an inverse-margin blow-up. The joint draft will include all constants and terminal/benchmark handling. {"id": "d91691f8880332e1", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T02:05:38.989013+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max21 changed-terminal joint draft: a same-word actual seed with x_end-g_end≠0 and a legal seven-step parent now carry the exact terminal value loss, both child energies, all original normals and true distance. Every transverse fifteen-step join of these fixed children fails for 0<eps<=1/100 by a positive combination of FOUR original rows, including the retained terminal normal. This is a new proper-family obstruction, not the old all-earlier-coupling result or an original-target fact. A larger explicit eps1/25 first parent is legal and its15-step join remains open.", "evidence": "# A priced nonzero terminal residual and an original-row separation\n\nStatus: **UNVERIFIED self-contained joint proof candidate**, max round21,\nthe final authorized round of the existing ninth launch. No optimization,\nnumerical evaluation, symbolic-computation job, installation, verifier call,\nor subagent is used. None of the claims below is a new fact. The original\nall-schedule exact-profile target remains open.\n\nAccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation convention\nand its necessity. All new constructions and estimates are derived here;\nearlier unverified findings are motivation, not proof premises. Local\nnotation is defined independently below.\n\n## 1. An unavoidable terminal price\n\nNormalize the optimum sample to x_star=g_star=f_star=0 and smoothness to1.\nFor actual terminal position x, gradient w, value f, and residual v=x-w,\nthe original optimum inequality is\n\n    Q_(end,star)=f-<w,x>+||w||^2/2\n                =f-||x||^2/2+||v||^2/2 <=0.             (1)\n\nThus a change with x fixed and v nonzero MUST lower an old saturated value\nf_old=||x||^2/2 by at least ||v||^2/2. Keeping that old value as well as x\nand optimum0 would force v=0. The construction below keeps every earlier\npoint, gradient, and value and pays exactly this required terminal loss.\nIt does not pretend that the terminal value can be held fixed.\n\nFor projection jets the price is an equality:\n\n    f=<x,w>-||w||^2/2,\n    J=2f=||w||^2+2<w,v>=||x||^2-||v||^2.               (2)\n\nA physical joining step of size1+r lands at\n\n    d=x-(1+r)w=v-rw,\n    Z=||d||^2=r^2||w||^2-2r<w,v>+||v||^2.              (3)\n\nIn particular alignment with -rw would be incorrect when v is nonzero.\n\n## 2. The complete reusable boundary contract\n\nA child has a finite nonnegative word p of length ell, positions x_i and\ngradients g_i for0<=i<=ell satisfying x_(i+1)=x_i-p_i*g_i. Write\n\n    K=conv(0,g_0,...,g_ell), y_i=x_i-g_i, e_i=<g_i,y_i>.\n\nRequire all vertex normals and the optimum normals:\n\n    <g_j-g_i,y_i><=0 for every i,j, and e_i>=0.           (4)\n\nAlso supply a nonnegative cone representation of x_0 in the gradients\ng_0,...,g_ell. This is a separate input: a nonzero terminal residual does\nnot automatically give it. Define\n\n    F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),\n    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,\n    A=1+sum p_i, R^2=||x_0||^2>0,\n    w=g_ell, v=y_ell, J=||w||^2+2<w,v>.\n\nHere and throughout, a convex hull contains all listed gradients, including\nduplicates and the genuine queried gradient at every joining endpoint.\n\nCompleting the square proves that the maximizer defining F_K is the unique\nEuclidean projection P_K(x). Adding the two projection optimality\ninequalities gives\n\n    ||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2<=<x-x',P_K(x)-P_K(x')>.\n\nIt follows that P_K is1-Lipschitz. Evaluating the two defining maxima at\neach other's maximizers bounds\nF_K(x')-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),x'-x> between0 and ||x'-x||^2.\nThus F_K is finite, convex, differentiable, and1-smooth, with gradient P_K.\nCondition(4) proves that it realizes every supplied jet.\n\nThe minimizers are exactly\n\n    K^polar={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in K}.\n\nFor sufficiency use the maximum defining F_K. For necessity, F_K(z)=0\nimplies <z,tg>-||tg||^2/2<=0 for every0<t<=1 and g in K; divide by t\nand let t decrease to0. The supplied cone representation gives\n<x_0,z><=0 on K^polar. Hence\n||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2, with equality at z=0: the true closest-minimizer\ndistance is R. This is not a choice of a favorable optimum.\n\nExpanding the original residual of fact1252b8823bb5a290 gives\n\n    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>, Q_(i,star)=0, Q_(star,i)=-e_i.   (5)\n\nConsequently(4) checks every original row, not a selected subset.\n\nTake two such children L,R, of capacities A,C and initial radii R_L,R_R.\nFor r>0 assume d=v_L-r*w_L is nonzero, and put Z=||d||^2. Set\n\n    e=d/sqrt(Z), b=x_R,0/R_R, alpha=sqrt(Z)/R_R,\n    J_L u=(<u,e>, u-<u,e>e, 0),\n    J_R z=(<z,b>, 0, z-<z,b>b).                          (6)\n\nThe common space is R direct-sum e^perp direct-sum b^perp. These are\nactual isometries. Embed all left data by J_L and all right data by\nalpha*J_R. The bridge1+r is exact because\n\n    J_L(x_L,ell-(1+r)w_L)=J_L d=alpha*J_R x_R,0.\n\nThe cross Gram is explicitly\n\n    <J_L u,alpha*J_R z>=<u,d><x_R,0,z>/R_R^2.             (7)\n\nPrescribe the left values unchanged and the right values multiplied by\nalpha^2. Every within-child and optimum row is retained. The complete\nremaining conditions, including all endpoint and bridge incidences, are\n\n    <d,y_L,i><x_R,0,g_R,j>/R_R^2 <= e_L,i,               (8L)\n    <d,g_L,i><x_R,0,y_R,j> <= Z*e_R,j                    (8R)\n\nfor every i,j. These are necessary and sufficient for this specified\nembedding and value prescription, by(5) and(7). If they hold, the convex\nhull of0 and all parent gradients gives one actual F_K. The retained left\ncone representation gives its true initial radius R_L.\n\nThe physical parent capacity is P=A+C+r. Its final gradient and residual\nare alpha*J_R w_R and alpha*J_R v_R; both survive. Its exact energy is\n\n    J_parent=Z*J_R/R_R^2.                                (9)\n\nWriting kappa_L=A^2*J_L/R_L^2 and kappa_R=C^2*J_R/R_R^2,\nif r*P=A*C then\n\n    kappa_parent=[Z/(r^2*J_L)]*kappa_L*kappa_R.            (10)\n\nIn terms of tau=<w_L,v_L>/||w_L||^2 and nu=||v_L||^2/||w_L||^2,\nthe first factor is\n\n    (1-2tau/r+nu/r^2)/(1+2tau).                          (11)\n\nThis keeps BOTH child energies and every terminal term. It is an energy\nidentity conditional on legality, not a claim that arbitrary children join.\n\n## 3. One terminal-gradient change on the same physical word\n\nLet\n\n    s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1,\n    a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300,\n    rho=599/600, delta=1-rho=1/600,\n    0<eps<=1/100, E=eps^2,\n    Klen=a+rho, z=b-1, m=a+b, eta=z/Klen,\n    A=1+2a+b=(143+105s)/50.\n\nTake orthogonal unit vectors u,v_axis,q_axis, and define\n\n    U=u, V=sqrt(eta)*v_axis,\n    x_3=rho*V+eps*q_axis,\n    w=g_3=rho*V+(eps/2)*q_axis,\n    v=y_3=(eps/2)*q_axis,\n    g_0=g_1=U, g_2=V,\n    x_2=x_3+aV, x_1=x_2+bU, x_0=x_1+aU.                (12)\n\nThe word is exactly(a,b,a), unchanged from the physical diagnostic word.\nFor eps=1/100 the earlier positions and gradients are exactly those of\nthe previously proposed three-vector seed. Only its terminal gradient is\nchanged. Allowing smaller eps changes the seed's q-coordinate amplitude\nbut never its word. We will exhibit the exact full-coupling obstruction\nat eps=1/10000.\n\nThe residuals and normal energies are\n\n    y_0=(m-1)U+Klen*V+eps*q_axis, e_0=m-1,\n    y_1=zU+Klen*V+eps*q_axis,     e_1=z,\n    y_2=(Klen-1)V+eps*q_axis,    e_2=(Klen-1)eta,\n    y_3=(eps/2)q_axis,          e_3=E/4.                  (13)\n\nHere is the complete seed normal check against vertices0,U,V,w:\n\n* At sample0, the normals against0,V,w respectively are\n  -(m-1), -a, -a-delta*z+E/2; the U normal is0.\n* At sample1, they are -z, 0, -delta*z+E/2; the U normal is0.\n* At sample2, the normals against0 and U both equal -(Klen-1)eta,\n  the w normal is -delta*(Klen-1)eta+E/2, and the V normal is0.\n* At sample3, the normals against0,U,V all equal -E/4; the w normal is0.\n\nThe elementary bounds7/5<s<10/7 imply\n\n    139/100<a<143/100, 2<b<201/100,\n    238/100<Klen<243/100, z>1, eta>2/5.\n\nThey give delta*z>1/600>E/2 and\ndelta*(Klen-1)eta>1/1500>E/2. All listed normals are nonpositive;\nall four e_i are positive. The function in section2 therefore realizes\nthese jets and every original Q. There is a genuine nonzero terminal\nresidual. No seed sample has both gradient and positive residual parallel\nto the terminal gradient w.\n\nFor the true distance use the explicit positive cone representation\n\n    x_0=mU+(a-rho)V+2w,                                 (14)\n\nwhose coefficients are positive. Define\n\n    R^2=m^2+Klen*z+E, N=||w||^2=rho^2*eta+E/4,\n    J=2f_3=rho^2*eta+3E/4,\n    <w,v>=||v||^2=E/4.                                  (15)\n\nIn particular all earlier values are unchanged by replacing the old\ng_3=x_3 by w. The terminal value decreases by E/8, exactly the amount\nforced by(1). The construction carries this loss into J; it is not hidden\nin a new normalization.\n\nFor completeness the gradient-energy surplus is positive too. Put\nRbar^2=m^2+Klen*z and Nbar=rho^2*eta. Substituting the displayed a,b,rho,\nm,Klen,z,A into [A^2*rho^2*z-Klen*m^2-Klen^2*z]/\n[Klen*(m^2+Klen*z)] and using s^2=2 gives\n\n    A^2*Nbar/Rbar^2-1=(-P0+Q0*s)/D0,\n    P0=19019047380837966731,\n    Q0=13474265781546157318,\n    D0=40842989603870102500.\n\nAll three integers are positive, and the exact integer identity\n\n    2(2000Q0)^2-(2000P0+D0)^2\n      =2441206548123238434623351342332719331750000>0\n\nproves A^2*Nbar/Rbar^2>1+1/2000 by squaring positive numbers.\nAlso Rbar^2>9 and Nbar<1, so Rbar^2>4Nbar. Adding E/4 to its numerator\nNbar and E to its denominator Rbar^2 raises their ratio. Therefore\n\n    kappa_N,0=A^2*N/R^2>1+1/2000.                        (16)\n\nThis paragraph supplies the algebra and sign certificate within this\njoint candidate; it does not assume an earlier unverified fact.\n\n## 4. An actual seven-step parent with the residual retained\n\nChoose r_0=theta*A>2. For any r>1 the true outgoing vector is\n\n    d_r=v-rw=-r*rho*V+((1-r)eps/2)*q_axis,\n    Z(r)=||d_r||^2=r^2*Nbar+(r-1)^2*E/4.                 (17)\n\nEvery seed gradient and residual has nonnegative coordinates in\n(u,v_axis,q_axis), and d_r has nonpositive coordinates. Moreover\n<x_0,g_i> and <x_0,y_i> are nonnegative. Both inequalities(8L),(8R)\nhold for every pair at r=r_0: their left sides are nonpositive and their\nright sides are nonnegative. This includes i=3 or j=3, so the actual\nqueried joining gradient and its nonzero residual are checked.\n\nSet alpha_0^2=Z(r_0)/R^2 and use the explicit embeddings(6). The convex\nhull construction is one actual smooth convex function for\n\n    h[1]=(a,b,a,1+r_0,a,b,a).                             (18)\n\nIts closest distance is R by the retained cone representation(14).\nIts terminal data are\n\n    w_1=alpha_0*J_R w, v_1=alpha_0*J_R v,\n    J_1=alpha_0^2*J, A_1=lambda*A.\n\nThe nonzero terminal residual survives in the parent. Every left e_i is\nretained and every right e_i is multiplied by alpha_0^2; none is deleted.\n\nFor this seed put tau=E/(4N). Equation(15) gives nu=tau, and the terminal\nshape is invariant under the right-copy scaling. Since Nbar>39/100 and\nE<=1/10000, one has\n\n    0<tau<1/15000.\n\nOn the formal balanced word recurrence\n\n    A_j=lambda^j*A, r_j=theta*A_j,\n    h[j+1]=(h[j],1+r_j,h[j]),\n\nthe unchanged construction, if legal, would have exactly\n\n    kappa_N,j+1=(1-2tau/r_j+tau/r_j^2)*kappa_N,j^2,\n    kappa_E,j=(1+2tau)*kappa_N,j,\n    kappa_E,j+1=[(1-2tau/r_j+tau/r_j^2)/(1+2tau)]\n                    *kappa_E,j^2.                       (19)\n\nHere kappa_N,j=A_j^2*||w_j||^2/R^2 and\nkappa_E,j=A_j^2*J_j/R^2. For r_j>1 the first factor in the first line\nexceeds1-tau. Thus the scalar energy calculation alone stays positive:\n\n    kappa_N,j >= (1-tau)^(2^j-1)*kappa_N,0^(2^j)>1,       (20)\n\nbecause(1-1/15000)(1+1/2000)>1. Equations(19)-(20) do NOT supply\nlegality at j>=2. The next sections exhibit the missing original rows.\nThey explain why the scalar surplus cannot be interpreted as a parent.\n\n## 5. The next specified rank-one join fails at queried rows\n\nRetain the actual seven-step child(18), including its specified first\nembeddings and values. Its initial point is the retained left p=x_0,\nwith norm R. The next balanced bridge has r_1=theta*A_1=A. Abbreviate\n\n    Z0=Z(r_0), Z1=Z(r_1), D=m+Klen=A-delta,\n    H0=r_0*rho*z+(r_0-1)E/2,\n    H1=r_1*rho*z+(r_1-1)E/2.\n\nThe true outgoing vector of the seven-step child is\n\n    d_1=v_1-r_1*w_1=alpha_0*J_R d_(r_1),\n    ||d_1||^2=alpha_0^2*Z1,\n    alpha_1^2=||d_1||^2/R^2.                             (21)\n\nThe prospective fifteen-step parent has eight samples from the left\nchild, then eight samples from the right child; its right-child index j\nhas original parent index8+j. In the rank-one construction(6), expanding\nthe two cross rows gives\n\n    Q_(2,9)=alpha_1^2*z*(C2-1),\n    C2=r_0*rho*eta*H1*(D+E/z)/(Z0*Z1),                  (22)\n\n    Q_(6,13)=alpha_1^2*alpha_0^2*z*(C6-1),\n    C6=r_1*rho*eta*H0*(D+E/z)/(Z0*Z1).                 (23)\n\nFor(22), child gradient2 is J_L V, while its residual1 and initial point\nare retained from the seed. Use\n\n    <J_L V,d_1>=r_0*rho*eta*H1/R^2,\n    <p,y_1>=zD+E, e_1=z.\n\nFor(23), child gradient6 and residual5 are\nalpha_0*J_R V and alpha_0*J_R y_1. Therefore\n\n    <g_6,d_1>=-alpha_0^2*r_1*rho*eta,\n    <p,y_5>=-H0*(zD+E)/R^2,\n    e_5=alpha_0^2*z.\n\nSubstitution in(7) and(5) gives(22)-(23), without suppressing any\nwithin-child row or optimum row. Every index2,9,6,13 is queried in the\nfifteen-step word.\n\nHere is a common exact lower bound for C2 and C6. At E=0 both equal\n\n    C0=Klen*(A-delta)/(theta*A^2*rho^2).                  (24)\n\nThe numerator factors in(22)-(23) increase with E. Also\nZ(r)<=r^2*(Nbar+E/4) for r>1. Consequently\n\n    C2,C6 >= C0/(1+E/(4Nbar))^2.                         (25)\n\nSquaring rational endpoints proves14142/10000<s<14143/10000.\nSubstitution into a,b,A yields Klen>2.411, A-delta>5.828,\nA<5.8301, theta<.4143, and rho^2<.9967. All terminating decimals here\nare exact rationals. Thus\n\n    Klen*(A-delta)>2.411*5.828=14.051308,\n    theta*A^2*rho^2<.4143*(5.8301)^2*.9967<14.036,\n    14.036*(1+1/1000)=14.050036<14.051308.\n\nThis proves C0>1+1/1000. Since E/(4Nbar)<1/15000,\n\n    (1+1/15000)^2<1+1/7000,\n    (1+1/2000)(1+1/7000)<1+1/1000,\n\nand(25) proves\n\n    C2>1+1/2000, C6>1+1/2000.                            (26)\n\nThe next rank-one join is therefore illegal for the entire interval\n0<eps<=1/100, despite the positive scalar recursion(20).\n\n## 6. A quantitative angle bound on every transverse coupling\n\nThis section allows ALL transverse couplings in the fifteen-step join,\nwhile keeping the actual seven-step child(18) fixed. The first parent\nhas its explicit embeddings(6). No claim about changing that earlier\nembedding is made.\n\nLet L,R be arbitrary linear isometries of the two copies of this child\ninto any common Euclidean space, and keep left scale1. A legal bridge\nforces\n\n    alpha_1*R p=L d_1,\n\nwhere alpha_1 is necessarily the positive number in(21). Let P_d denote\northogonal projection perpendicular to d_1 inside the left child space,\nand P_p perpendicular to p inside the right child space. Orthogonal\ndecomposition gives the exact identity\n\n    <L g_6,alpha_1*R y_5>\n      =<g_6,d_1><p,y_5>/R^2\n          +alpha_1*<L P_d g_6,R P_p y_5>.                (27)\n\nBoth mixed parallel/perpendicular terms vanish by the forced alignment.\nBy the ordinary Cauchy-Schwarz inequality the second term is bounded\nbelow by -alpha_1*||P_d g_6||*||P_p y_5||. This is valid in every\ndimension and for every real positive-semidefinite completion; no\nparticular contraction or matrix rank is imposed.\n\nBecause g_6=alpha_0*J_R V and d_1=alpha_0*J_R d_(r_1),\nthe transverse norm is exactly\n\n    ||P_d g_6||\n      =alpha_0*sqrt(eta-r_1^2*rho^2*eta^2/Z1)\n      =alpha_0*(r_1-1)*eps*sqrt(eta)/(2sqrt(Z1)).         (28)\n\nFurther ||P_p y_5||<=alpha_0*||y_1||. Divide the possible correction\nin(27) by the positive row scale alpha_1^2*e_5, where e_5=alpha_0^2*z.\nEquations(21),(28) imply the upper bound\n\n    Tcorr <= R^2*(r_1-1)*eps*sqrt(eta)*||y_1||\n                     /(2sqrt(Z0)*Z1*z)\n          <= R^2*(r_1-1)*eps*||y_1||\n                     /(2r_0*r_1^2*rho^3*eta*z).         (29)\n\nThe second inequality uses Z0>=r_0^2*rho^2*eta and\nZ1>=r_1^2*rho^2*eta. From the rational bounds in section3,\n\n    R^2<15, ||y_1||<2, r_1-1<5,\n    r_0>2, r_1>5, rho^3>97/100, eta>2/5, z>1.\n\nFor the first two bounds, explicitly\nR^2<(3.44)^2+2.43*1.01+.0001<15 and\n||y_1||^2<1.01^2+2.43*1.01+.0001<4.\nIt follows from(29) that\n\n    Tcorr <[150/(2*2*25*(97/100)*(2/5))]*eps<4eps.       (30)\n\nThe aligned contribution of(27) is still exactly(23). Thus for EVERY\ncoupling with the required physical alignment and the prescribed values,\n\n    Q_(6,13)/(alpha_1^2*e_5) >= C6-1-Tcorr\n                              >1/2000-4eps.             (31)\n\nIn particular at the explicit eps=1/10000,\n\n    Q_(6,13)/(alpha_1^2*e_5)>1/10000>0.                  (32)\n\nThis is an exact original-row separation for all transverse choices at\nthe next join of these fixed actual seven-step children. Both children\nare individually legal, have nonzero terminal residual, and retain the\nstrict energy surplus(16); their prospective fifteen-step parent cannot\nexist with this alignment, internal data, and value prescription.\n\nMore generally any successful next join inside this parametrized family\nmust satisfy the explicit compatibility condition\n\n    alpha_1*||P_d g_6||*||P_p y_5||\n           >=alpha_1^2*e_5*(C6-1).                      (33)\n\nFor0<eps<=1/100 the bounds above give the simpler necessary condition\neps>1/8000. It is only necessary. Increasing eps beyond this bound does\nnot prove feasibility. The obstruction is to arbitrarily small changes\nof this specified geometry, not to every nonlinear geometry.\n\n## 7. The retained terminal normal gives a stronger four-row separation\n\nThe original interpolation rows give more information than the norm bound\nin section6. This section excludes the next join for the entire interval\n0<eps<=1/100, including the original q-coordinate amplitude1/100.\nThe actual seven-step child and its first embeddings remain fixed.\n\nTwo exact vector identities in that child are decisive:\n\n    d_1=-r_1*rho*g_6-(r_1-1)*v_1,\n    y_5=z*g_4+(a-rho)*g_6+2*g_7.                         (34)\n\nFor the first identity, use w_1=rho*g_6+v_1 and d_1=v_1-r_1*w_1.\nFor the second, use the seed identity\ny_1=zU+(a-rho)V+2w, and apply alpha_0*J_R.\nEvery coefficient in the second identity is positive. The energy of the\nleft endpoint residual v_1 is\n\n    e_7=<g_7,v_1>=alpha_0^2*E/4.\n\nLet\n\n    k=(r_1-1)/(r_1*rho)>0, T_y=z+(a-rho)+2=m+delta.\n\nUse arbitrary second-join isometries L,R with\nalpha_1*R p=L d_1, as in section6, and write G_i,Y_i for the resulting\nparent gradient and residual at original index i. The first identity(34)\nimplies\n\n    <G_6,Y_13>=-alpha_1^2*<p,y_5>/(r_1*rho)\n                        -k*<L v_1,Y_13>.\n\nThe second identity(34) says\n\n    Y_13=z*G_12+(a-rho)*G_14+2*G_15.\n\nSince Q_(j,7)=<G_j,L v_1>-e_7 for j=12,14,15, the following nonnegative\ncombination of ORIGINAL interpolation rows has no free transverse term:\n\n    S_rows=Q_(6,13)+k[z*Q_(12,7)+(a-rho)*Q_(14,7)+2*Q_(15,7)]\n          =alpha_1^2[-<p,y_5>/(r_1*rho)-e_5]-k*T_y*e_7\n          =alpha_1^2*e_5*[I-1-k*T_y*E/(4z*alpha_1^2)],   (35)\n\nwhere\n\n    I=H0*(D+E/z)/(r_1*rho*Z0).                           (36)\n\nHere e_5=alpha_0^2*z and the initial pairing <p,y_5> was calculated\nexplicitly in section5. The identity does not impose a rank or an angle\ncondition. The queried row(6,13) and the rows entering the queried bridge\nendpoint7 all must hold. The term(15,7) is OUTGOING from the unqueried\nfinal sample, so even the accepted unqueried-terminal reduction retains\nit. There is no use of a deleted incoming-final row.\n\nAt E=0 expression(36) equals C0 from(24). Its numerator factors increase\nwith E, and Z0<=r_0^2*(Nbar+E/4). Hence\n\n    I>=C0/(1+E/(4Nbar))>1+1/1200,                       (37)\n\nbecause C0>1+1/1000, E/(4Nbar)<1/15000, and\n(1+1/1200)(1+1/15000)<1+1/1000.\n\nThe terminal-normal charge is bounded without dropping it. We have\nk<1 because r_1*delta<6/600<1, and T_y<7/2. Also r_0>12/5 and\nr_1>29/5 follow from the rational bounds on s and A. Equations(17),(21),\nNbar>39/100, and R^2<15 give\n\n    alpha_1^2=Z0*Z1/R^4\n       >[(12/5)^2*(29/5)^2*(39/100)^2]/225>1/8.          (38)\n\nThe final rational comparison in(38) is, on clearing denominators,\n8*144*841*1521>625*10000*225; the two sides are1473593472 and1406250000.\nConsequently\n\n    0<k*T_y*E/(4z*alpha_1^2)<7E<=7/10000.\n\nCombining this with(35),(37) gives the strict positive separation\n\n    S_rows/(alpha_1^2*e_5)>1/1200-7/10000=1/7500>0.      (39)\n\nAll four coefficients in S_rows are positive. Therefore all four original\nrows cannot be nonpositive. This excludes EVERY transverse coupling at\nthe next join of the displayed actual seven-step children for every\n0<eps<=1/100. The terminal residual was not erased: its original normal\nis the source of the finite charge in(35), and this charge is too small\nto repair the compatibility gap.\n\nMore generally the exact necessary compatibility condition for the same\nfirst-parent family is\n\n    I-1 <= k*T_y*E/(4z*alpha_1^2).                        (40)\n\nWithin0<eps<=1/100, the coarse estimates already require\nE>1/8400, in conflict with E<=1/10000. This is a necessary condition\nonly; satisfying(40) would not imply the other original rows or a parent.\n\n## 8. Exact scope and the smallest unresolved object\n\nThere are now three distinct statements, all at UNVERIFIED draft level:\n\n1. For every0<eps<=1/100, (12) is an actual changed terminal seed and(18)\n   is one actual seven-step parent. All original interpolation rows,\n   values, the physical bridge, both child effects, normal energies, and\n   true closest distance are supplied explicitly.\n2. For that fixed first parent, the next rank-one operation fails at\n   original queried rows(2,9) and(6,13) throughout this interval.\n3. For every0<eps<=1/100, EVERY transverse coupling at the next join\n   fails by the nonnegative four-row combination(35)-(39). This permits\n   arbitrary ambient dimension, but fixes the first parent's data and\n   all displayed values. The weaker angle estimate remains a separately\n   derived diagnostic; it is not the scope of the final separation.\n\nThe next concrete unresolved instance can be specified without a solver:\ntake the SAME formulas(12),(17),(18) with eps=1/25, preserving the word,\nrho, and all other definitions. This is still an actual legal seed and\nfirst parent. Indeed Klen>12/5 and eta>2/5 imply\ndelta*(Klen-1)eta>7/7500>1/1250=E/2, and delta*z>1/600>E/2.\nThese verify the only potentially delicate seed normals; the sign proof\nof(8L),(8R) still applies. The positive cone, terminal price, and exact\nenergy law are unchanged. Formula(16) remains valid because its monotonic\nratio argument holds for every E>0. This larger amplitude is not excluded\nby the bounds used to prove(39), and no legality of its fifteen-step join\nis asserted. Its smallest pending parent is the explicit word h[2] with\nthe true alignment(21), all prescribed values, and all original Q rows.\n\nAnother remaining freedom is to change the FIRST seven-step embedding,\neven at eps=1/100, and then form a parent. Sections6-7 do not cover\narbitrary earlier couplings. General value-height changes are also outside\nthis result. Thus this is a proper-family obstruction, not a claim that\nall terminal residuals or all nonlinear geometry are impossible.\n\nThe physical word was retained throughout. A_j=1+sum h[j] is its actual\nshifted stepsize sum, NOT the exact Zhang-Jiang objective denominator\nB_(4*2^j-1). The actual seed and seven-step objective lower bounds are\nrespectively J/R^2 and Z0*J/R^4. No identification of these numbers with\n1/B_n is made, no new horizon-dependent word is substituted, and no\nasymptotic implication for the original target follows from this finite\nconstruction or this proper-family obstruction.\n\nBackground audit only: Taylor, Hendrickx, and Glineur,\n\"Smooth Strongly Convex Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance\nof First-order Methods\", arXiv:1502.05666, Theorem4 proof and Corollary1,\nwere reread in downloads/1502.05666.txt. {"id": "291eba21d8638c63", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T02:14:33.986874+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max2 new uniform original-prefix estimate, fully proved in draft: pi_k=B_(2^k-1),k>17/100 for every actual m>=128 and every same-epsilon state k. The q-product proof retains the additive1 and uses the actual parameter derivative to cancel its adverse factor. Also obtain an exact clock-slack formula for ellhat whose baseline exceeds2d_k, but its signed exit-moment difference remains OPEN; actual third/fourth steps disprove a tempting pathwise monotonicity shortcut.", "evidence": "# Positive off-diagonal entries and exact chronological decimation\n\nStatus: **unverified proof draft**, sixth launch, first continuation round.\nNo kernel evaluation, optimizer, installation, or fact submission was used.\nThe uniform positive-semidefinite conclusion and the original minimax Goal\nremain open. This document proves some of the actual coefficient claims;\nit does not assume the remaining thirteen-band estimates.\n\n## 1. Definitions and the coefficient that is being estimated\n\nFix an integer m>=128 and keep epsilon=1/m fixed throughout. Set\n\n lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,\n d_k=1+a*c^k  (k>=0), H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j).\n\nThe extension of d_k past m-1 is only a coefficient device. The actual\nqueried word is H_m with its last d_0 removed. Write h_i for its steps,\ns_i=sum_(r<i)h_r, and s'=s_i+h_i when discussing a single step.\n\nOn the infinite original ruler word define B_00=1 and\n\n B_(i+1),k=(1-h_i/d_k)B_ik+(h_i/d_(k-1))B_i,(k-1),\n\nwith a zero second term at k=0. For each occupied state k, h_i<=d_k:\nthe first occurrence of d_j kills state j, and all smaller states have\nalready died. This also proves B_ik>=0, sum_k B_ik=1, and\nB_ik=0 for i>=2^k or k>i.\n\nFor arbitrary formal vectors y_k put g_i=sum_k v_ik*y_k, where\nv_ik=B_ik/d_k. The row-finite symmetric matrix E is defined by\n\n sum_(k,l)E_kl<y_k,y_l>\n =sum_i [2s_(i+1)h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>\n       +s_(i+1)||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2].   (1)\n\nEach coefficient is a finite sum: a term containing state k occurs by\nthe step immediately before query2^k. In particular E_kl=0 for k<l\nand l>2^k. The m-principal block is the actual finite residual kernel.\nThe coefficient convention in (1) means that an off-diagonal entry\nappears twice in the quadratic form.\n\n## 2. Two elementary bounds for the actual chronological word\n\nFor the elementary parameter bounds used below, lambda>12/5 and\nlambda<29/12 give 12/5<c<39/16. The upper comparison follows from\n(29/12)(129/128)=3741/1536<39/16. Consequently\na>(127/128)/(39/16)=127/312>2/5. Moreover\n\n d_0=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],\n d_2=2+sqrt(2)-lambda*epsilon^2,\n\nso 7/5<d_0<3/2 and d_2<7/2; for the first lower bound use\nsqrt(2)>141/100 and 2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]<5/768.\nIn particular 2<c<3 and 1<d_0<2. Also\n\n                         d_(k+1)>2d_k  (k>=2).            (2)\n\nIndeed d_(k+1)-2d_k=(c-2)a*c^k-1. At k=2 the quantity\nbefore subtracting1 is (1-epsilon)c(c-2), and\n\n c(c-2)=1+2lambda(lambda-1)epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2\n        >1+6epsilon.\n\nConsequently (1-epsilon)c(c-2)>1+5epsilon-6epsilon^2>1.\nThe expression increases with k, proving (2).\n\nSecond, every original physical step satisfies\n\n                             h_i<s_i+2.                  (3)\n\nLet S_j=sum H_j and C_j=S_j+2-d_j. Then C_0=2-d_0>0.\nThe recurrences S_(j+1)=2S_j+d_j and\nd_(j+1)=c*d_j-(c-1) give the exact identity\n\n C_(j+1)=2C_j+(3-c)(d_j-1)>0.\n\nThe prefix before the FIRST d_j is H_j, so (3) holds there. Every later\noccurrence has a larger elapsed clock and the same step size. This proves\n(3) at every position, without changing the enclosing epsilon.\n\nThere is a sharper envelope that does exclude the recorded swap foil.\nPut rho=c-2 and gamma=rho-a=c-1-d_0>0. The exact word-sum formula gives\n\n rho(S_j+1)+1-d_j=gamma*2^j.\n\nThus every original occurrence h_i=d_j obeys\n\n rho(s_i+1)+1-h_i=gamma*2^j+rho(s_i-S_j)>=0.              (3a)\n\nIf the third and fourth steps are exchanged, the first d_2 has\ns_i=d_0+d_1 instead of S_2=2d_0+d_1. Its slack in (3a) becomes\n4gamma-rho*d_0<13/128-14/25<0. Here rho>2/5, d_0>7/5, and\ngamma<(13/4)epsilon, as proved explicitly in Section7 below. This\ndistinguishes the original chronology from that particular foil.\nEquation (3a) alone is not asserted to prove b_i or u_i.\n\n## 3. Nonnegative off-diagonal entries, term by term\n\nFor a fixed step h=h_i, write v_k=v_ik and v_(-1)=0. The scaled birth\nrecurrence is\n\n v'_k=v_k+(h/d_k)(v_(k-1)-v_k).\n\nFor k<l, expansion of that step's contribution to E_kl gives exactly\n\n C_kl*v_k*v_l\n +[s'h^2(d_l-1)/(d_k*d_l)]v_(k-1)*v_l\n +[s'h^2(d_k-1)/(d_k*d_l)]v_k*v_(l-1)\n +[s'h^2/(d_k*d_l)]v_(k-1)*v_(l-1),                     (4)\n\nwhere\n\n C_kl=h[2s'+1-h-s'h(1/d_k+1/d_l-1/(d_k*d_l))].           (5)\n\nTo check (4), the first term of (1) contributes\ns'h(v'_k*v_l+v_k*v'_l), the second contributes\ns'(v'_k-v_k)(v'_l-v_l), and the third contributes\nh(1-h)v_k*v_l. Expanding these three expressions gives (4)-(5).\n\nThe last three coefficients in (4) are nonnegative. Suppose k>=2.\nIf v_k*v_l=0, the possibly signed coefficient C_kl costs nothing.\nOtherwise state k is occupied, so h<=d_k. Equation (2) gives\nd_l>=d_(k+1)>2d_k and hence\n\n h(1/d_k+1/d_l-1/(d_k*d_l))<3/2.\n\nIt follows from (3) that\n\n C_kl>h(s'/2+1-h)=(h/2)(s_i+2-h)>0.                     (6)\n\nThus every physical step contributes a nonnegative amount to E_kl\nwhen 2<=k<l. This proof keeps the signed terms of the original step\nuntil they combine into C_kl; it does not take their absolute values.\n\nFor k=0,1, support leaves only E_01 and E_12. The first two steps,\nwhose unscaled state vectors are successively V_0,V_1,V_2, give\n\n E_01=(d_0-1)/d_1>0,\n E_12=(d_0+d_1)(d_1-1)/(d_1*d_2)>0.\n\nThese calculations and (4)-(6) prove\n\n                    E_kl>=0 for every k!=l.              (7)\n\nIn particular the actual second-neighbor weights obey e_0=e_1=0 and\n\n                    e_k=E_(k,k+2)>0 for k>=2.             (8)\n\nFor strictness, just before the first d_k both B_ik and B_i,(k+1)\nare positive when k>=2. For B_ik, use the path that takes a birth on\neach of its first k steps and then stays. All those birth probabilities\nare positive, and every subsequent step before the first d_k is smaller\nthan d_k. For B_i,(k+1), take the first k+1 births and then stay; this\nis possible because k+1<=2^k-1, and all later pre-d_k steps are smaller\nthan d_(k+1). At the d_k step, the third term in (4) with l=k+2 is\nstrictly positive: s'>0, d_k>1, v_k>0, v_(k+1)>0. This proves (8).\n\nNo uniform lower bound such as e_k>=1/15 is proved by this strictness\nargument: the displayed positive path probabilities have not been\nestimated uniformly.\n\n## 4. Exact consequences for the unresolved row margins\n\nFor all nonnegative indices define\n\n A_i=E_(i,i+1), e_i=E_(i,i+2),\n b_i=A_i-2(e_(i-1)+e_i),\n u_i=E_ii-A_(i-1)-A_i+e_(i-2)+e_i\n                         -sum_(|l-i|>=3)|E_il|,\n\nwith negative subscripts equal to zero. Let F_i=sum_l E_il be the\nfinite unweighted row sum, and ellhat_i=sum_l E_il*d_l the constant\nload obtained by polarizing (1) against the constant gradient.\n\nEquation (7) removes all absolute values from the far off-diagonal\nsum. Therefore the exact full-row identity is\n\n                     u_i=2E_ii+2(e_(i-2)+e_i)-F_i.         (9)\n\nIn particular a proof of u_i does not need to identify alternating\nsigns of individual far entries. It still needs signed cancellation\nbetween the diagonal, the neighboring entries, and the row sum.\nNonnegative entries do not imply either b_i>=2/15 or u_i>=0.\n\nThis does not discard a terminal coefficient. In the inherited finite\ndecomposition, the triple starting at m-2 remains the square\ne_(m-2)||y_(m-2)+2y_(m-1)||^2. Its coefficient now has the sign in (8).\nThe last diagonal remains\n\n u_(m-1)+sum_(l>=m+2)E_(m-1,l)+b_(m-1)+e_(m-1).\n\nNeither its required quantitative lower bound nor the completed\nconstant-column remainder follows from (7) alone.\n\n## 5. A scalar constant-load formula with the signed difference retained\n\nFor this section all sums run over the infinite coefficient word, but\nevery state-specific sum is finite. Set\n\n mu_ik=h_i B_ik/d_k,\n L_k=sum_(r=0)^k d_r,\n M_k=sum_i h_i*mu_ik,\n N_k=sum_i s_(i+1)*h_i*mu_ik,\n\nand put M_(-1)=N_(-1)=0. The flow equation is\nB_(i+1),k-B_ik=mu_i,(k-1)-mu_ik. Summing it proves\nsum_i mu_ik=1 for each transient state. Weighting it by s_(i+1)\nand summing by parts gives\n\n sum_i s_(i+1)(mu_ik-mu_i,(k-1))=sum_i h_i B_ik=d_k.\n\nStarting with a zero predecessor moment shows\nsum_i s_(i+1)mu_ik=L_k. Thus M_k and N_k are respectively the mean\nexit step and the mean product of exit clock and exit step for state k.\n\nPolarizing (1) against the constant gradient gives\n\n ellhat_k=sum_i h_i[(s_i+1)v_ik+s_(i+1)v_(i+1),k].         (10)\n\nFor example, the first term in (1) contributes\ns'h(v_ik+v_(i+1),k), its increment-square term contributes zero,\nand its last term contributes h(1-h)v_ik. Their sum is (10).\nSubstitute the birth recurrence into (10), then use the two occupation\nidentities just proved. The result is\n\n             ellhat_k=2L_k+1-M_k-(N_k-N_(k-1))/d_k.        (11)\n\nIn detail, before the recurrence contribution the sum is\n2L_k+1-M_k. The remaining term is\nsum_i s'h_i^2(v_i,(k-1)-v_ik)/d_k\n=(N_(k-1)-N_k)/d_k. This checks its sign and its denominator.\n\nFormula (11) is an identity, not a new condition replacing the load\ninequalities. It identifies the chronological mixed moment that must\nbe estimated. Bounding N_k and N_(k-1) separately loses the signed\nquantity needed for the desired 2d_k<=ellhat_k<=11d_k/4.\n\n### 5.1 The first required constant-load row is settled uniformly\n\nThe row k=2 can be evaluated and bounded without estimating the higher\nbirth states. Put t=d_0, u=d_1, d=d_2, and theta=1-t/d. The nonzero\nvalues of B_n2 before its extinction are B_22=1 and B_32=theta.\nAlso B_12=0 and B_42=0. Substitution in (10), using the original\nfirst four steps (t,u,t,d), gives\n\n ellhat_2=[u(t+u)+t(t+u+1)\n                 +(t(2t+u)+d(2t+u+1))*theta]/d.\n\nCollecting its terms, with no inequality, gives\n\n ellhat_2/d=((t+u)/d)^2+(2t+u+1)/d-t^2(2t+u)/d^3.         (11a)\n\nThe actual parameters obey\n7/5<t<3/2, 199/100<u<2, and 17/5<d<7/2.\nThe only new lower bound, d>17/5, follows from\nd=2+sqrt(2)-lambda*epsilon^2,\nsqrt(2)>141/100, and lambda*epsilon^2<(5/2)/128^2<1/100.\n\nFor a lower bound in (11a), its three displayed positive magnitudes\nobey, in order,\n\n ((t+u)/d)^2>(339/350)^2>9/10,\n (2t+u+1)/d>579/350>33/20,\n t^2(2t+u)/d^3<(45/4)/(75/2)=3/10.\n\nHere d^3>(17/5)^3>75/2. Hence ellhat_2/d>9/4.\nFor the upper bound, the corresponding comparisons are\n\n ((t+u)/d)^2<(35/34)^2<17/16,\n (2t+u+1)/d<30/17<177/100,\n t^2(2t+u)/d^3>(49/25)(19/4)/43=931/4300>21/100.\n\nThe last line uses 2t+u>19/4 and d^3<(7/2)^3<43. Therefore\n\n                   9/4<ellhat_2/d_2<1049/400<21/8.        (11b)\n\nIn particular the required interval [2,11/4] holds at k=2 for every\nactual m>=128. The unresolved constant-load rows start at k=3.\n\n### 5.2 A clock-slack identity, with the nonmonotone term exposed\n\nPut rho=c-2, alpha_clock=rho/(1+rho), tau_i=s_(i+1), and\n\n omega_i=alpha_clock*tau_i+1-h_i\n        =[rho(s_i+1)+1-h_i]/(c-1)>=0,\n Z_k=sum_i mu_ik*omega_i,\n W_k=sum_i mu_ik*tau_i*omega_i,\n H2_k=sum_i mu_ik*tau_i^2.\n\nTake all predecessor quantities with index-1 to be zero. The sharp\noriginal clock inequality (3a) supplies omega_i>=0. Weighting the\nbirth flow by tau_i^2 and summing by parts gives\n\n H2_k-H2_(k-1)=sum_i(2tau_i*h_i-h_i^2)B_ik\n              =d_k(2L_k-M_k).\n\nThe definitions also give\nM_k=alpha_clock*L_k+1-Z_k and\nN_k=alpha_clock*H2_k+L_k-W_k. Substitution in (11) yields the exact\nsigned formula\n\n ellhat_k=c*L_k/(c-1)^2-1/(c-1)\n                  +Z_k/(c-1)+(W_k-W_(k-1))/d_k.           (11c)\n\nThe explicit level sum is\nL_k=c*d_k/(c-1)+k-1+gamma/(c-1). Thus the first two terms on the\nright of (11c) equal\n\n [c^2/(c-1)^3]d_k+c(k-1)/(c-1)^2\n                         +c*gamma/(c-1)^3-1/(c-1).       (11d)\n\nFor k>=2 this exceeds2d_k: c^2/(c-1)^3 decreases for c>1,\nand its value at39/16 is24336/12167=2+2/12167. The remaining\nnon-gamma terms are at least1/(c-1)^2, and gamma>0.\n\nThis calculation does NOT prove the lower load bound. Although\nZ_k>=0 and W_k>=0, the difference W_k-W_(k-1) has not been signed.\nIts summand cannot be treated as increasing just because birth-state\nexit times increase. In fact it has a large decrease in the ORIGINAL\nactual word: at the third step, i=2 and h_2=t=d_0,\n\n tau_2*omega_2=(2t+u)[gamma+rho(t+u)]/(c-1)>4,\n\nwhereas at the fourth step, the first d_2,\n\n tau_3*omega_3=(2t+u+d_2)*4gamma/(c-1)<1.\n\nFor the first comparison use 2t+u>47/10 and\nrho(t+u)/(c-1)>(2/5)(339/100)/(3/2)>9/10.\nFor the second use 2t+u+d_2<17/2 and\n4gamma/(c-1)<13/[128(7/5)]=65/896. These hold for every\nactual m>=128. The expected difference in (11c) may still be\nnonnegative, but a proof must use the original exit distribution;\npathwise monotonicity of this clock weight is false.\n\n## 6. An exact reduction that distinguishes the original lower-level order\n\nPut t=d_0, ell=c-1, gamma=ell-t, and\n\n Z(z)=c*z/(1+ell*z),\n A(z)=(1-t*z)(1+ell*z)=1+gamma*z-t*ell*z^2.\n\nThese are new scalar functions, unrelated to the adjacent entries A_i.\nThey are well-defined at every nonnegative reciprocal physical node.\nThe affine-geometric node relation gives\n\n (1-t*z)(1-d_(j+1)*z)=A(z)(1-d_j*Z(z)),\n Z(1/d_(j+1))=1/d_j.                                    (12)\n\nWrite the original infinite step word in consecutive pairs. The exact\npair at position r is (t,d_(nu_2(r+1)+1)), where nu_2 is the exponent\nof2 in a positive integer. After removing every t and lowering the\nremaining level by1, the compressed word is the same original ruler\nword, with the SAME epsilon. For its first r steps write\nP_r(z)=product_(i<r)(1-h_i*z) and s_r=sum_(i<r)h_i. Then\n\n P_(2r)(z)=A(z)^r P_r(Z(z)),\n P_(2r+1)(z)=(1-t*z)A(z)^r P_r(Z(z)),\n s_(2r)=c*s_r-gamma*r,\n s_(2r+1)=c*s_r-gamma*r+t.                               (13)\n\nAll four identities follow by multiplying or summing the individual\npairs in (12). This is a chronological identity, not an identity of\nthe full multiset alone. The foil that exchanges the third and fourth\nsteps destroys the displayed consecutive-pair description.\n\nThe Newton factors also transform exactly:\n\n phi_(k+1)(z)=(1-t*z)(1+ell*z)^k phi_k(Z(z)),              (14)\n\nwhere phi_k(z)=product_(j<k)(1-d_j*z). At epsilon=0, interpreted only\nas a coefficient limit, t=ell=sqrt(2), gamma=0, and A(z)=1-2z^2.\nAt every actual epsilon>0 the linear term gamma*z and the clock defect\n-gamma*r in (13) must both be retained. In fact\n\n gamma=lambda*epsilon+2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]>0.\n\nEquations (12)-(14) do not yet establish a bound for E or the mixed\nmoments in (11). They give an exact original-order transformation in\nwhich those estimates can be pursued without replacing an embedded\nblock's parameters by epsilon=1/j.\n\n## 7. The decimation multiplier changes sign around one\n\nIt is false that the multiplier A at all required physical nodes can\nbe discarded by a uniform inequality A<=1, or by a uniform inequality\nA>=1. Both failures occur at every actual m>=128, without using a\nstandalone-depth parameter in an embedded block.\n\nFor z=1/d_j,\n\n             A(1/d_j)-1=(gamma*d_j-t*ell)/d_j^2.           (15)\n\nWe have 7/5<t<3/2, t<ell<3/2, and\n\n 2epsilon<gamma\n   <(lambda+2/lambda)epsilon\n   =(3sqrt(2)-1)epsilon<(13/4)epsilon.                    (16)\n\nAt j=2, d_2<7/2, so\ngamma*d_2<91/1024<49/25<t*ell. Therefore A(1/d_2)<1.\nAt the actual highest level j=m-1,\n\n gamma*d_(m-1)>(4/5)2^(m-1)/m>9/4>t*ell.\n\nHere a>2/5 and c>2 give the first bound. For the second, 2^(m-1)/m\nis increasing for m>=2, and even 2^10/128=8 already exceeds45/16.\nThis proves A(1/d_(m-1))>1. Both indices are in the original residual\ncoordinate set. These are counterexamples only to discarding the\ndecimation factor in one direction, not to the desired band bounds.\n\nThere is nevertheless a useful uniform estimate. For k>=8 define\n\n X_k=(65/16)epsilon*(2/c)^k+(225/32)*(2/c^2)^k.\n\nThen, for every 0<=z<=1/d_k and every integer 0<=r<=2^(k-1),\n\n 0<A(z),\n r|A(z)-1|<=X_k<1/80,\n |A(z)^r-1|<=(80/79)X_k<1/79,\n 0<=gamma*r/d_k<=(65/16)epsilon*(2/c)^k.                 (17)\n\nFor r=0 all expressions with r vanish. For positive r, (16),\nt*ell<9/4, a>2/5, and d_k>a*c^k give\n\n r|A(z)-1|\n <=2^(k-1)[gamma/d_k+t*ell/d_k^2]\n <(65/16)epsilon*(2/c)^k+(225/32)*(2/c^2)^k.\n\nAlso c>12/5, so 2/c<5/6 and 2/c^2<3/8. For k>=8, the exact rational\ncomparisons (5/6)^8<1/4 and (3/8)^8<1/2500 show\n\n X_k<65/8192+9/3200<1/80.\n\nPositivity follows from z<=1/d_k<1/t. With delta=|A(z)-1|, if A(z)>=1\nthe binomial expansion gives\n\n A(z)^r-1<=sum_(j>=1)(r*delta)^j<=X_k/(1-X_k).\n\nIf A(z)<1, 1-A(z)^r<=r*delta<=X_k. These prove the third estimate in\n(17); the last follows from the linear part of the same calculation.\n\nThus the multiplier and clock defects are uniformly small far up the\nactual spectrum, despite the change of sign in (15). Their precise\nrates include BOTH epsilon*(2/c)^k and (2/c^2)^k. This does not yet\ncontrol E: converting pointwise spectral estimates to a birth coefficient\nrequires retaining the signed divided differences, not taking a large\nabsolute bound on the entire inverse Newton matrix.\n\n## 8. An early-time bound for the entire nearby band\n\nThis localization is in PHYSICAL TIME, in addition to the earlier\nlocalization in matrix-column distance. It controls the part of a near\nentry accumulated before the low reciprocal columns die.\n\nFix j>=2 and let E^early_kl be the contribution to (1) of the first\n2^j steps, namely the prefix (H_j,d_j). If k,l>=j+6, then\n\n                         |E^early_kl|<10^(-10).           (18)\n\nHere is a uniform proof. Write T=sum(H_j,d_j). The exact word sum is\n\n sum H_j=a*c^j/(c-2)+(1-a/(c-2))*2^j-1.\n\nBecause 0<a/(c-2)<1 and 2<c, this sum is less than c^j-1.\nConsequently T<(1+1/a)d_j<(7/2)d_j. All steps in the prefix are at\nmost d_j, and d_j>3.\n\nFor an integer r>=0 define\n\n p_r=min{1,(7/2)^r/[r!*2^(r(r-1)/2)]}.\n\nAt each query of this prefix, the probability of state j+r is at most\np_r. To prove this, condition on a birth path's first arrival at j.\nReaching j+r requires r subsequent births on increasing distinct\nsteps. Each specified set has probability at most the product of its\nsteps divided by d_j...d_(j+r-1); all stay factors are at most one.\nThe sum of those step products is at most T^r/r!, and (2) bounds the\ndenominator below by d_j^r*2^(r(r-1)/2). This proof applies at either\nend of every step and for every arrival time.\n\nThe sequence p_r decreases for r>=3. Thus, for k>=j+6,\n\n 0<=v_ik,v_(i+1),k<=p_6/d_k,\n |v_(i+1),k-v_ik|<=h_i[(5/2)p_5+p_6]/d_k^2.\n\nFor the second line, apply the birth recurrence and use\nd_k/d_(k-1)<c<5/2. Define Q_6=(5/2)p_5+p_6. The exact fractions give\n\n p_5=16807/3932160<1/230,\n p_6=117649/1509949440<1/12500,\n Q_6<1/92+1/12500<11/1000.\n\nThe first and last terms of (1), polarized for a general k,l, are at\nmost [T^2+2d_j*T]p_6^2/(d_k*d_l) in absolute value. This uses\n2sum_i s_(i+1)h_i=T^2+sum_i h_i^2 and\nsum_i h_i^2<=d_j*T. The increment-square term is at most\nT^2*d_j*Q_6^2/(d_k^2*d_l^2), since\nsum_i s_(i+1)h_i^2<=T^2*d_j. These statements also apply when k=l.\n\nNow d_k,d_l>2^6*d_j. The two contributions are respectively less than\n\n 77/[4*2^12*12500^2] < 1/30000000000,\n 49*121/[12*2^24*1000^2] < 1/30000000000.\n\nTheir sum is less than10^(-10), proving (18). Every estimate is\nuniform in both the row indices and the enclosing m.\n\nFor a thirteen-entry row centered at i>=14, take j=i-12. Then both\nindices of each entry E_(i,i+r), -6<=r<=6, are at least j+6, so (18)\ncontrols its entire initial prefix through the first d_(i-12). This\ndoes not remove any of the later repeated low steps; their physical\norder and elapsed clock remain part of the late contribution.\n\n## 9. Only eighteen reciprocal nodes remain in the late contribution\n\nFor this section fix an ACTUAL required row 24<=i<=m-1, put j=i-12,\nand consider l=i+r with -6<=r<=6. Extended indices l>=m are only the\nsame-epsilon coefficient device, as in Section1. All contributing\nphysical steps are still through the first d_i and are in the original\nqueried word.\n\nDefine R_kb=(d_k/d_b)phi_k(1/d_b) for b>=k, and zero for b<k.\nIts triangular inverse D has entries\n\n D_bk=product_(0<=r<=k,r!=b)(1-d_r/d_b)^(-1),  0<=b<=k,\n D_bk=0 for b>k.                                         (19)\n\nOne direct check uses Lagrange interpolation at the distinct nodes\n1/d_0,...,1/d_k. Comparing highest-degree coefficients shows that the\nweights reciprocal to the products of node differences annihilate\npolynomials of degree below k and recover the leading coefficient\nat degree k. Apply this identity to phi_r, whose leading coefficient\nis (-1)^r product_(s<r)d_s, and rescale by d_b/d_k. The resulting\ninverse of R is exactly (19).\n\nWriting P_n(z)=product_(t<n)(1-h_t*z), the corresponding scalar\ncoefficient identity is\n\n                  v_nk=sum_(b=0)^k D_bk*P_n(1/d_b)/d_b.  (20)\n\nIndeed P_n(z)=sum_k B_nk phi_k(z), by the birth recurrence. Evaluation\nat the first k+1 reciprocal nodes kills every phi_r with r>k, and\ninversion by (19) gives (20).\n\nFor every query n>=2^j and every b<=j, P_n(1/d_b)=0 exactly; the same\nis true at the following query. Thus all such nodes disappear from\nthe late part of (1). Define M^late_ab to be the quadratic coefficient\nof the normalized modal columns P_n(1/d_a)/d_a and P_n(1/d_b)/d_b,\nusing only physical steps 2^j<=n<2^i, with their ORIGINAL elapsed\nclocks s_n. Then (20) gives the exact identity\n\n E_il=E^early_il\n          +sum_(a=j+1)^i sum_(b=j+1)^l D_ai*M^late_ab*D_bl. (21)\n\nFor l<i every term vanishes already by query2^l; for l>=i the first\ncolumn vanishes by query2^i. Consequently the displayed final cutoff\nis correct in both cases and adds no physical step beyond the original\ntrajectory. Only the eighteen nodes i-11,...,i+6 can occur in (21).\n\nCrucially, the remaining inverse columns have a uniform absolute sum.\nFor each k in [i-6,i+6],\n\n                         sum_(b=j+1)^k |D_bk|<6.          (22)\n\nTo prove this, put q_c=1/c and P^q_s=product_(r=1)^s(1-q_c^r),\nwith P^q_0=1. Factoring each difference in (19) gives\n\n |D_bk|=q_c^((k-b)(k-b+1)/2)\n             *(d_b/(d_b-1))^k/(P^q_b P^q_(k-b)).\n\nSince q_c<5/12, the elementary inequality\nproduct_r(1-x_r)>=1-sum_r x_r for 0<=x_r<=1 gives\nP^q_s>=1-q_c-q_c^2>59/144>2/5 for s>=1.\nAlso P^q_1>7/12. For b>=i-11 and k<=i+6,\n\n k/(a*c^b)<(5/2)(i+6)/2^(i-11)<=75/8192<1/100.\n\nThe middle expression decreases with i>=24. Hence\n(d_b/(d_b-1))^k<=exp(k/(a*c^b))<exp(1/100).\nSumming with r=k-b and then extending the positive sum to all r>=0,\n\n sum_b |D_bk|\n <(5/2)exp(1/100)\n       *[1+5/7+(5/2)*sum_(r>=2)(5/12)^(r(r+1)/2)]\n <=(5/2)exp(1/100)[1+5/7+625/3206]\n <5 exp(1/100)<500/99<6.\n\nFor the second line, ratios of successive terms starting at r=2\nare at most(5/12)^3, so their sum is at most125/1603. For the last\nline, exp(x)<=1/(1-x) for 0<=x<1 follows by comparing its power\nseries with the geometric series. This proves (22).\n\nFor example, if an analytic approximation changes every normalized\nlate modal coefficient in this eighteen-node window by at most eta,\nthen (21)-(22) change each reconstructed near entry by at most36eta.\nDiscarding the early part costs an additional10^(-10), by (18).\nThis is a genuine uniform coefficient-stability estimate; no small\nfixed-index inverse coefficient is included in its norm.\n\nThe pointwise decimation bound (17) is only needed while a reciprocal\ncolumn is alive. For the node 1/d_b it is identically zero after\nquery2^b, so its even-query formula only requires r<=2^(b-1), exactly\nthe range of (17). Multiplying that bound by an unnecessarily longer\nhorizon would lose the extinction information.\n\nThis leaves a specific analytic step: control the signed late K/T\ncoefficients under (12)-(14), with the original clock, and bound the\nresulting finite window uniformly. Neither (21) nor (22) asserts those\nremaining estimates or the signs of b_i and u_i.\n\n## 10. A uniform lower bound for the surviving mass at a killing step\n\nLet pi_k=B_(2^k-1),k be the probability still in state k immediately\nBEFORE the original first d_k. Then, at all actual m>=128 and every\nsame-epsilon coefficient index k>=0,\n\n                              pi_k>17/100.                (23)\n\nThis is a quantitative probability bound, not the required quantitative\nsecond-neighbor bound e_k>=1/15.\n\nHere is a product proof. Put q_c=1/c and n_exp,k=2^k-1-k.\nAll smaller states have died before the first d_k. Evaluating\nP_(H_k)(z)=sum_r B_(2^k-1),r phi_r(z) at z=1/d_k therefore gives\n\n pi_k=product_(r=1)^k (1-d_(k-r)/d_k)^(2^(r-1)-1)\n     =(1-1/d_k)^(n_exp,k)\n           product_(r=1)^k(1-q_c^r)^(2^(r-1)-1).           (24)\n\nThe second equality uses\n1-d_(k-r)/d_k=(1-1/d_k)(1-q_c^r). In particular pi_0=pi_1=1,\npi_2=1-d_0/d_2>19/34, and\npi_3=(1-d_0/d_3)^3(1-d_1/d_3)>(3/4)^3(7/10)>17/100.\nFor the last comparison, d_3>34/5: the formula\nd_3=1+lambda^2(1+epsilon-epsilon^2-epsilon^3) exceeds\n1+lambda^2=4+2sqrt(2)>34/5. The bounds on d_0,d_1 are in Sections2,5.\n\nFor k>=4 define the positive convergent product\n\n Phi(q)=product_(r>=1)(1-q^r)^(2^(r-1)-1),  0<q<1/2,\n S_1(q)=sum_(r>=1)(2^(r-1)-1)q^r\n       =q/(1-2q)-q/(1-q).\n\nConvergence, as well as the termwise differentiations below on the\nclosed parameter interval, follows by comparison with sum_r r(2q)^r.\nUsing log(1-x)>=-x/(1-x) and -log(1-x)>=x in (24) gives\n\n log(pi_k)-log Phi(q_c)\n >=-n_exp,k/(d_k-1)+sum_(r>k)(2^(r-1)-1)q_c^r\n =-[gamma/(a(c-2))](2q_c)^k\n           +[(k+1)/a-1/(c-1)]q_c^k\n >-(325/16)epsilon*(5/6)^k.                              (25)\n\nHere gamma=c-2-a, a>2/5, c-2>2/5, gamma<(13/4)epsilon,\nand 2q_c<5/6. The second bracket is positive.\n\nWe compare Phi(q_c) with its critical parameter q_0=1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1.\nFor 0<=epsilon<=1/128, q_c=q_0/(1+epsilon), and\n\n d/d epsilon log Phi(q_c)\n =1/(1+epsilon) sum_(r>=1)\n             (2^(r-1)-1)r q_c^r/(1-q_c^r)\n >=[q_c/(1+epsilon)]S_1'(q_c)>11.                         (26)\n\nIndeed q_c>16/39>41/100 implies q_c/(1+epsilon)>2/5.\nAlso q_c<5/12 and\n\n S_1'(q_c)=1/(1-2q_c)^2-1/(1-q_c)^2\n          >2500/81-144/49>55/2.\n\nIntegrating (26) from0 to epsilon and combining with (25) proves\n\n log(pi_k)>log Phi(q_0)\n              +[11-(325/16)(5/6)^k]epsilon\n          >=log Phi(q_0),\n\nsince (325/16)(5/6)^4=203125/20736<11.\n\nFinally Phi(q_0)>17/100 follows from elementary bounds, with no\ninfinite-product evaluation. For 0<=x<1,\n-log(1-x)<=x+x^2/[2(1-x)]. The r=1 exponent is zero, and\nq_0^2<1/5. Thus\n\n -log Phi(q_0)<=S_1(q_0)+(5/8)S_1(q_0^2)\n =1+sqrt(2)/2+(5/8)(5-3sqrt(2))/14\n <12/7+11/320<7/4.\n\nThe last inequalities use sqrt(2)<10/7 and sqrt(2)>141/100.\nThe exponential series gives exp(1)<11/4: the sum through degree4\nis65/24, and its remaining tail is at most1/100. It also gives\nexp(3/4)<17/8: the sum through degree3 is269/128, and the tail\nstarting at degree4 is at most135/8704; their sum is18427/8704,\nwhich is smaller than17/8. Hence\n\n exp(7/4)<(11/4)(17/8)=187/32<100/17,\n Phi(q_0)>exp(-7/4)>32/187>17/100.\n\nThis finishes (23), including the small indices handled separately.\nThe cancellation in (25) retains the additive1 in d_k and the actual\nepsilon dependence. Bounding the two product factors in (24)\nindependently would lose that cancellation.\n\n## 11. Scope and remaining work\n\nThe new proved-in-draft part of the stated interface is e_i>=0 for\nevery required index. The stronger entrywise result (7), the identities\n(9), (11), and (13), the uniform decimation estimates (17), and the\ntime localization and stable finite inverse (18)-(22), the row2 load\nbound (11b), and the uniform surviving-mass bound (23) have explicit\nproofs above.\n\nFor a fixed original m, the inherited-boundary identity only requires\ne_i>=0, u_i>=0 and b_i>=2/15 at 0<=i<=m-1, the stronger e_i>=1/15\nand u_i>=1/16 at the SINGLE index i=m-1, and the load bounds at\n2<=i<=m-1. The early formulas already handle b_0,b_1,b_2 and\nu_0,u_1,u_2; they are recorded in the preceding boundary draft and\nare not replaced here by a positive margin independent of epsilon.\nUniform claims at all i>=127 with fixed epsilon would be stronger\nthan the quantitative terminal bounds actually used at i=m-1.\n\nAfter (8) and (11b), the unresolved claims remain b_i>=2/15 and\nu_i>=0 for 3<=i<=m-1, the stronger e_(m-1)>=1/15 and\nu_(m-1)>=1/16, and the load bounds for 3<=i<=m-1.\nThis document does not prove the full\nkernel positive semidefinite, and it is not a counterexample to those\nremaining actual-word claims. Even their proof would only concern the\nspecified reciprocal-level temporal quotient, not arbitrary legal GD.\n\nThe existing same-multiset foil is still a useful warning: its first\nfew steps also satisfy h_i<s_i+2, and entrywise off-diagonal positivity\nalone does not distinguish its negative diagonal-margin behavior.\nThus (7) must not be promoted into a substitute for the original-order\nestimates.
