{"id": "d6d76de419f81e83", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T02:17:43.446792+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Complete joint critical-angular obstruction through the EXACT finite-m return boundary beta_R, including equality: beta_R tends2^(-1/4), so the old1/sqrt2 threshold was an estimate artifact. For every positive queried amplitude history of the stated common-prefix/full-support law with7/10<=beta<=beta_R, all anchors/values/free terminal completions obey T_path<8192R and V<=2048(3/5)^((n+1)/8)+2^29*m/(n+1)^2; hence exact B_n*V->0. Also gives a sharp legal two-ray local expansion above the boundary, and proves that this same triangle fails the next actual level2 bridge/leaf.", "evidence": "# A sharper original-step angular boundary, with a finite mass bound at equality\n\nStatus: complete joint proof candidate, **UNVERIFIED**. Ninth max4 launch,\nfirst round (cumulative20). No numerical evaluation, optimizer, installation,\nor fact submission is used. This document gives all new arguments together;\nearlier unverified common-prefix drafts are not correctness predecessors.\nThe accepted inputs are `1252b8823bb5a290` (original interpolation and maximal\nterminal completion) and `b98bb807d514e80d` (exact benchmark definitions and\nthe bound B_n<=1+4 n^(log_2 3)).\n\nThe old 1/sqrt(2) boundary is not the boundary of the original-step argument.\nRetaining the physical leaf preceding the level1 bridge changes it. Moreover,\nthe movement bound need not divide by its vanishing angular coefficient:\none-block look-ahead controls the missing mass at equality. These assertions\nare scoped to the explicit measure below. A separate two-ray construction\nshows the limiting sharper boundary is sharp for a local four-step return;\nthat construction is NOT a full-word construction for the positive mixture.\n\n## 1. Word, measure, normalization, and quantitative statement\n\nLet m>=1024 be an integer, and define\n\n    sigma=1+sqrt(2), delta=1-1/m, c_chr=sigma(1+1/m),\n    a_chr=delta/c_chr, N=2^m, n=N-1,\n    epsilon=1/N, eta=1/N^2, l=1+a_chr, k=1+delta.\n\nUse the ACTUAL word H_chr,0=() and\n\n    H_chr,j+1=(H_chr,j,1+a_chr*c_chr^j,H_chr,j), 0<=j<m.\n\nEvery subword keeps these enclosing-m parameters. Equivalently the original\nstep at index i<n is h_i=1+a_chr*c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)), where nu_2 is the exponent\nof2 in a positive integer. Set rho=1-epsilon/2 and gamma=9/10.\n\nFor a bit vector s=(s_0,...,s_(m-1)) define\n\n    chi_s(i)=(-1)^(sum_j s_j i_j),\n    B(s)=(-1)^s_0, C_j(s)=(-1)^(s_1+...+s_j), 1<=j<m,\n\nwhere i_j are the standard binary digits of the original index i. The map\ns -> (B,C_1,...,C_(m-1)) is bijective. Choose beta in(0,1), and set\nmu_+=-beta, mu_-=gamma. Conditional on B=b, define\n\n    A_b(c)=(1+mu_b)/2 * 1_(all c_j=1)\n                +(1-mu_b)/2 * 1_(all c_j=-1),\n    P_b(c)=product_(j=1)^(m-1) (1+mu_b c_j)/2,\n    w_s=(1+(1-epsilon)B(s))/2\n                 *[(1-eta)A_(B(s))(C(s))+eta P_(B(s))(C(s))].       (1)\n\nThese are positive weights summing to1. Define the following explicit\nfinite-m quantities BEFORE choosing beta:\n\n    lambda=rho(1-eta), omega=1-lambda, e=omega/lambda,\n    d_mass=(l+k)/(l+1+(1-l)e),\n    beta_M=sqrt((1-e)/d_mass)-e,\n    p=1-epsilon, d_eps=gamma epsilon/2,\n    r_leaf=[(2-l)p+sqrt((2-l)^2 p^2+4(l-1))]/2,\n    L_leaf=l/r_leaf,\n    beta_R=[k d_eps+sqrt((k+L_leaf)(1+L_leaf p)\n                                  +L_leaf^2 d_eps^2)]\n                          /[(k+L_leaf)rho].                       (2)\n\nThe candidate theorem covers EVERY\n\n    7/10 <= beta <= beta_R,                                      (3)\n\nincluding the endpoint beta=beta_R. In particular4/5<beta_R<6/7 and\nbeta_R tends to2^(-1/4), strictly beyond1/sqrt(2). The auxiliary beta_M\nis the zero of the simpler mass coefficient; the complete proof crosses\nit and reaches beta_R by retaining the actual final level2 bridge.\n\nPrescribe only QUERIED gradients\n\n    g_i=(Gamma_i sqrt(w_s) chi_s(i))_s, 0<=i<n,                    (4)\n\nwith arbitrary positive amplitudes Gamma_i. Suppose a finite-dimensional\nconvex differentiable1-smooth function F realizes (4) along its actual GD\ntrajectory X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i, and has a minimizer. The anchor, values,\namplitudes and unqueried terminal gradient are otherwise unrestricted.\nAll isometric realizations of the Gram matrix in(4) are included. Let\n\n    X_star be a true closest minimizer to X_0,\n    R=||X_0-X_star||, F_star=min F,\n    T_path=sum_(i<n)h_i Gamma_i,\n    V_chr=2(F(X_n)-F_star)/R^2.\n\nThen the claimed bounds, uniform over(3), are\n\n    R>0,  T_path<8192 R,\n    ||X_i-X_star||<512 R for every0<=i<=n,                        (5)\n    ||grad F(X_n)|| <= R[2(3/5)^(N/8)+3*2^17*m/N^2],              (6)\n    V_chr <=2048(3/5)^(N/8)+2^29*m/N^2.                          (7)\n\nThe comparison uses the exact original benchmark and its normalized\nobjective2(F(X_n)-F_star)/R^2, preserving the project's factor of2:\n\n    B_n V_chr <=(1+4n^(log_2 3))\n                       [2048(3/5)^((n+1)/8)+2^29*m/(n+1)^2]\n                   ->0,  n=2^m-1.                              (8)\n\nFor L-smooth f apply the theorem to F=(f-f_star)/L, so the normalized\nobjective is exactly2(f(X_n)-f_star)/(L R^2). No surrogate denominator\nor inverse-Gram anchor norm replaces the true closest-minimizer distance.\n\n## 2. Original inequalities and elementary finite bounds\n\nA closest minimizer exists because the nonempty minimizer set is closed,\nand a distance-minimizing sequence can be chosen bounded in finite dimension.\nThe positive initial gradient makes R>0. Translate X_star to0.\nAccepted `1252b8823bb5a290` gives all actual original inequalities\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0,\n    f_i=F(X_i)-F_star.\n\nThe optimum sample has position, gradient and value0. Adding orientations\ngives, for any two actual samples,\n\n    ||g_i-g_j||^2 <= <g_i-g_j,X_i-X_j>.                           (9)\n\nIn particular <g_i,X_i>>=||g_i||^2, and smoothness gives\n||g_i||<=||X_i||. A physical step0<h<=2 therefore satisfies\n\n    ||X-hg||^2<=||X||^2-h(2-h)||g||^2,\n    ||grad F(X-hg)||<=||g||.                                    (10)\n\nFor the second assertion put d_g=grad F(X-hg)-g in(9):\n-h<d_g,g>>=||d_g||^2. Expanding ||g+d_g||^2 gives the assertion.\nFor h>=2 the same optimum inequality gives instead\n\n    ||X-hg||^2<=||X||^2+h(h-2)||g||^2\n                  <=(h-1)^2||X||^2.                            (11)\n\nAll applications below distinguish these two regimes.\n\nThe enclosing parameters satisfy\n\n    7/5<l<3/2, 199/100<k<2,\n    1<delta*c_chr<5/2,\n    e<1/1000, 1/sqrt(lambda)<1001/1000,\n    sqrt(omega)/lambda<1/255, 1-epsilon>999/1000.                 (12)\n\nFor example sqrt2<99/70, c_chr<49/20 and delta>99/100 give l>7/5;\nc_chr>2 gives l<3/2. Also delta*c_chr=sigma(1-1/m^2)<5/2 and is>1.\nThe powers of2 give omega<=epsilon/2+eta<1/65536, which implies the\ndisplayed mixture bounds. These estimates require no numerical evaluation.\n\nBecause l>1 and e>=0,\n\n    d_mass>1+(99/100)/(5/2)=349/250>11/8,\n    d_mass<3/2.                                                (13)\n\nFor the second inequality, subtract l+2 from\n(3/2)[l+1-(l-1)e]; the result is (l-1)(1/2-3e/2)>0.\nThus\n\n    beta_M>sqrt(333/500)-1/1000>4/5,\n    beta_M<sqrt(8/11)<6/7.                                    (14)\n\nThe first last comparison follows from (801/1000)^2<333/500; the second\nfollows from392<396.\n\nWe also need a finite bound for beta_R BEFORE the mass proof. Define,\nfor a trial beta in[0,1],\n\n    z=-rho beta+d_eps, w=-rho beta-d_eps,\n    alpha_ret=1-k z^2+L_leaf(p-zw),\n    alpha_0=(l+1)-(l+k)beta^2.\n\nThe quadratic alpha_ret has negative leading coefficient, positive\nconstant term, and exactly one positive root, the beta_R in(2).\nThe polynomial in(25) below, evaluated at p and1, gives\np<=r_leaf<1 and therefore l<=L_leaf<=l+3epsilon. Direct substitution gives\nz^2>=beta^2-2epsilon and p-zw<=1-beta^2+epsilon. Consequently\n\n    alpha_ret<=alpha_0+9epsilon for0<=beta<=1.                 (14a)\n\nIndeed the extra upper bound is\n2k epsilon+3epsilon(1-beta^2)+l epsilon+3epsilon^2<9epsilon.\nAt beta=6/7 this gives\nalpha_ret< -157/2450+9epsilon<0, using l<3/2,k>199/100.\nAt beta=4/5, z^2<=beta^2,zw<=beta^2 and p-zw>0 give instead\nalpha_ret>=alpha_0-l epsilon>28/125-(3/2)epsilon>0.\nThus4/5<beta_R<6/7. For every beta in(3), alpha_ret>=0, and(14a) implies\n\n    (l+k)beta^2<=l+1+9epsilon.                               (14b)\n\nFinally epsilon,e->0, r_leaf->1,l->sqrt2,k->2. Both beta_M and beta_R\ntend to sqrt((1+sqrt2)/(2+sqrt2))=2^(-1/4).\n\n## 3. Movement without division by a vanishing angular margin\n\nSplit the SAME probability measure into the component B=1 with all prefix\nsigns common, of mass lambda, and its remaining component of mass omega.\nIn the first component the unit directions repeat a_0,a_0,b_0,b_0 and\n<a_0,b_0>=-beta. Hence the full gradients admit the direct-sum realization\n\n    g_i=Gamma_i(sqrt(lambda)u_i,sqrt(omega)z_i),\n    u_i in{a_0,b_0}, ||z_i||=1.                                (15)\n\nAn isometry on the gradient span, extended to the initial displacement,\npreserves the Gram data and true radius; thus this representation loses\nno actual realization. Write X_0=(x^c,x^r).\n\nAt a block start t=4r,0<=t<=N-4, let P_a and P_b be the total previous\namplitude-weighted physical step masses on a_0 and b_0, respectively.\nPut T=P_a+P_b and\n\n    A_x=<a_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)-P_a+beta P_b,\n    B_x=<b_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)-P_b+beta P_a,\n    r_0=sqrt(omega)R/lambda.\n\nThe remainder displacement before t has norm at most sqrt(omega)T.\nLet A=Gamma_t,C=Gamma_(t+1). The first physical leaf gives A>=C by(10).\nAt the QUERIED sample t+1, its optimum inequality yields\n\n    A_x >= l A+C/lambda-r_0-e(T+l A)\n         = l(1-e)A+(1+e)C-r_0-eT.                            (16)\n\nIndeed the remainder contribution to <g_(t+1),X_(t+1)>/C is at most\nsqrt(omega)[R+sqrt(omega)(T+l A)]. Division by lambda proves(16).\n\nThe mass of the first two physical steps is D_a=l A+k C. For0<=C/A<=1,\nthe function (l+k r)/[l(1-e)+(1+e)r] is increasing: its derivative has\nthe sign of k(1-e)-(1+e)>0 by(12). Its value at1 is d_mass. Consequently\n\n    D_a<=d_mass(A_x+r_0+eT).                                 (17)\n\nAt the QUERIED sample t+2, the coarse direction is b_0. Dropping its\npositive norm term from the optimum inequality gives\n\n    B_x+(beta+e)D_a+r_0+eT>=0.                               (18)\n\nSubstituting(17), then expanding A_x,B_x,T, gives EXACTLY\n\n    c_a P_a+c_b P_b\n       <=<b_0+d_mass(beta+e)a_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)\n                         +[1+d_mass(beta+e)]r_0,\n    c_a=(beta+e)[d_mass(1-e)-1],\n    c_b=1-e-d_mass(beta+e)^2.                                (19)\n\nThroughout(3),\n\n    c_a>(7/10)[(11/8)(999/1000)-1]>1/4.\n\nSince beta+e<1, the right side of(19) is<4R by(12),(13).\nWhen c_b>=0 this already gives P_a<16R at every block start, including\nequality c_b=0. To cover the entire interval(3), let\nxi=max(0,-c_b). Equation(14b), epsilon<=2e, and the denominator\nD_mass=l+1+(1-l)e>9/4 imply\n\n    xi <=[9epsilon+10e+3e^2]/D_mass<13e<13/1000.              (19a)\n\nFor this calculation, the exact numerator of\nd_mass(beta+e)^2-(1-e) is\n(l+k)beta^2-(l+1)+[2(l+k)beta+2l]e+(k+1)e^2;\nthe last two coefficients are<10 and<3, respectively.\n\nWe do not divide by c_b. First rearrange(18):\n\n    (1-e)P_b <=<b_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)\n                            +(beta+e)(P_a+D_a)+r_0.           (21)\n\nWrite A_rad=(1/sqrt(lambda)+sqrt(omega)/lambda)/(1-e)<2 and\nv_mass=(beta+e)/(1-e)<2. If the next block start exists, its a-mass is\nexactly P_a+D_a. Thus(21) reads P_b<=A_rad R+v_mass P_a(next).\n\nFor the LAST block start t_last=N-4, use its preceding block t_prev=N-8.\nThe bridge between them has ORIGINAL level2, H_2=1+delta*c_chr<7/2.\nWriting C=Gamma_(t_prev+1), the two following short steps k,l imply\nGamma_(t_prev+2),Gamma_(t_prev+3)<=C. The b-mass added between these\nblock starts is therefore at most(l+H_2)C. Since the a-mass added there\nis D_a=l Gamma_(t_prev)+k C, the added b-mass is at most\n[(l+H_2)/k]D_a<3D_a. It follows from(21) at t_prev that\n\n    P_b(last)<=2R+5P_a(last).                               (20a)\n\nIf c_b<0, apply(19) only at this last start and substitute(20a):\n\n    [c_a-5xi]P_a(last)<=(4+2xi)R<5R.\n\nBy(19a), c_a-5xi>1/4-65/1000>1/8. Hence P_a(last)<40R.\nIf c_b>=0, the earlier bound16R applies instead. In either case,\nthe nonnegative chronological a-mass is nondecreasing, so\n\n    P_a<64R at EVERY block start.                            (20)\n\nFor each t<=N-8, (21) and the bound at its next block start now give\nP_b<2R+2*64R=130R. Hence\n\n    T<194R and ||X_t||<195R at every block start t<=N-8.       (22)\n\nThe three following steps are l,k,l<2, so their samples also obey195R.\nThis covers every sample before the final block. From t=N-8 the next\nfourth step, at N-5, has ORIGINAL level2 and size1+delta*c_chr.\nEquation(11) bounds the norm after it by (delta*c_chr)195R<512R.\nThe final three short steps keep this bound. Thus all samples obey(5).\n\nThere are just seven physical steps left from N-8 to n. Their total\nstepsize is4l+2k+(1+delta*c_chr)<14. Every gradient there has norm<512R.\nTherefore\n\n    T_path<194R+14*512R<8192R,\n    K_path:=R+T_path<2^14 R.                                (23)\n\nThis mass bound is obtained BEFORE estimating any eta perturbation.\nIt remains uniform as c_b decreases through zero to the range(19a).\nNo factors1/c_b,\n1/(1-2beta^2),1/(1-beta),1/epsilon, or1/eta are concealed in(23).\n\n## 4. Exact finite-epsilon correlations and the physical-leaf threshold\n\nFor the return argument split(1) differently: the entire common-prefix\ncomponent has mass1-eta. Write its common prefix sign as V. Its four unit\ndirections, in the original order modulo4, are\n\n    a_e=1,a_o=B,b_e=V,b_o=BV.\n\nSet\n\n    p=E B=1-epsilon,\n    z=E V=-rho beta+gamma epsilon/2,\n    w=E(BV)=-rho beta-gamma epsilon/2.                        (24)\n\nThe correlations are <a_e,a_o>=<b_e,b_o>=p,\n<a_o,b_o>=<a_e,b_e>=z, and <a_e,b_o>=<a_o,b_e>=w.\nAll finite-epsilon terms are retained. The full measure has these same\nthree correlations whenever only B and one individual prefix sign occur.\n\nAt ANY original leaf with amplitude ratio r>0, (9) is exactly\n\n    r^2+(l-2)p r-(l-1)<=0.\n\nLet r_leaf be its unique positive root. Evaluation at p and1 gives\n\n    p<=r_leaf<1,\n    r_leaf=[(2-l)p+sqrt((2-l)^2 p^2+4(l-1))]/2.                (25)\n\nThe relevant original-step return coefficient is\n\n    alpha_ret=1-k z^2+(l/r_leaf)(p-zw).                       (26)\n\nTo state its finite-m zero exactly, put L_leaf=l/r_leaf and\nd_eps=gamma epsilon/2. Substitution in(26) gives\n\n    alpha_ret=1+L_leaf p-(k+L_leaf)rho^2 beta^2\n                   +2k rho d_eps beta+(L_leaf-k)d_eps^2.\n\nIts positive zero is\n\n    beta_R=[k d_eps+sqrt((k+L_leaf)(1+L_leaf p)\n                                  +L_leaf^2 d_eps^2)]\n                          /[(k+L_leaf)rho].                  (27)\n\nIn contrast, the old coefficient1-kz^2 has positive zero\n(1/sqrt(k)+d_eps)/rho. Replacing either by1/sqrt2 at finite m is\nincorrect. The limit of(27) is2^(-1/4), while the old zero tends1/sqrt2.\n\nFor(3), alpha_ret>=0 by its exact positive root(27). Also -z>=2/3, p>0, and\np-zw>=p-beta^2>0 by(12),(14). Also z^2<=beta^2, zw<=beta^2 and\nL_leaf>=l. To compare the auxiliary mass boundary with this return\nboundary, specialize temporarily to beta<=beta_M. Then\n\n    alpha_ret >=(l+1)-(l+k)beta^2-l epsilon\n       >=2l e-(l-1)e^2-l epsilon.                            (28)\n\nThe second inequality uses beta^2<=(1-e)/d_mass and the exact denominator\nin(2). Since omega>=epsilon/2, epsilon<=2e/(1+e); the last expression in\n(28) is at least e^2[2l/(1+e)-(l-1)]>0. In particular beta_M<=beta_R.\nThe two explicit finite thresholds need not coincide. The strengthened\nmass proof in section3 covers their gap, including beta=beta_R. Neither\nthreshold is asserted to be the full-word feasibility boundary beyond\nthe obstruction interval for positive epsilon and eta.\n\n## 5. Original return pairs, with no inverse-alpha loss\n\nFor any original i,j the full direction correlation differs from its\ncommon-prefix correlation by at most2eta. If endpoint amplitudes are A,B\nand the intervening total physical mass is T_ij, the resulting difference\nin the cocoercivity expression is at most\n\n    2eta(A+B)T_ij+4eta AB <=4eta K_path(A+B).                  (29)\n\nHere T_ij<=T_path<K_path and A,B<=K_path, which were proved first.\nLet e_0=4eta K_path.\n\nTake an original queried odd-b return j=4r+3 with j+4<n. Write its\nphysical steps and gradient amplitudes as\n\n    j: H on b_o with B_0,\n    j+1: l on a_e with A_0,\n    j+2: k on a_o with C_0,\n    j+3: l on b_e with E_0,\n    j+4: b_o with D_0.\n\nThe original high step H is arbitrary here. Put tau=z, bbar=-z>0,\nM=B_0+bbar C_0 and Delta=D_0-B_0. In the common component set\n\n    Z=-H B_0 b_o-l A_0 a_e,\n    X=<b_o,Z>, Y=<a_o-tau b_o,Z>=-l A_0(p-zw).\n\nThe ORIGINAL pair(j,j+2), with(29), gives\n\n    C_0^2+B_0^2-2tau B_0 C_0\n       <=(tau C_0-B_0)X-l A_0 C_0(p-zw)+e_0(C_0+B_0).       (30)\n\nIf Delta>0, the ORIGINAL pair(j,j+4) gives\n\n    X>=Delta+k C_0 tau+l E_0 p-e_0(D_0+B_0)/Delta.            (31)\n\nThe coefficient tau C_0-B_0=-M is negative. Substitution in(30), followed\nby A_0>=C_0/r_leaf from the ACTUAL leaf at j+1, gives\n\n    alpha_ret C_0^2+bbar(2-k)B_0 C_0+B_0^2\n                      +M Delta+M l E_0 p\n       <=e_0(C_0+B_0)+M e_0(D_0+B_0)/Delta.                 (32)\n\nThis is where the old proof discarded the nonnegative term\nl A_0 C_0(p-zw), losing the sharper coefficient.\n\nBecause bbar>=2/3, C_0+B_0<=(3/2)M. Suppose Delta>=2m e_0.\nThe right side of(32) is at most\n\n    (5/2)e_0 M+(1/m)B_0 M\n      <=(5/2)e_0 M+B_0^2+(bbar/m)B_0 C_0.\n\nThe left side is at least B_0^2+(bbar/m)B_0 C_0+M Delta, since\n2-k=1/m and alpha_ret>=0. Cancellation forces Delta<=(5/2)e_0,\ncontradicting m>=1024. Thus EVERY such original return satisfies\n\n    Gamma_(j+4)<=Gamma_j+d_amp,\n    d_amp=2^17*m*eta*R,                                     (33)\n\nby(23). This proof also works with alpha_ret=0. It retains the explicit\nfactor m=1/(2-k), rather than hiding it in a perturbation symbol.\nThere is no1/alpha_ret loss. For eta=0 the same calculation directly\nforbids Delta>0 whenever alpha_ret>=0 and the stated signs hold.\n\n## 6. Uniform contractions at actual low bridges\n\nFor any original bridge of level q>=1 and its following leaf, let their\nthree amplitudes be A,M,D and steps1+u,l. The relative character signs are\n1,B C_q,C_q. Therefore the FULL law gives correlations w,p,z, respectively,\nand the original endpoint-pair sum is\n\n    0>=-u A^2+D^2+(u-1)z A D+l M(pD-wA).                     (34)\n\nThe leaf is short, so M>=D. Also pD-wA>0. The positive ratio t=D/A\ntherefore satisfies\n\n    F_u(t):=(1+lp)t^2+[(u-1)z-lw]t-u<=0.                   (35)\n\nThis polynomial has one positive root. On(3), -w>=beta and -z<=beta,\nbecause beta<6/7<gamma. Also1+lp>239/100.\nAt level1, u=delta<1, so [(u-1)z-lw]>=l beta>49/50. Hence\n\n    F_delta(1/2)>239/400+49/100-1=35/400>0.\n\nEvery level1 compound gain is consequently<1/2. At level2,\n1<u=delta*c_chr<5/2, and\n\n    (u-1)z-lw >=beta(1+l-u)>-1/10.\n\nIt follows that F_u(6/5)>2054/2500>0, so every level2 compound gain\nis<6/5. A four-step return at level2 is exactly the concatenation of\nthese two compounds; its gain is therefore<theta=3/5. All these estimates\nare for the full measure and allow arbitrary adjacent slack.\n\n## 7. Actual last steps, free terminal, and the exact benchmark\n\nLet K=N/4. The queried odd-b amplitudes are\n\n    B_r=Gamma_(4r+3),0<=r<=K-2.\n\nThe omitted next odd-b sample is n, whose actual gradient is free.\nFor0<=r<=K-3 the return bridge has level2+nu_2(r+1); it is level2\nexactly when r is even. Thus(33),(35) give\n\n    B_(2s+1)<=theta B_(2s),\n    B_(2s+2)<=B_(2s+1)+d_amp\n\nwhenever all the displayed queried indices exist. The first three steps\nare l,k,l<2, so B_0<=R. Iterating for N/8-1 pairs yields\n\n    Gamma_(n-4)<=theta^(N/8-1)R+(5/2)d_amp.                    (36)\n\nThe last FOUR actual steps are, in order,\n\n    1+delta*c_chr, l, k, l.\n\nThe first two have queried endpoints n-4 and n-2, so their compound gain\nis<6/5. The last two short steps satisfy(10) for the realizing function,\nwithout any prescribed terminal direction. Thus\n\n    ||grad F(X_n)||<=(6/5)Gamma_(n-4)\n                      <=2theta^(N/8)R+3d_amp.                (37)\n\nConvexity, the true minimizer, and(5) give\nF(X_n)-F_star<=512R||grad F(X_n)||. This proves(6),(7), since\n3072*2^17=3*2^27<2^29.\nAccepted `b98bb807d514e80d` gives the exact-B_n comparison(8).\nThe second term tends to zero because m=log_2(n+1) and log_2 3<2;\nthe first decays geometrically in n.\n\nThe same conclusion applies to the maximal prefix interpolant in accepted\n`1252b8823bb5a290`: apply the proof to that actual global function and its\nown true closest minimizer. Every reduced-feasible terminal value is at\nmost its value at X_n. If the supplied optimum has distance at most1 from\nX_0, the true R is at most1 and(7) bounds twice the reduced terminal value\nas well. No incoming formal terminal inequality is imposed.\n\nEvery character-specific endpoint used above is queried: the movement\noptima are t+1,t+2<=n-1; return pairs end at j+4<=n-4; and the last\ncompound ends at n-2. The terminal norm step concerns the actual function.\n\n## 8. Nonvacuity for these same positive weights\n\nThis calibration is not a competitive lower bound. For the same actual\nword and weights, additionally prescribe the terminal character gradient.\nLet H_max=max_i h_i, kappa=min(1,(min_i h_i-1)/4)>0 and\n\n    q_decay=kappa/[8(H_max+kappa+1)], Gamma_i=q_decay^i,\n    f_i=(kappa+1/2)Gamma_i^2, 0<=i<=n.\n\nThe full gradient Gram G is positive definite: the full character matrix\nis invertible, as summing chi_s(i)chi_s(j) over s is N for i=j and0\notherwise, and every weight and amplitude is positive. Define\n\n    b_i=(kappa+1)Gamma_i^2+sum_(t<i)h_t G_ti,\n    X_0=sum_i(G^(-1)b)_i g_i,\n    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t.\n\nThen <X_i,g_i>=(kappa+1)Gamma_i^2. For i<j, direct substitution gives\n\n    Q_ij=(kappa+1-h_i)Gamma_i^2-kappa Gamma_j^2\n                            -sum_(i<t<j)h_t G_it-G_ij,\n    Q_ji=(kappa+1)Gamma_j^2-kappa Gamma_i^2\n                            +(h_i-1)G_ij+sum_(i<t<j)h_t G_jt.\n\nUsing |G_it|<=Gamma_i Gamma_t,q_decay<1/2,h_i-1>=4kappa,\nthe first divided by Gamma_i^2 is at most\n-3kappa+2(H_max+1)q_decay<=-11kappa/4. The second is at most\n-kappa+(2H_max+kappa+1)q_decay<=-3kappa/4.\nThe optimum residuals are Q_(i,star)=0 and Q_(star,i)=-kappa Gamma_i^2.\nThis checks ALL ordered pairs with the optimum and terminal.\n\nAn explicit global function is\n\n    C_poly=conv(0,g_0,...,g_n),\n    F_poly(X)=max_(v in C_poly)[<X,v>-||v||^2/2].\n\nIts maximizer is the Euclidean projection P_(C_poly)(X). The projection\nnormal condition is <X-P(X),v-P(X)><=0 for v in C_poly. Adding this\ncondition at two positions shows ||P(X)-P(Y)||<=||X-Y||. Comparing the\ntwo maximizing expressions bounds the first-order remainder between0\nand||X-Y||^2, so F_poly is convex differentiable1-smooth with gradient P.\nAt X_i the displayed Q inequalities are precisely the projection normal\nconditions at each vertex (and the optimum). Thus the function realizes\nevery prescribed jet and the entire original recursion.\nIts minimizer set is the polar cone {Z:<Z,g_i><=0 for all i}. The true\ndistance to that set is positive and is the R used in the theorem; no\nidentity with||X_0|| or with an inverse-Gram expression is asserted.\n\n## 9. Sharp local scope test beyond the limiting boundary\n\nThe following exact construction tests a proposed extension of return\nnonexpansion to all beta<1. It is for the two-ray boundary epsilon=eta=0,\nand uses a whole global function on its own four-step segment.\nIt is not a realization of the finite positive mixture over H_chr,m.\n\nFix1<l<2,1<k<2, unit vectors a,b with<a,b>=-beta,0<beta<1, and set\n\n    t=l+1, s=l+k, E_ang=s beta^2-t.\n\nSuppose E_ang>0. For a positive number J with J E_ang>t^2, set\n\n    H=1+J, A=beta J/t, D=J E_ang/t^2>1,\n    C_triangle=conv(0,Aa,Db),\n    F_triangle(X)=max_(v in C_triangle)[<X,v>-||v||^2/2].       (38)\n\nBy the argument in section8 this is one convex differentiable1-smooth\nfunction. Start at X_0=b and take the ACTUAL four steps H,l,k,l. The\nclaimed gradients and positions are\n\n    g_0=b, g_1=Aa, g_2=Aa, g_3=Db, g_4=Db,\n    X_1=-Jb, X_2=-Jb-lAa,\n    X_3=-Jb-sAa, X_4=-(J+lD)b-sAa.                            (39)\n\nHere are all required vertex normal checks, which imply every original\nordered interpolation inequality through the same global function.\nAt X_0, b belongs to the segment[0,Db], so its projection is itself.\nAt X_1 the residual X_1-Aa has a-projection lA>0 and b-projection\n-J+beta A<0. Its products with -Aa and Db-Aa are therefore nonpositive.\nAt X_2 the residual X_2-Aa has a-projection0 and b-projection\n-J(1-beta^2)<0; both vertex normals again hold.\nAt X_3 the residual X_3-Db has b-projection lD>0 and a-projection\nbeta D-(k-1)A<0. The latter follows from\n\n    D/A=E_ang/(beta t)<(k-1)beta/t.\n\nBoth products with -Db and Aa-Db are negative. At X_4 the residual\nX_4-Db has b-projection0 and a-projection\n\n    t beta D-(k-1)A=-s(1-beta^2)A<0.\n\nThe normals hold at all vertices and their convex hull, proving(39).\n\nThe minimizers form the polar cone of C_triangle. For any minimizer Z,\n<b,Z><=0; hence||b-Z||^2>=1, with equality at0. Thus the TRUE initial\ndistance is1. Since <X_4,b>=D, the terminal value is D^2/2 and the\nnormalized objective is D^2>1. In particular the b-return amplitude\nstrictly increases from1 toD.\n\nFor the actual enclosing-m values l,k, the local condition is exactly\n\n    beta^2>(l+1)/(l+k).                                     (40)\n\nAn original level q>=2 bridge has J=a_chr*c_chr^q and is followed by\nl,k,l. Thus the four physical steps coincide with such an actual segment\nwhenever q<m and a_chr*c_chr^q*E_ang>(l+1)^2. This exposes the entire\nangular-gap cost J>(l+1)^2/E_ang. It does not assume that the segment can\nbe spliced into the preceding or following H_chr,m trajectory.\n\nConversely at epsilon=eta=0, (25) has r_leaf=1 and (26) becomes\nalpha_ret=(l+1)-(l+k)beta^2. Equation(32), with zero error, forbids ANY\npositive return growth when alpha_ret>=0. Therefore(40) is the exact\nboundary for the existence of an expanding two-ray four-step return\nwith arbitrarily selectable high step H. This establishes local\nsharpness, not a finite-mixture or full-word threshold.\n\n### The explicit expanding triangle cannot pass the next actual leaf\n\nThere is a concrete compatibility failure for the SAME function(38),\nnot merely an absent proof of concatenation. Suppose its expanding\nsegment starts at an actual bridge of level q>=3. The next bridge,\nfour indices later, necessarily has level2; write H_2=1+delta*c_chr.\nStarting from X_4 in(39), its next position is\n\n    X_5=X_4-H_2 Db=-(J+(l+H_2)D)b-sAa,\n    <X_5,a>/A=-(k-1)+(l+H_2)E_ang/t.\n\nSince beta<1, E_ang<s-t=k-1=delta. Hence\n\n    <X_5,a>/A <delta(H_2-1)/t\n                       <(5/2)/(12/5)=25/24<l.               (41)\n\nFor the original prescribed ray pattern, the next two gradients must\nboth lie on the positive a ray. If g_5=C a with0<C<A, projection onto\nC_triangle and the two vertices0,Aa force <X_5,a>=C. The next actual\nleaf gives <X_6,a>=(1-l)C<0. If g_5=Aa instead, (41) gives\n<X_6,a>=<X_5,a>-lA<0. No positive a-ray projection is possible at X_6:\nits normal with the zero vertex would require <X_6,a>>=||g_6||>0.\nIf g_5 is not on that ray, the prescribed pattern has already failed.\n\nAn original level2 choice cannot satisfy J E_ang>t^2 in(38), since\nJ=delta*c_chr<5/2,E_ang<delta<1 and t^2>(12/5)^2. Thus ANY expanding\nsegment of(38) embedded at an actual bridge has q>=3, and the above\ntwo queried samples actually exist in the following block. Therefore\nthis explicit sharp local constructor cannot continue through the next\nlevel2 bridge and leaf for ANY beta<1. It is not a candidate full-word\nadversary. A different function, changing its same-ray gradient/value\ngeometry, would have to repair precisely this next-leaf compatibility.\n\n## 10. Remaining compatibility condition and budget scope\n\nThe interval[7/10,beta_R] includes a fixed supercritical neighborhood of\nthe OLD1/sqrt2 threshold, and the explicit law beta_m=beta_R is the exact\nfinite-m zero of the original-step return coefficient. It tends\nto the sharper2^(-1/4) boundary. Equations(5)--(8) exclude all its positive\namplitude histories, all anchors and values, and all terminal completions.\n\nThere is no full-word counterexample at beta>beta_R here. The concrete\nremaining issue is to reconcile expanding high returns beyond(26) with\none function's earlier and later ray data, the alternating actual level2\nreturns, and a true-distance payoff surviving the exact B_n comparison.\nThe triangle in(38) uses one fixed cap on each ray and fails the explicit\nnext-leaf compatibility(41). UNVERIFIED, no fact; next31-step coupling/all-depth rule and original target remain open.", "evidence": "# An exact full coupling for the changed fifteen-step instance\n\nStatus: **UNVERIFIED self-contained joint proof candidate**, max round21.\nThis answers the finite coupling problem in the refreshed TASK associated\nwith guidance04cc04960aa1755d. It is not a verifier-accepted fact and does\nnot establish the original all-schedule asymptotic target.\n\nThe specific changed seed from finding1d4af67cf6124842, its actual\nseven-step child, its word, its values, and its optimum are retained.\nThat finding is motivation, not a correctness premise: the data and\nrealizing-function argument are supplied again here. The only cited\naccepted result is fact1252b8823bb5a290, for the original Q convention\nand its necessity.\n\nThe new numerical option is closed: three optimizer evaluations and one\nconservatively charged exact fixed-candidate replay used all four slots\nand19.17568400500022 measured seconds. No verifier was called. All source,\nraw arrays and original statuses are public. The exact rational coupling\nand the arithmetic certificate below are independent of solver status.\n\n## 1. The exact unchanged seed and every seed interpolation row\n\nLet\n\n    s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1,\n    a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300,\n    rho=599/600, eps=1/100, E=1/10000,\n    Klen=a+rho, z=b-1, m=a+b, eta=z/Klen,\n    A=1+2a+b.\n\nIn Euclidean R^3 with its displayed coordinate order define\n\n    U=(1,0,0), V=(0,sqrt(eta),0), W=(0,rho*sqrt(eta),eps),\n    g_0=g_1=U, g_2=V, g_3=W,\n    x_3=W, x_2=W+aV, x_1=W+aV+bU,\n    p=x_0=W+aV+(a+b)U.                                  (1)\n\nThe GD word is exactly h[0]=(a,b,a). Its residuals y_i=x_i-g_i are\n\n    y_0=(m-1)U+Klen*V+(0,0,eps),\n    y_1=zU+Klen*V+(0,0,eps),\n    y_2=(Klen-1)V+(0,0,eps), y_3=0.                      (2)\n\nLet e_i=<g_i,y_i>. The four energies are\n\n    e_0=m-1, e_1=z, e_2=(Klen-1)eta, e_3=0.\n\nHere is the complete list of vertex normals <g-g_i,y_i>:\n\n* At i=0, the vertices g=0,V,W give -(m-1), -a,\n  and -a-(1-rho)z+E respectively; g=U gives0.\n* At i=1, the vertices0,V,W give -z,0,-(1-rho)z+E;\n  g=U gives0.\n* At i=2, the vertices0,U both give -(Klen-1)eta,\n  g=W gives -(1-rho)(Klen-1)eta+E, and g=V gives0.\n* At i=3 all vertex normals are0.\n\nThe elementary bounds7/5<s<10/7 imply\n1.39<a<1.43, 2<b<2.01, 2.38<Klen<2.43, z>1, eta>2/5.\nConsequently(1-rho)z>1/600>E and\n(1-rho)(Klen-1)eta>1/1500>E. Every displayed normal is nonpositive.\n\nFor any finite data with these normal conditions, set\n\n    K=conv(0,g_0,...,g_n),\n    F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),\n    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.                           (3)\n\nCompleting the square identifies the unique maximizer in(3) as the\nEuclidean projection P_K(x). The two projection inequalities, added,\ngive ||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2<=<x-x',P_K(x)-P_K(x')>.\nThus P_K is1-Lipschitz. Evaluating the defining maxima at each other's\nmaximizers bounds the differentiability remainder between0 and\n||x'-x||^2. Hence F_K is finite convex differentiable and1-smooth,\nwith gradient P_K. The displayed vertex normals give P_K(x_i)=g_i.\n\nExpansion of the original residual in accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 gives\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<g_i-g_j,y_j>.\n\nThe optimum rows are Q_(i,star)=0 and Q_(star,i)=-e_i<=0.\nThus the normal list checks ALL original seed rows.\n\nThe minimizer set is K^polar={v:<v,g><=0 for every g in K}.\nSufficiency follows from(3). For necessity test tg in(3), divide the\nresulting inequality by t>0 and let t decrease to0. The positive cone\nidentity\n\n    p=mU+aV+W                                             (4)\n\ngives <p,v><=0 on K^polar. Therefore0 is a closest minimizer and its\ntrue squared distance is\n\n    R^2=||p||^2=m^2+Klen*z+E,\n    N=||W||^2=rho^2*eta+E.                               (5)\n\nThis same retained cone identity will prove true distance after both\njoins. No change of optimum or chosen normalization is made.\n\nThe seed has positive gradient-energy surplus kappa=A^2*N/R^2>1+1/2000.\nHere is a complete exact sign certificate. Put\nRbar^2=m^2+Klen*z and Nbar=rho^2*eta. Substitution and s^2=2 yield\n\n    A^2*Nbar/Rbar^2-1=(-P0+Q0*s)/D0,\n    P0=19019047380837966731,\n    Q0=13474265781546157318,\n    D0=40842989603870102500.\n\nAll are positive and\n\n    2(2000Q0)^2-(2000P0+D0)^2\n      =2441206548123238434623351342332719331750000>0.\n\nSquaring positive numbers therefore proves the claimed bound for the\nbarred ratio. Also Rbar^2>Nbar, since\nRbar^2-Nbar=m^2+eta*(Klen^2-rho^2)>0. Adding E to numerator and denominator\nraises the ratio Nbar/Rbar^2, proving the displayed bound for kappa.\n\n## 2. Fully specified frames and the actual seven-step child\n\nFor a nonzero column vector t in R^d whose unit vector is not e_1, define\n\n    u_t=t/||t||, v_t=e_1-u_t,\n    B(t)=I-2*v_t*v_t^T/(v_t^T*v_t), T_t(q)=B(t)q.         (6)\n\nB(t) is a real symmetric orthogonal matrix, with first column u_t.\nThe first coordinate of T_t(q) is <q,t>/||t||. All the axes used below\nhave u_t!=e_1. This also follows from the displayed seed data and the\ninterval enclosures in section4, so there is no zero denominator.\n\nWrite T_W(q)=(ell_0,ell_1,ell_2) and\nT_p(q)=(c_0,c_1,c_2). In R^5 define\n\n    L0(q)=(ell_0,ell_1,ell_2,0,0),\n    R0(q)=(-c_0,0,0,c_1,c_2),\n    r_0=theta*A, alpha_0=r_0*||W||/R.                   (7)\n\nBoth L0 and R0 are actual isometries, and\n\n    <L0(u),alpha_0*R0(v)>\n         =-r_0*<u,W><p,v>/R^2.                          (8)\n\nThe seven-step child has, for0<=i<=3,\n\n    X_i=L0(x_i), G_i=L0(g_i), Y_i=L0(y_i), F_i=f_i,\n    X_(4+i)=alpha_0*R0(x_i), G_(4+i)=alpha_0*R0(g_i),\n    Y_(4+i)=alpha_0*R0(y_i), F_(4+i)=alpha_0^2*f_i.\n\nIts word and capacity are\n\n    h[1]=(a,b,a,1+r_0,a,b,a), A_1=lambda*A.\n\nThe bridge is exact: alpha_0*R0(p)=-r_0*L0(W), and X_3=G_3=L0(W).\nAll four seed lists <g_i,W>,<y_i,W>,<p,g_i>,<p,y_i> are nonnegative:\nthe coordinates in(1)-(2) have nonnegative entries. Formula(8) therefore\nmakes each cross inner product between a gradient and a residual\nnonpositive. Subtracting the nonnegative receiving normal energy gives\nevery cross Q<=0. Within-child and optimum rows follow from section1\nand scaling. Formula(3), now applied to all eight G_i, gives one actual\nseven-step function with the prescribed values.\n\nIts initial point P=X_0 has squared norm R^2 and remains in the positive\ncone of its retained first-seed gradients by(4). Hence R is still its\ntrue closest-minimizer distance. Its endpoint is\n\n    w=G_7=X_7=alpha_0*R0(W),\n    N_1=||w||^2=alpha_0^2*N,\n    E_i=<G_i,Y_i>=e_i for i<4,\n    E_(4+i)=alpha_0^2*e_i for i<4.                       (9)\n\nThis fixes all internal positions, gradients, values and indices of the\nchildren in the mandated fifteen-step problem.\n\n## 3. A rational full transverse block and a real nine-dimensional parent\n\nIn the exact coordinate order(7), use the two five-dimensional frames\nB(w) and B(P) from(6). For a child vector q write\n\n    B(w)q=(q_l0,q_lT), B(P)q=(q_r0,q_rT),\n\nwhere the tails are columns in R^4. Fix the rational matrix\n\n    C=(1/100)*[[7,0,7,0], [1,0,0,0],\n              [18,-1,-10,0], [-17,0,-11,0]].             (10)\n\nIts squared Frobenius norm is934/10000<1. Thus ||C||_op<1, and\nH=(I-C^T*C)^(1/2) is a well-defined real positive-definite matrix.\nNo eigenvalue clipping, numerical rank choice, or PSD repair is involved.\nIn R^9=R direct-sum R^4 direct-sum R^4 define\n\n    L(q)=(q_l0,q_lT,0),\n    Rmap(q)=(-q_r0,C*q_rT,H*q_rT).                       (11)\n\nThey are isometries because C^T*C+H^2=I. This explicitly realizes every\ncross inner product; there is no unspecified Gram completion.\n\nThe next bridge and scale are\n\n    r_1=theta*A_1=A, alpha_1=r_1*||w||/R,\n    h[2]=(h[1],1+r_1,h[1]), A_2=lambda^2*A.               (12)\n\nFor0<=i<=7, the parent data are\n\n    (x'_i,g'_i,y'_i,f'_i)=(L X_i,L G_i,L Y_i,F_i),\n    (x'_(8+i),g'_(8+i),y'_(8+i),f'_(8+i))\n       =(alpha_1 Rmap X_i,alpha_1 Rmap G_i,\n         alpha_1 Rmap Y_i,alpha_1^2 F_i).                (13)\n\nThe bridge is exact since alpha_1 Rmap(P)=-r_1 L(w) and X_7=G_7=w.\nAll within-child GD updates and values are preserved. Both children have\nthe same prescribed optimum0. Every future claim about this parent uses\nthe genuine last gradient g'_15; it is not deleted or freely reset.\n\nDefine the exact bilinear form\n\n    B_C(u,v)=alpha_1[-u_l0*v_r0+u_lT^T*C*v_rT].          (14)\n\nThe full cross normal conditions are exactly\n\n    Q'_(i,8+j)=B_C(G_i,Y_j)-alpha_1^2*E_j<=0,\n    Q'_(8+i,j)=B_C(Y_j,G_i)-E_j<=0                       (15)\n\nfor every0<=i,j<=7. Section4 proves every one. All internal rows are\nunchanged or scaled copies of section2; all optimum rows are0 or minus\nthe corresponding nonnegative normal energy. Applying(3) to all16 g'_i\ntherefore gives one actual finite convex1-smooth F:R^9->R realizing the\nwhole supplied fifteen-step trajectory and values.\n\nThe retained identity(4), through the two left isometries, proves that\n0 is a closest minimizer of this parent as well. Its true radius is R.\nThe endpoint residual is0 and its normalized terminal objective is\n\n    2F(x'_15)/dist(x'_0,argmin F)^2\n        =alpha_1^2*alpha_0^2*N/R^2\n        =kappa^4/A_2^2.                                 (16)\n\nThus the whole-run value W_15(h[2]) is at least the explicit quantity(16).\nThe core surplus satisfies A_2^2 times(16)>1+1/500, by kappa>1+1/2000\nand the binomial formula. This does not identify A_2^2 with the benchmark\nB_15; the distinction is essential to the target scope.\n\n## 4. The finite certificate\nbelow was produced by the four explicitly authorized benchmark runs; it is\nan exact integer enclosure computation, not a floating-point extrapolation.\nIts source, every finite DP row, base-test slacks, and source hashes are public.\nThe proof still requires independent mathematical/verifier acceptance.\n\nSeventh max3 launch, first round, cumulative round 16. No seed optimization, installation, or fact_submit was performed.\n\n## 1. Definitions and exact scope\n\nUse the exact recurrences in accepted facts 0be8b54e0a33381c and\nb98bb807d514e80d. Set\n\n    P_n = 1+S_circle(n),  B_n = 1+2S_bullet(n),  P_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(a,b) = (a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,\n    G(a,d) = 2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2,\n    P_n = max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(P_k,P_l),\n    B_n = max_(k+l=n-1) G(P_k,B_l).\n\nAll maxima run over nonnegative integer k,l, with EVERY original split\nretained. Write lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda), s=sqrt(lambda), and\nq_n=sqrt(B_n). In this document q_n is a square-root mass, not an objective\ncoefficient. To discuss leaf counts, also write U_N=P_(N-1) and V_N=B_(N-1)\nfor N>=1, and U_0=0. The source paper's W_N equals V_N, not the project's\nworst-case functional W_n(h).\n\nThe target scalar claim is\n\n    Z_b = lim_(j->infinity) lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1),\n    Z_15 < (773/100) Z_3.                                  (E5)\n\nThe proof below derives existence of these limits as well as their enclosure.\nIt does not need the source's qualitative objective phase theorem.\n\nThe stronger conjecture B_(2n)<=lambda B_n for all n is NOT used. The\nargument allows a rigorously bounded positive defect beyond the finite base.\nThe source's global one-pivot support envelope and the zero-prefix loophole\nin C75 are not used.\n\n## 2. Precisely matched literature input\n\nThe only non-elementary external input is balanced optimality of the\nSYMMETRIC scalar recurrence, as proved in Corollary 4.2 of:\n\nYu Liu, Kang Chen, Rujun Jiang, Tianyu Wang (2026), *Optimal Recursive\nComposition and Dyadic Phase Laws for Gradient Descent with Predetermined\nStepsizes*, arXiv:2609.11788v1, https://arxiv.org/html/2609.11788v1 .\n\nIts complete statement used here is: for U_0=0, U_1=1 and\nU_N=max_(1<=m<N) Omega(U_m,U_(N-m)), the sequence is increasing and\n\n    U_(2a)=lambda U_a,\n    U_(2a+1)=Omega(U_a,U_(a+1)),  a>=0.                    (2.1)\n\nThe second identity includes U_1=Omega(U_0,U_1). The source defines its\nkernel K by exactly our Omega. Proposition 2.5 and identical initial values\nmatch its U_N to P_(N-1), by induction. No assertion about equality of ambient\nprimitive and s-composable certificate classes is imported. In particular,\nbalanced splitting is NEVER assumed for the asymmetric B recurrence.\n\nThe exact paper was downloaded in the prior round; its local text is\ndownloads/liu_chen_jiang_wang_2609.11788v1.txt. Section 2, Theorem 3.1 and\nits proof, Theorem 4.1/Corollary 4.2 and their proofs, and the adjacent-ratio\nproof in Appendix C.2 were read. Matlas returned unrelated statements;\nthis reference is grounded directly in the primary arXiv paper, not falsely\nrepresented as a Matlas hit. No external_refs submission is made because no\nfact_submit is authorized. The subsequent scalar estimates are proved here.\n\n## 3. Elementary growth and adjacent-value bounds\n\nFirst, 1<rho<4/3: lambda>2 and lambda^3=7+5sqrt(2)<16. Thus s>3/2\nand lambda^(3/2)<4.\n\nFor completeness, Omega(x,y)<=(x^(1/rho)+y^(1/rho))^rho for x,y>=0.\nFor positive x,y, put A=x^(1/rho), C=y^(1/rho), and T=(A+C)^rho.\nLet D(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. For g(z)=log D(exp z), differentiation gives\n\n    g''(z) = rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-2)\n             *((1+t)^rho-1-rho*t)/D(t)^2 > 0,  t=exp z.\n\nThe last bracket has value zero at t=0 and strictly positive derivative for\nt>0. Hence D(t)D(1/t)>=D(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. It follows that\n(T-x)(T-y)>=2xy, or T^2-(x+y)T-xy>=0. Since T>=x+y and Omega is the\npositive root of that polynomial, T>=Omega(x,y). Zero arguments follow by\ncontinuity. Induction in the full primitive recurrence gives U_N<=N^rho.\nBalanced dyadic joins give U_(2^j)=lambda^j; monotonicity then yields\n\n    N^rho/lambda <= U_N <= N^rho,  N>=1.                  (3.1)\n\nAlso G(a,d)>=Omega(a,d) for a,d>=0. Indeed, after multiplying their\ndifference by two, the required inequality is\n3a+sqrt(d^2+8ad)>=sqrt(a^2+6ad+d^2); squaring the nonnegative sides\nleaves 8a^2+2ad+6a sqrt(d^2+8ad)>=0. Induction gives V_N>=U_N.\n\nHere is the adjacent primitive estimate derived from (2.1). Let\nR_N=U_(N+1)/U_N, Delta(r)=sqrt(r^2+6r+1), and\n\n    T0(r)=(1+r+Delta(r))/(2lambda),\n    T1(r)=lambda*(Delta(r)-r-1)/2.\n\nThen R_(2N)=T0(R_N), R_(2N+1)=T1(R_N). Both maps fix 1 and have positive\nderivative at most 1/2 on [1,lambda]: their derivatives are\n(1+(r+3)/Delta(r))/(2lambda) and lambda*((r+3)/Delta(r)-1)/2;\n(r+3)/Delta(r) has derivative -8/Delta(r)^3. Starting at R_1=lambda,\nbinary induction gives\n\n    0<R_N-1 <= (lambda-1)/2^floor(log_2 N) <= 2sqrt(2)/N.\n\nUsing (3.1), U_(N+1)-U_N<=2sqrt(2) N^(rho-1). With U_0=0 this implies\nU_m-U_(m-1)<=2sqrt(2) m^(rho-1) for every m>=1.\n\nThe full dominant recurrence now gives\n\n    0 < V_(N+1)-V_N <= 8sqrt(2) N^(rho-1).                (3.2)\n\nFor the upper bound, take a maximizing prefix m for V_(N+1). If m>=2,\ncompare it to the admissible split (m-1,N+1-m) for V_N. If m=1, use\nG(U_0,V_N)=V_N. The derivative of G with respect to its first variable is\n2+2d/sqrt(d^2+8ad)<=4. Since m<=N, the bound follows. Strict increase\nfollows by choosing prefix 1 in the recurrence.\n\nConsequently, with Delta_n=q_(n+1)-q_n and barq_n=(q_n+q_(n+1))/2,\n\n    Delta_n <= 4sqrt(2lambda) (n+1)^(rho/2-1),\n    delta_n := Delta_n^2/(4barq_n)\n             <= 8lambda^(3/2) (n+1)^(rho/2-2)\n             < 32 n^(-4/3),  n>=1.                       (3.3)\n\nThe denominator bound used here is q_n>=sqrt(U_(n+1))\n>=(n+1)^(rho/2)/sqrt(lambda). No concavity of q_n is asserted.\n\n## 4. Exact merge inequality and contraction along maximizing trees\n\nLet a,b,d>0, write Q=sqrt(d), u=T_b(Q), t=T_a(u), where\n\n    T_a(v)=(v+sqrt(v^2+8a))/2,  T_a(v)^2=G(a,v^2).\n\nIf a<=d, then\n\n    G(a,G(b,d)) < G(Omega(a,b),d).                        (4.1)\n\nTo prove this, u>Q and Q(u+Q)>2Q^2>=2a. Since t is the positive\nsolution of t^2-ut=2a, this implies t<u+Q. Set r=t/u and c=a+br.\nThe polynomial defining Omega gives\n\n    c^2-(a+b)c-ab = ab*(t-u-Q)/u < 0.\n\nHere r(r-1)=2a/u^2 and 2b/u^2=1-Q/u were substituted to obtain the\nidentity. Thus c<Omega(a,b). Finally,\nt^2-Qt=2a+(u-Q)t=2(a+bt/u)=2c<2Omega(a,b), so T_Omega(a,b)(Q)>t.\nSquaring proves (4.1).\n\nConsider two successive right-continuation joins in ANY maximizing B tree.\nLet their primitive leaf counts be m1,m2 and the remaining leaf count be R.\nTheir values are a=U_m1, b=U_m2, d=V_R. The same total leaf count permits\nmerging the two primitive blocks: Omega(a,b)<=U_(m1+m2). Therefore (4.1)\nwould strictly improve the maximizing tree if a<=d. Necessarily a>d.\nBy (3.1) and V_R>=U_R,\n\n    m1^rho >= a > d >= R^rho/lambda,\n    R < 2m1,\n    R < (2/3)(m1+m2+R).                                  (4.2)\n\nThus the remaining leaf count decreases by a factor less than 2/3 every\nTWO edges along a maximizing right spine. This is an optimality argument\nfor the exact full DP, not a restriction imposed on allowed splits.\n\n## 5. A one-step rounding defect inequality\n\nExtend P_-1=0. Let v(x) be linear interpolation of q_n on nonnegative\nintegers: v(n+theta)=(1-theta)q_n+theta q_(n+1), 0<=theta<=1. Define\n\n    e_t = max(0, q_t/s - v(t/2)),  t>=0.                  (5.1)\n\nFor a maximizing split k+l=t-1, the claim is\n\n    e_t <= e_l                              if t is even,\n    e_t <= e_l+delta_floor(t/2)              if t is odd. (5.2)\n\nOnly t>4095 will require the odd formula, so delta_0 need not be bounded.\n\nThe primitive bounds from (2.1) are\n\n    P_(2a+1)/lambda=P_a,\n    P_(2a)/lambda<= (P_(a-1)+P_a)/2.                      (5.3)\n\nFor a>=1 the second follows from\nOmega(x,y)<=lambda(x+y)/2, obtained using 4xy<=(x+y)^2. For a=0,\nP_0/lambda<=1/2=(P_-1+P_0)/2 because lambda>2.\n\nThe exact dominant split inequality can be written without a radical:\n\n    2P_i+q_j q_m <= q_m^2,  i+j=m-1.                    (5.4)\n\nIt is equivalent to T_(P_i)(q_j)<=q_m. We additionally permit the formal\nboundary i=-1,j=m, where (5.4) is equality.\n\nIf t=2n and k=2a+1,l=2b, then a+b=n-1. Use (5.4) directly. If\nk=2a,l=2b+1, then a+b=n-1; average (5.4) at the two splits\n(a-1,b+1) and (a,b), both with parent n. Using (5.3) gives a root at\nmost q_n in either case before adding the continuation defect e_l.\n\nFor t=2n+1, set Qbar=(q_n+q_(n+1))/2. If k=2a+1,l=2b+1,\nthen a+b=n-1. Average the inequalities with parent/split\n(n;a,b) and (n+1;a,b+1). For u=P_a and w=(q_b+q_(b+1))/2, this gives\n\n    2u+Qbar*w-Qbar^2 <= (Delta_n^2-Delta_n*Delta_b)/4\n                       <= Delta_n^2/4.\n\nIf k=2a,l=2b, then a+b=n. Average parent/split\n(n;a-1,b) and (n+1;a,b). With u=(P_(a-1)+P_a)/2 and w=q_b,\nthe corresponding upper bound is Delta_n^2/4. Boundary a=0 uses the\nformal equality in (5.4). In both cases w<=Qbar. The derivative of\ny^2-wy-2u for y>=Qbar is at least Qbar. Its positive root is therefore\nat most Qbar+Delta_n^2/(4Qbar)=Qbar+delta_n.\n\nFinally, T_a is increasing and 1-Lipschitz in its nonnegative argument:\nT_a'(v)=(1+v/sqrt(v^2+8a))/2<=1. Homogeneity and\nq_l/s<=v(l/2)+e_l imply\n\n    q_t/s = T_(P_k/lambda)(q_l/s)\n           <= T_(P_k/lambda)(v(l/2))+e_l.\n\nCombining this with the preceding parity cases proves (5.2).\n\n## 6. The exact finite base and the resulting uniform error\n\nThe saved interval calculation checks the following FINITE certificate:\n\n    e_t=0 for every integer 0<=t<=4095.                  (6.1)\n\nThe integer tests and reproducibility information are given in section 8.\nAssuming this explicit finite certificate, follow a maximizing right spine\nin (5.2) until its index is at most 4095. For visited indices t_i>4095,\nput N_i=t_i+1 and n_i=floor(t_i/2)>=2048. By (4.2), whenever i and i+2\nare both visited, N_i>(3/2)N_(i+2). Consequently\n\n    n_i >= (N_i-2)/2 > (3/4)N_(i+2)-1\n        >= (3/2)n_(i+2)-1/4 > (4/3)n_(i+2).\n\nThe last step uses n_(i+2)>=2048. Split the visited nodes into the two\nparities of their spine position. Applying (3.3) backward from the last\nnode of each parity yields\n\n    e_t <= sum_i delta_(n_i)\n        < 64*2048^(-4/3)/(1-(3/4)^(4/3))\n        < 256*2048^(-4/3)\n        < 1/100.                                        (6.2)\n\nFor the final exact comparison, 2048^4=17592186044416 exceeds\n25600^3=16777216000000. Thus (6.2) holds at every t, with (6.1) handling\nthe finite base. In particular,\n\n    q_(2n) <= s*(q_n+1/100) for every n>=0.              (6.3)\n\nThis is the required uniform estimate. The unproved zero-defect shifted\ndoubling conjecture has been replaced by an explicit permitted defect.\n\n## 7. Uniform ray enclosure and the oriented comparison\n\nDirectly from the original recurrences,\n\n    P_(2n+1)>=lambda P_n,  B_(2n+1)>=lambda B_n.         (7.1)\n\nThe primitive bound follows from the balanced join of two copies. For the\ndominant bound, B_1=4>=lambda B_0. Induct on n and double both child leaf\ncounts in any maximizing split of B_n; homogeneity of G and the previous\nprimitive/dominant lower bounds give (7.1). Therefore the sequence\nlambda^(-j)B_(b2^j-1) is increasing for every integer b>=1.\n\nIterating (6.3) gives\n\n    s^(-r)q_(2^r N)\n      <= q_N+(1/100)*sum_(i=0)^(r-1)s^(-i)\n      < q_N+3/100,                                     (7.2)\n\nbecause s>3/2. Monotonicity B_(2^r N-1)<=B_(2^r N) bounds each increasing\nray, proving existence of Z_b without invoking a qualitative phase limit.\nApplying (7.2) at N=b2^j yields the uniform enclosure\n\n    lambda^(-j)B_(b2^j-1)\n      <= Z_b\n      <= lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(b2^j))+3/100)^2             (7.3)\n\nfor EVERY integer b>=1 and j>=0.\n\nChoose upper ray b=15,j=8 (so the upper finite index is 3840) and lower\nray b=3,j=10 (finite index 3071). These two depths are used for the proved\nenclosure; the discovery screen separately compared SAME-j ray ratios.\nThe finite certificate shows B_3840<90000, hence\n\n    (sqrt(B_3840)+3/100)^2 < B_3840+19.\n\nIt also gives the exact strict inequality\n\n    lambda^2*(B_3840+19) < (773/100)*B_3071.             (7.4)\n\nSince 10-8=2, (7.3) and (7.4) establish the proposed E5 inequality,\n\n    Z_15/Z_3 < lambda^2*(B_3840+19)/B_3071 < 773/100.    (7.5)\n\nThe computed rational upper quotient in (7.5) is approximately\n7.719573399222938; the displayed exact integer slack in section 8 is the\ncertificate, not this decimal. In fact the same endpoints certify the\nslightly stronger comparison\n\n    Z_15/Z_3 < 193/25 = 7.72.                            (7.6)\n\n## 8. Finite computation, integer certificate, and source preservation\n\nPublic ledger: research/max3_phase_ratio_discovery_budget.json.\n\n1. Run 1: n<=31, binary64, all original F/G splits, small calibration.\n2. Run 2: n<=4095, binary64, all original F/G splits, same-j and adjacent rays.\n3. Run 3: n<=63, Decimal with 80 significant digits, independently evaluated\n   using the ORIGINAL phi/psi and S_circle/S_bullet transformations.\n4. Run 4: n<=4095, EXACT integer interval endpoints on denominator D=2^80,\n   all original splits, integer square roots rounded outward.\n\nThe source for runs 1--3 is research/c76_phase_dp.py. The source for run 4\nis research/c77_interval_dp.py. No NumPy was present and none was installed.\nOnly the existing Python standard library was used, in one process.\n\nHere is the enclosing arithmetic for run 4. If a,b are endpoint numerators\non denominator D, let\n\n    R_Omega=(a+b)^2+4ab, c_Omega=a+b,\n    R_G=b^2+8ab,         c_G=4a+b.\n\nFor a lower endpoint, return floor((c+floor(sqrt(R)))/2); for an upper\nendpoint, return ceil((c+ceil(sqrt(R)))/2). The result is again an integer\nnumerator on denominator D. Starting with exact D for P_0 and B_0, apply\nthese formulas to lower endpoints and upper endpoints separately, and\nmaximize each over ALL original k=0,...,n-1. Positivity and monotonicity\nof Omega,G prove by induction that each stored rational interval encloses\nthe exact recurrence value. Integer sqrt is checked by its defining\nr^2<=R<(r+1)^2 condition. No original split is pruned and no computed\nargmax is assumed exact. All possible maximizing splits are retained as\nthose whose upper bound is at least the largest lower bound.\n\nThe complete table is research/c77_dp_run04.csv, SHA256\n2a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.\nIts largest interval widths for P and B are below 2.768e-20 and 5.920e-20,\nrespectively. These widths are diagnostics, not additional hypotheses.\n\nThe exact saved-table postprocessor research/c77_postprocess_certificate.py\ndoes NOT evaluate the recurrence again. It encloses q_i by the floor/ceiling\ninteger square roots of D times the saved B endpoint numerators. With\n\n    D = 1208925819614629174706176,\n    s_lower_numerator = 1878397475050142397719609,\n    lambda_squared_upper_numerator = 7046136038847924384392699,\n\nit checks, for all fine indices t=0,...,4095, positivity of\n\n    s_lower_numerator*q_lower_(t/2) - D*q_upper_t\n                                                    (t even),\n    s_lower_numerator*(q_lower_floor(t/2)+q_lower_(floor(t/2)+1))\n                          -2D*q_upper_t             (t odd).\n\nThe minimum positive slack is\n33709536743225496543599832619768805273186776891, at t=4094.\nAll 4096 slacks, not just this minimum, are retained in\nresearch/c77_finite_base_slacks.csv, SHA256\ndfeb3c318e08fba6a889ff78154cf7b762029b86ffa73b64d20fd6f99aa9fb73.\nThis is the finite certificate (6.1).\n\nFor (7.4), the saved endpoint numerators are\n\n    B_3071 lower = 77954524439662110401113475160,\n    B_3840 upper = 103225412920462918384305104511.\n\nWriting these as L and H, and the lambda-squared upper numerator as A,\nthe integer cross-multiplication gives\n\n    773*L*D - 100*A*(H+19D)\n      = 98261575859406148591490057041393620527049740462983180 > 0.\n\nFor (7.6), replace 773 by 772 in that expression. Its exact positive value is\n4020338508518990670593580566018450094572713188395020. The separate saved\naddendum research/c77_stronger_threshold.json retains this comparison and\nthe P_4 upper endpoint, which also proves P_4<77/10. Thus the new sufficient\ncapacity threshold 193/25 exceeds the finite primitive baseline by more\nthan 1/50. These deductions use the existing table, with zero recurrence\nevaluations; their negligible measured postprocessing is charged in the ledger.\n\nThe certificate JSON is research/c77_finite_certificate.json. Every run\nand postprocessing source/output hash is in the public ledger. Numerical\nfloating-point ties were retained in the earlier runs; mathematical ties\nare not inferred from them. The independent 80-digit original recurrence\nagreed with binary64 to below 4e-16 relative error on its prefix. The exact\ninterval run independently encloses the full original recurrence and is\nthe only finite arithmetic used in (6.1) and (7.4).\n\n## 9. A failed shortcut and the remaining original-target obligations\n\nConcavity of q_n is false in the recorded interval computation. At n=721,\nthe outward square-root intervals on denominator D yield\n\n    q_720 lower = 122144100434199085638661907 / D,\n    q_721 upper = 122251834840112040002834251 / D,\n    q_722 lower = 122359583204786422642255132 / D.\n\nConsequently q_720+q_722-2q_721 is at least\n13958761428275248537/D > 0. The exact underlying B intervals are saved in\nresearch/c77_concavity_countercheck.json. This rejects a proof shortcut;\nit does not reject (E5) or the zero-defect shifted-doubling conjecture.\nThe successful defect argument in sections 5--7 uses only monotonicity.\n\nC75's actual substitution, full-function certificate identities, source\nnormalization, and index audit remain intact in the public mirror\nresearch/shared_evidence/ca69568f812f2b3d.md. The bounded output\noffset is handled by C75's independently audited phase/adjacent-value step.\nNo such seed has been found or searched for in this round. The scalar\ncomparison does not prove the original unrestricted GD target.\n\nFor clarity, the unchanged actual seed interface is four nonnegative steps\nh_0,...,h_3, s_h=sum h_i, all twenty nonnegative ordered multipliers\nLambda_ij for distinct i,j in {0,...,4}, and the full five-gradient PSD\ncondition from C75. Explicitly, let e be the all-ones column,\nT_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i and T_it=0 for t>i,\nL_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_3,2s_h)^T,\nand D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_3(2-h_3),-s_h(s_h+2)). Require\n\n    1+s_h>193/25,\n    L_flow^T e=omega,\n    D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >= 0.\n\nThis is a proposed downstream feasibility target, not a feasible seed or\na new search allocation.
