{"id": "8c9b71e252e4172d", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T02:32:25.722173+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max2: NEW uniform bulk constant-load proof in the original same-epsilon word. For every actual m>=128 and 17<=k<=m-1, 2d_k<ellhat_k<(11/4)d_k. This is a complete analytic subresult in an UNVERIFIED joint draft; only load rows3..16 remain. No b/u margin or full kernel conclusion follows.", "evidence": "# Positive off-diagonal entries and exact chronological decimation\n\nStatus: **unverified proof draft**, sixth launch, second continuation round.\nNo kernel evaluation, optimizer, installation, or fact submission was used.\nThe uniform positive-semidefinite conclusion and the original minimax Goal\nremain open. This document proves some of the actual coefficient claims;\nit does not assume the remaining thirteen-band estimates.\n\n## 1. Definitions and the coefficient that is being estimated\n\nFix an integer m>=128 and keep epsilon=1/m fixed throughout. Set\n\n lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,\n d_k=1+a*c^k  (k>=0), H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j).\n\nThe extension of d_k past m-1 is only a coefficient device. The actual\nqueried word is H_m with its last d_0 removed. Write h_i for its steps,\ns_i=sum_(r<i)h_r, and s'=s_i+h_i when discussing a single step.\n\nOn the infinite original ruler word define B_00=1 and\n\n B_(i+1),k=(1-h_i/d_k)B_ik+(h_i/d_(k-1))B_i,(k-1),\n\nwith a zero second term at k=0. For each occupied state k, h_i<=d_k:\nthe first occurrence of d_j kills state j, and all smaller states have\nalready died. This also proves B_ik>=0, sum_k B_ik=1, and\nB_ik=0 for i>=2^k or k>i.\n\nFor arbitrary formal vectors y_k put g_i=sum_k v_ik*y_k, where\nv_ik=B_ik/d_k. The row-finite symmetric matrix E is defined by\n\n sum_(k,l)E_kl<y_k,y_l>\n =sum_i [2s_(i+1)h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>\n       +s_(i+1)||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2].   (1)\n\nEach coefficient is a finite sum: a term containing state k occurs by\nthe step immediately before query2^k. In particular E_kl=0 for k<l\nand l>2^k. The m-principal block is the actual finite residual kernel.\nThe coefficient convention in (1) means that an off-diagonal entry\nappears twice in the quadratic form.\n\n## 2. Two elementary bounds for the actual chronological word\n\nFor the elementary parameter bounds used below, lambda>12/5 and\nlambda<29/12 give 12/5<c<39/16. The upper comparison follows from\n(29/12)(129/128)=3741/1536<39/16. Consequently\na>(127/128)/(39/16)=127/312>2/5. Moreover\n\n d_0=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],\n d_2=2+sqrt(2)-lambda*epsilon^2,\n\nso 7/5<d_0<3/2 and d_2<7/2; for the first lower bound use\nsqrt(2)>141/100 and 2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]<5/768.\nIn particular 2<c<3 and 1<d_0<2. Also\n\n                         d_(k+1)>2d_k  (k>=2).            (2)\n\nIndeed d_(k+1)-2d_k=(c-2)a*c^k-1. At k=2 the quantity\nbefore subtracting1 is (1-epsilon)c(c-2), and\n\n c(c-2)=1+2lambda(lambda-1)epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2\n        >1+6epsilon.\n\nConsequently (1-epsilon)c(c-2)>1+5epsilon-6epsilon^2>1.\nThe expression increases with k, proving (2).\n\nSecond, every original physical step satisfies\n\n                             h_i<s_i+2.                  (3)\n\nLet S_j=sum H_j and C_j=S_j+2-d_j. Then C_0=2-d_0>0.\nThe recurrences S_(j+1)=2S_j+d_j and\nd_(j+1)=c*d_j-(c-1) give the exact identity\n\n C_(j+1)=2C_j+(3-c)(d_j-1)>0.\n\nThe prefix before the FIRST d_j is H_j, so (3) holds there. Every later\noccurrence has a larger elapsed clock and the same step size. This proves\n(3) at every position, without changing the enclosing epsilon.\n\nThere is a sharper envelope that does exclude the recorded swap foil.\nPut rho=c-2 and gamma=rho-a=c-1-d_0>0. The exact word-sum formula gives\n\n rho(S_j+1)+1-d_j=gamma*2^j.\n\nThus every original occurrence h_i=d_j obeys\n\n rho(s_i+1)+1-h_i=gamma*2^j+rho(s_i-S_j)>=0.              (3a)\n\nIf the third and fourth steps are exchanged, the first d_2 has\ns_i=d_0+d_1 instead of S_2=2d_0+d_1. Its slack in (3a) becomes\n4gamma-rho*d_0<13/128-14/25<0. Here rho>2/5, d_0>7/5, and\ngamma<(13/4)epsilon, as proved explicitly in Section7 below. This\ndistinguishes the original chronology from that particular foil.\nEquation (3a) alone is not asserted to prove b_i or u_i.\n\n## 3. Nonnegative off-diagonal entries, term by term\n\nFor a fixed step h=h_i, write v_k=v_ik and v_(-1)=0. The scaled birth\nrecurrence is\n\n v'_k=v_k+(h/d_k)(v_(k-1)-v_k).\n\nFor k<l, expansion of that step's contribution to E_kl gives exactly\n\n C_kl*v_k*v_l\n +[s'h^2(d_l-1)/(d_k*d_l)]v_(k-1)*v_l\n +[s'h^2(d_k-1)/(d_k*d_l)]v_k*v_(l-1)\n +[s'h^2/(d_k*d_l)]v_(k-1)*v_(l-1),                     (4)\n\nwhere\n\n C_kl=h[2s'+1-h-s'h(1/d_k+1/d_l-1/(d_k*d_l))].           (5)\n\nTo check (4), the first term of (1) contributes\ns'h(v'_k*v_l+v_k*v'_l), the second contributes\ns'(v'_k-v_k)(v'_l-v_l), and the third contributes\nh(1-h)v_k*v_l. Expanding these three expressions gives (4)-(5).\n\nThe last three coefficients in (4) are nonnegative. Suppose k>=2.\nIf v_k*v_l=0, the possibly signed coefficient C_kl costs nothing.\nOtherwise state k is occupied, so h<=d_k. Equation (2) gives\nd_l>=d_(k+1)>2d_k and hence\n\n h(1/d_k+1/d_l-1/(d_k*d_l))<3/2.\n\nIt follows from (3) that\n\n C_kl>h(s'/2+1-h)=(h/2)(s_i+2-h)>0.                     (6)\n\nThus every physical step contributes a nonnegative amount to E_kl\nwhen 2<=k<l. This proof keeps the signed terms of the original step\nuntil they combine into C_kl; it does not take their absolute values.\n\nFor k=0,1, support leaves only E_01 and E_12. The first two steps,\nwhose unscaled state vectors are successively V_0,V_1,V_2, give\n\n E_01=(d_0-1)/d_1>0,\n E_12=(d_0+d_1)(d_1-1)/(d_1*d_2)>0.\n\nThese calculations and (4)-(6) prove\n\n                    E_kl>=0 for every k!=l.              (7)\n\nIn particular the actual second-neighbor weights obey e_0=e_1=0 and\n\n                    e_k=E_(k,k+2)>0 for k>=2.             (8)\n\nFor strictness, just before the first d_k both B_ik and B_i,(k+1)\nare positive when k>=2. For B_ik, use the path that takes a birth on\neach of its first k steps and then stays. All those birth probabilities\nare positive, and every subsequent step before the first d_k is smaller\nthan d_k. For B_i,(k+1), take the first k+1 births and then stay; this\nis possible because k+1<=2^k-1, and all later pre-d_k steps are smaller\nthan d_(k+1). At the d_k step, the third term in (4) with l=k+2 is\nstrictly positive: s'>0, d_k>1, v_k>0, v_(k+1)>0. This proves (8).\n\nNo uniform lower bound such as e_k>=1/15 is proved by this strictness\nargument: the displayed positive path probabilities have not been\nestimated uniformly.\n\n## 4. Exact consequences for the unresolved row margins\n\nFor all nonnegative indices define\n\n A_i=E_(i,i+1), e_i=E_(i,i+2),\n b_i=A_i-2(e_(i-1)+e_i),\n u_i=E_ii-A_(i-1)-A_i+e_(i-2)+e_i\n                         -sum_(|l-i|>=3)|E_il|,\n\nwith negative subscripts equal to zero. Let F_i=sum_l E_il be the\nfinite unweighted row sum, and ellhat_i=sum_l E_il*d_l the constant\nload obtained by polarizing (1) against the constant gradient.\n\nEquation (7) removes all absolute values from the far off-diagonal\nsum. Therefore the exact full-row identity is\n\n                     u_i=2E_ii+2(e_(i-2)+e_i)-F_i.         (9)\n\nIn particular a proof of u_i does not need to identify alternating\nsigns of individual far entries. It still needs signed cancellation\nbetween the diagonal, the neighboring entries, and the row sum.\nNonnegative entries do not imply either b_i>=2/15 or u_i>=0.\n\nThis does not discard a terminal coefficient. In the inherited finite\ndecomposition, the triple starting at m-2 remains the square\ne_(m-2)||y_(m-2)+2y_(m-1)||^2. Its coefficient now has the sign in (8).\nThe last diagonal remains\n\n u_(m-1)+sum_(l>=m+2)E_(m-1,l)+b_(m-1)+e_(m-1).\n\nNeither its required quantitative lower bound nor the completed\nconstant-column remainder follows from (7) alone.\n\n## 5. A scalar constant-load formula with the signed difference retained\n\nFor this section all sums run over the infinite coefficient word, but\nevery state-specific sum is finite. Set\n\n mu_ik=h_i B_ik/d_k,\n L_k=sum_(r=0)^k d_r,\n M_k=sum_i h_i*mu_ik,\n N_k=sum_i s_(i+1)*h_i*mu_ik,\n\nand put M_(-1)=N_(-1)=0. The flow equation is\nB_(i+1),k-B_ik=mu_i,(k-1)-mu_ik. Summing it proves\nsum_i mu_ik=1 for each transient state. Weighting it by s_(i+1)\nand summing by parts gives\n\n sum_i s_(i+1)(mu_ik-mu_i,(k-1))=sum_i h_i B_ik=d_k.\n\nStarting with a zero predecessor moment shows\nsum_i s_(i+1)mu_ik=L_k. Thus M_k and N_k are respectively the mean\nexit step and the mean product of exit clock and exit step for state k.\n\nPolarizing (1) against the constant gradient gives\n\n ellhat_k=sum_i h_i[(s_i+1)v_ik+s_(i+1)v_(i+1),k].         (10)\n\nFor example, the first term in (1) contributes\ns'h(v_ik+v_(i+1),k), its increment-square term contributes zero,\nand its last term contributes h(1-h)v_ik. Their sum is (10).\nSubstitute the birth recurrence into (10), then use the two occupation\nidentities just proved. The result is\n\n             ellhat_k=2L_k+1-M_k-(N_k-N_(k-1))/d_k.        (11)\n\nIn detail, before the recurrence contribution the sum is\n2L_k+1-M_k. The remaining term is\nsum_i s'h_i^2(v_i,(k-1)-v_ik)/d_k\n=(N_(k-1)-N_k)/d_k. This checks its sign and its denominator.\n\nFormula (11) is an identity, not a new condition replacing the load\ninequalities. It identifies the chronological mixed moment that must\nbe estimated. Bounding N_k and N_(k-1) separately loses the signed\nquantity needed for the desired 2d_k<=ellhat_k<=11d_k/4.\n\n### 5.1 The first required constant-load row is settled uniformly\n\nThe row k=2 can be evaluated and bounded without estimating the higher\nbirth states. Put t=d_0, u=d_1, d=d_2, and theta=1-t/d. The nonzero\nvalues of B_n2 before its extinction are B_22=1 and B_32=theta.\nAlso B_12=0 and B_42=0. Substitution in (10), using the original\nfirst four steps (t,u,t,d), gives\n\n ellhat_2=[u(t+u)+t(t+u+1)\n                 +(t(2t+u)+d(2t+u+1))*theta]/d.\n\nCollecting its terms, with no inequality, gives\n\n ellhat_2/d=((t+u)/d)^2+(2t+u+1)/d-t^2(2t+u)/d^3.         (11a)\n\nThe actual parameters obey\n7/5<t<3/2, 199/100<u<2, and 17/5<d<7/2.\nThe only new lower bound, d>17/5, follows from\nd=2+sqrt(2)-lambda*epsilon^2,\nsqrt(2)>141/100, and lambda*epsilon^2<(5/2)/128^2<1/100.\n\nFor a lower bound in (11a), its three displayed positive magnitudes\nobey, in order,\n\n ((t+u)/d)^2>(339/350)^2>9/10,\n (2t+u+1)/d>579/350>33/20,\n t^2(2t+u)/d^3<(45/4)/(75/2)=3/10.\n\nHere d^3>(17/5)^3>75/2. Hence ellhat_2/d>9/4.\nFor the upper bound, the corresponding comparisons are\n\n ((t+u)/d)^2<(35/34)^2<17/16,\n (2t+u+1)/d<30/17<177/100,\n t^2(2t+u)/d^3>(49/25)(19/4)/43=931/4300>21/100.\n\nThe last line uses 2t+u>19/4 and d^3<(7/2)^3<43. Therefore\n\n                   9/4<ellhat_2/d_2<1049/400<21/8.        (11b)\n\nIn particular the required interval [2,11/4] holds at k=2 for every\nactual m>=128. The unresolved constant-load rows start at k=3.\n\n### 5.2 A clock-slack identity, with the nonmonotone term exposed\n\nPut rho=c-2, alpha_clock=rho/(1+rho), tau_i=s_(i+1), and\n\n omega_i=alpha_clock*tau_i+1-h_i\n        =[rho(s_i+1)+1-h_i]/(c-1)>=0,\n Z_k=sum_i mu_ik*omega_i,\n W_k=sum_i mu_ik*tau_i*omega_i,\n H2_k=sum_i mu_ik*tau_i^2.\n\nTake all predecessor quantities with index-1 to be zero. The sharp\noriginal clock inequality (3a) supplies omega_i>=0. Weighting the\nbirth flow by tau_i^2 and summing by parts gives\n\n H2_k-H2_(k-1)=sum_i(2tau_i*h_i-h_i^2)B_ik\n              =d_k(2L_k-M_k).\n\nThe definitions also give\nM_k=alpha_clock*L_k+1-Z_k and\nN_k=alpha_clock*H2_k+L_k-W_k. Substitution in (11) yields the exact\nsigned formula\n\n ellhat_k=c*L_k/(c-1)^2-1/(c-1)\n                  +Z_k/(c-1)+(W_k-W_(k-1))/d_k.           (11c)\n\nThe explicit level sum is\nL_k=c*d_k/(c-1)+k-1+gamma/(c-1). Thus the first two terms on the\nright of (11c) equal\n\n [c^2/(c-1)^3]d_k+c(k-1)/(c-1)^2\n                         +c*gamma/(c-1)^3-1/(c-1).       (11d)\n\nFor k>=2 this exceeds2d_k: c^2/(c-1)^3 decreases for c>1,\nand its value at39/16 is24336/12167=2+2/12167. The remaining\nnon-gamma terms are at least1/(c-1)^2, and gamma>0.\n\nThis calculation does NOT prove the lower load bound. Although\nZ_k>=0 and W_k>=0, the difference W_k-W_(k-1) has not been signed.\nIts summand cannot be treated as increasing just because birth-state\nexit times increase. In fact it has a large decrease in the ORIGINAL\nactual word: at the third step, i=2 and h_2=t=d_0,\n\n tau_2*omega_2=(2t+u)[gamma+rho(t+u)]/(c-1)>4,\n\nwhereas at the fourth step, the first d_2,\n\n tau_3*omega_3=(2t+u+d_2)*4gamma/(c-1)<1.\n\nFor the first comparison use 2t+u>47/10 and\nrho(t+u)/(c-1)>(2/5)(339/100)/(3/2)>9/10.\nFor the second use 2t+u+d_2<17/2 and\n4gamma/(c-1)<13/[128(7/5)]=65/896. These hold for every\nactual m>=128. The expected difference in (11c) may still be\nnonnegative, but a proof must use the original exit distribution;\npathwise monotonicity of this clock weight is false.\n\n## 6. An exact reduction that distinguishes the original lower-level order\n\nPut t=d_0, ell=c-1, gamma=ell-t, and\n\n Z(z)=c*z/(1+ell*z),\n A(z)=(1-t*z)(1+ell*z)=1+gamma*z-t*ell*z^2.\n\nThese are new scalar functions, unrelated to the adjacent entries A_i.\nThey are well-defined at every nonnegative reciprocal physical node.\nThe affine-geometric node relation gives\n\n (1-t*z)(1-d_(j+1)*z)=A(z)(1-d_j*Z(z)),\n Z(1/d_(j+1))=1/d_j.                                    (12)\n\nWrite the original infinite step word in consecutive pairs. The exact\npair at position r is (t,d_(nu_2(r+1)+1)), where nu_2 is the exponent\nof2 in a positive integer. After removing every t and lowering the\nremaining level by1, the compressed word is the same original ruler\nword, with the SAME epsilon. For its first r steps write\nP_r(z)=product_(i<r)(1-h_i*z) and s_r=sum_(i<r)h_i. Then\n\n P_(2r)(z)=A(z)^r P_r(Z(z)),\n P_(2r+1)(z)=(1-t*z)A(z)^r P_r(Z(z)),\n s_(2r)=c*s_r-gamma*r,\n s_(2r+1)=c*s_r-gamma*r+t.                               (13)\n\nAll four identities follow by multiplying or summing the individual\npairs in (12). This is a chronological identity, not an identity of\nthe full multiset alone. The foil that exchanges the third and fourth\nsteps destroys the displayed consecutive-pair description.\n\nThe Newton factors also transform exactly:\n\n phi_(k+1)(z)=(1-t*z)(1+ell*z)^k phi_k(Z(z)),              (14)\n\nwhere phi_k(z)=product_(j<k)(1-d_j*z). At epsilon=0, interpreted only\nas a coefficient limit, t=ell=sqrt(2), gamma=0, and A(z)=1-2z^2.\nAt every actual epsilon>0 the linear term gamma*z and the clock defect\n-gamma*r in (13) must both be retained. In fact\n\n gamma=lambda*epsilon+2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]>0.\n\nEquations (12)-(14) do not yet establish a bound for E or the mixed\nmoments in (11). They give an exact original-order transformation in\nwhich those estimates can be pursued without replacing an embedded\nblock's parameters by epsilon=1/j.\n\n## 7. The decimation multiplier changes sign around one\n\nIt is false that the multiplier A at all required physical nodes can\nbe discarded by a uniform inequality A<=1, or by a uniform inequality\nA>=1. Both failures occur at every actual m>=128, without using a\nstandalone-depth parameter in an embedded block.\n\nFor z=1/d_j,\n\n             A(1/d_j)-1=(gamma*d_j-t*ell)/d_j^2.           (15)\n\nWe have 7/5<t<3/2, t<ell<3/2, and\n\n 2epsilon<gamma\n   <(lambda+2/lambda)epsilon\n   =(3sqrt(2)-1)epsilon<(13/4)epsilon.                    (16)\n\nAt j=2, d_2<7/2, so\ngamma*d_2<91/1024<49/25<t*ell. Therefore A(1/d_2)<1.\nAt the actual highest level j=m-1,\n\n gamma*d_(m-1)>(4/5)2^(m-1)/m>9/4>t*ell.\n\nHere a>2/5 and c>2 give the first bound. For the second, 2^(m-1)/m\nis increasing for m>=2, and even 2^10/128=8 already exceeds45/16.\nThis proves A(1/d_(m-1))>1. Both indices are in the original residual\ncoordinate set. These are counterexamples only to discarding the\ndecimation factor in one direction, not to the desired band bounds.\n\nThere is nevertheless a useful uniform estimate. For k>=8 define\n\n X_k=(65/16)epsilon*(2/c)^k+(225/32)*(2/c^2)^k.\n\nThen, for every 0<=z<=1/d_k and every integer 0<=r<=2^(k-1),\n\n 0<A(z),\n r|A(z)-1|<=X_k<1/80,\n |A(z)^r-1|<=(80/79)X_k<1/79,\n 0<=gamma*r/d_k<=(65/16)epsilon*(2/c)^k.                 (17)\n\nFor r=0 all expressions with r vanish. For positive r, (16),\nt*ell<9/4, a>2/5, and d_k>a*c^k give\n\n r|A(z)-1|\n <=2^(k-1)[gamma/d_k+t*ell/d_k^2]\n <(65/16)epsilon*(2/c)^k+(225/32)*(2/c^2)^k.\n\nAlso c>12/5, so 2/c<5/6 and 2/c^2<3/8. For k>=8, the exact rational\ncomparisons (5/6)^8<1/4 and (3/8)^8<1/2500 show\n\n X_k<65/8192+9/3200<1/80.\n\nPositivity follows from z<=1/d_k<1/t. With delta=|A(z)-1|, if A(z)>=1\nthe binomial expansion gives\n\n A(z)^r-1<=sum_(j>=1)(r*delta)^j<=X_k/(1-X_k).\n\nIf A(z)<1, 1-A(z)^r<=r*delta<=X_k. These prove the third estimate in\n(17); the last follows from the linear part of the same calculation.\n\nThus the multiplier and clock defects are uniformly small far up the\nactual spectrum, despite the change of sign in (15). Their precise\nrates include BOTH epsilon*(2/c)^k and (2/c^2)^k. This does not yet\ncontrol E: converting pointwise spectral estimates to a birth coefficient\nrequires retaining the signed divided differences, not taking a large\nabsolute bound on the entire inverse Newton matrix.\n\n## 8. An early-time bound for the entire nearby band\n\nThis localization is in PHYSICAL TIME, in addition to the earlier\nlocalization in matrix-column distance. It controls the part of a near\nentry accumulated before the low reciprocal columns die.\n\nFix j>=2 and let E^early_kl be the contribution to (1) of the first\n2^j steps, namely the prefix (H_j,d_j). If k,l>=j+6, then\n\n                         |E^early_kl|<10^(-10).           (18)\n\nHere is a uniform proof. Write T=sum(H_j,d_j). The exact word sum is\n\n sum H_j=a*c^j/(c-2)+(1-a/(c-2))*2^j-1.\n\nBecause 0<a/(c-2)<1 and 2<c, this sum is less than c^j-1.\nConsequently T<(1+1/a)d_j<(7/2)d_j. All steps in the prefix are at\nmost d_j, and d_j>3.\n\nFor an integer r>=0 define\n\n p_r=min{1,(7/2)^r/[r!*2^(r(r-1)/2)]}.\n\nAt each query of this prefix, the probability of state j+r is at most\np_r. To prove this, condition on a birth path's first arrival at j.\nReaching j+r requires r subsequent births on increasing distinct\nsteps. Each specified set has probability at most the product of its\nsteps divided by d_j...d_(j+r-1); all stay factors are at most one.\nThe sum of those step products is at most T^r/r!, and (2) bounds the\ndenominator below by d_j^r*2^(r(r-1)/2). This proof applies at either\nend of every step and for every arrival time.\n\nThe sequence p_r decreases for r>=3. Thus, for k>=j+6,\n\n 0<=v_ik,v_(i+1),k<=p_6/d_k,\n |v_(i+1),k-v_ik|<=h_i[(5/2)p_5+p_6]/d_k^2.\n\nFor the second line, apply the birth recurrence and use\nd_k/d_(k-1)<c<5/2. Define Q_6=(5/2)p_5+p_6. The exact fractions give\n\n p_5=16807/3932160<1/230,\n p_6=117649/1509949440<1/12500,\n Q_6<1/92+1/12500<11/1000.\n\nThe first and last terms of (1), polarized for a general k,l, are at\nmost [T^2+2d_j*T]p_6^2/(d_k*d_l) in absolute value. This uses\n2sum_i s_(i+1)h_i=T^2+sum_i h_i^2 and\nsum_i h_i^2<=d_j*T. The increment-square term is at most\nT^2*d_j*Q_6^2/(d_k^2*d_l^2), since\nsum_i s_(i+1)h_i^2<=T^2*d_j. These statements also apply when k=l.\n\nNow d_k,d_l>2^6*d_j. The two contributions are respectively less than\n\n 77/[4*2^12*12500^2] < 1/30000000000,\n 49*121/[12*2^24*1000^2] < 1/30000000000.\n\nTheir sum is less than10^(-10), proving (18). Every estimate is\nuniform in both the row indices and the enclosing m.\n\nFor a thirteen-entry row centered at i>=14, take j=i-12. Then both\nindices of each entry E_(i,i+r), -6<=r<=6, are at least j+6, so (18)\ncontrols its entire initial prefix through the first d_(i-12). This\ndoes not remove any of the later repeated low steps; their physical\norder and elapsed clock remain part of the late contribution.\n\n## 9. Only eighteen reciprocal nodes remain in the late contribution\n\nFor this section fix an ACTUAL required row 24<=i<=m-1, put j=i-12,\nand consider l=i+r with -6<=r<=6. Extended indices l>=m are only the\nsame-epsilon coefficient device, as in Section1. All contributing\nphysical steps are still through the first d_i and are in the original\nqueried word.\n\nDefine R_kb=(d_k/d_b)phi_k(1/d_b) for b>=k, and zero for b<k.\nIts triangular inverse D has entries\n\n D_bk=product_(0<=r<=k,r!=b)(1-d_r/d_b)^(-1),  0<=b<=k,\n D_bk=0 for b>k.                                         (19)\n\nOne direct check uses Lagrange interpolation at the distinct nodes\n1/d_0,...,1/d_k. Comparing highest-degree coefficients shows that the\nweights reciprocal to the products of node differences annihilate\npolynomials of degree below k and recover the leading coefficient\nat degree k. Apply this identity to phi_r, whose leading coefficient\nis (-1)^r product_(s<r)d_s, and rescale by d_b/d_k. The resulting\ninverse of R is exactly (19).\n\nWriting P_n(z)=product_(t<n)(1-h_t*z), the corresponding scalar\ncoefficient identity is\n\n                  v_nk=sum_(b=0)^k D_bk*P_n(1/d_b)/d_b.  (20)\n\nIndeed P_n(z)=sum_k B_nk phi_k(z), by the birth recurrence. Evaluation\nat the first k+1 reciprocal nodes kills every phi_r with r>k, and\ninversion by (19) gives (20).\n\nFor every query n>=2^j and every b<=j, P_n(1/d_b)=0 exactly; the same\nis true at the following query. Thus all such nodes disappear from\nthe late part of (1). Define M^late_ab to be the quadratic coefficient\nof the normalized modal columns P_n(1/d_a)/d_a and P_n(1/d_b)/d_b,\nusing only physical steps 2^j<=n<2^i, with their ORIGINAL elapsed\nclocks s_n. Then (20) gives the exact identity\n\n E_il=E^early_il\n          +sum_(a=j+1)^i sum_(b=j+1)^l D_ai*M^late_ab*D_bl. (21)\n\nFor l<i every term vanishes already by query2^l; for l>=i the first\ncolumn vanishes by query2^i. Consequently the displayed final cutoff\nis correct in both cases and adds no physical step beyond the original\ntrajectory. Only the eighteen nodes i-11,...,i+6 can occur in (21).\n\nCrucially, the remaining inverse columns have a uniform absolute sum.\nFor each k in [i-6,i+6],\n\n                         sum_(b=j+1)^k |D_bk|<6.          (22)\n\nTo prove this, put q_c=1/c and P^q_s=product_(r=1)^s(1-q_c^r),\nwith P^q_0=1. Factoring each difference in (19) gives\n\n |D_bk|=q_c^((k-b)(k-b+1)/2)\n             *(d_b/(d_b-1))^k/(P^q_b P^q_(k-b)).\n\nSince q_c<5/12, the elementary inequality\nproduct_r(1-x_r)>=1-sum_r x_r for 0<=x_r<=1 gives\nP^q_s>=1-q_c-q_c^2>59/144>2/5 for s>=1.\nAlso P^q_1>7/12. For b>=i-11 and k<=i+6,\n\n k/(a*c^b)<(5/2)(i+6)/2^(i-11)<=75/8192<1/100.\n\nThe middle expression decreases with i>=24. Hence\n(d_b/(d_b-1))^k<=exp(k/(a*c^b))<exp(1/100).\nSumming with r=k-b and then extending the positive sum to all r>=0,\n\n sum_b |D_bk|\n <(5/2)exp(1/100)\n       *[1+5/7+(5/2)*sum_(r>=2)(5/12)^(r(r+1)/2)]\n <=(5/2)exp(1/100)[1+5/7+625/3206]\n <5 exp(1/100)<500/99<6.\n\nFor the second line, ratios of successive terms starting at r=2\nare at most(5/12)^3, so their sum is at most125/1603. For the last\nline, exp(x)<=1/(1-x) for 0<=x<1 follows by comparing its power\nseries with the geometric series. This proves (22).\n\nFor example, if an analytic approximation changes every normalized\nlate modal coefficient in this eighteen-node window by at most eta,\nthen (21)-(22) change each reconstructed near entry by at most36eta.\nDiscarding the early part costs an additional10^(-10), by (18).\nThis is a genuine uniform coefficient-stability estimate; no small\nfixed-index inverse coefficient is included in its norm.\n\nThe pointwise decimation bound (17) is only needed while a reciprocal\ncolumn is alive. For the node 1/d_b it is identically zero after\nquery2^b, so its even-query formula only requires r<=2^(b-1), exactly\nthe range of (17). Multiplying that bound by an unnecessarily longer\nhorizon would lose the extinction information.\n\nThis leaves a specific analytic step: control the signed late K/T\ncoefficients under (12)-(14), with the original clock, and bound the\nresulting finite window uniformly. Neither (21) nor (22) asserts those\nremaining estimates or the signs of b_i and u_i.\n\n## 10. A uniform lower bound for the surviving mass at a killing step\n\nLet pi_k=B_(2^k-1),k be the probability still in state k immediately\nBEFORE the original first d_k. Then, at all actual m>=128 and every\nsame-epsilon coefficient index k>=0,\n\n                              pi_k>17/100.                (23)\n\nThis is a quantitative probability bound, not the required quantitative\nsecond-neighbor bound e_k>=1/15.\n\nHere is a product proof. Put q_c=1/c and n_exp,k=2^k-1-k.\nAll smaller states have died before the first d_k. Evaluating\nP_(H_k)(z)=sum_r B_(2^k-1),r phi_r(z) at z=1/d_k therefore gives\n\n pi_k=product_(r=1)^k (1-d_(k-r)/d_k)^(2^(r-1)-1)\n     =(1-1/d_k)^(n_exp,k)\n           product_(r=1)^k(1-q_c^r)^(2^(r-1)-1).           (24)\n\nThe second equality uses\n1-d_(k-r)/d_k=(1-1/d_k)(1-q_c^r). In particular pi_0=pi_1=1,\npi_2=1-d_0/d_2>19/34, and\npi_3=(1-d_0/d_3)^3(1-d_1/d_3)>(3/4)^3(7/10)>17/100.\nFor the last comparison, d_3>34/5: the formula\nd_3=1+lambda^2(1+epsilon-epsilon^2-epsilon^3) exceeds\n1+lambda^2=4+2sqrt(2)>34/5. The bounds on d_0,d_1 are in Sections2,5.\n\nFor k>=4 define the positive convergent product\n\n Phi(q)=product_(r>=1)(1-q^r)^(2^(r-1)-1),  0<q<1/2,\n S_1(q)=sum_(r>=1)(2^(r-1)-1)q^r\n       =q/(1-2q)-q/(1-q).\n\nConvergence, as well as the termwise differentiations below on the\nclosed parameter interval, follows by comparison with sum_r r(2q)^r.\nUsing log(1-x)>=-x/(1-x) and -log(1-x)>=x in (24) gives\n\n log(pi_k)-log Phi(q_c)\n >=-n_exp,k/(d_k-1)+sum_(r>k)(2^(r-1)-1)q_c^r\n =-[gamma/(a(c-2))](2q_c)^k\n           +[(k+1)/a-1/(c-1)]q_c^k\n >-(325/16)epsilon*(5/6)^k.                              (25)\n\nHere gamma=c-2-a, a>2/5, c-2>2/5, gamma<(13/4)epsilon,\nand 2q_c<5/6. The second bracket is positive.\n\nWe compare Phi(q_c) with its critical parameter q_0=1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1.\nFor 0<=epsilon<=1/128, q_c=q_0/(1+epsilon), and\n\n d/d epsilon log Phi(q_c)\n =1/(1+epsilon) sum_(r>=1)\n             (2^(r-1)-1)r q_c^r/(1-q_c^r)\n >=[q_c/(1+epsilon)]S_1'(q_c)>11.                         (26)\n\nIndeed q_c>16/39>41/100 implies q_c/(1+epsilon)>2/5.\nAlso q_c<5/12 and\n\n S_1'(q_c)=1/(1-2q_c)^2-1/(1-q_c)^2\n          >2500/81-144/49>55/2.\n\nIntegrating (26) from0 to epsilon and combining with (25) proves\n\n log(pi_k)>log Phi(q_0)\n              +[11-(325/16)(5/6)^k]epsilon\n          >=log Phi(q_0),\n\nsince (325/16)(5/6)^4=203125/20736<11.\n\nFinally Phi(q_0)>17/100 follows from elementary bounds, with no\ninfinite-product evaluation. For 0<=x<1,\n-log(1-x)<=x+x^2/[2(1-x)]. The r=1 exponent is zero, and\nq_0^2<1/5. Thus\n\n -log Phi(q_0)<=S_1(q_0)+(5/8)S_1(q_0^2)\n =1+sqrt(2)/2+(5/8)(5-3sqrt(2))/14\n <12/7+11/320<7/4.\n\nThe last inequalities use sqrt(2)<10/7 and sqrt(2)>141/100.\nThe exponential series gives exp(1)<11/4: the sum through degree4\nis65/24, and its remaining tail is at most1/100. It also gives\nexp(3/4)<17/8: the sum through degree3 is269/128, and the tail\nstarting at degree4 is at most135/8704; their sum is18427/8704,\nwhich is smaller than17/8. Hence\n\n exp(7/4)<(11/4)(17/8)=187/32<100/17,\n Phi(q_0)>exp(-7/4)>32/187>17/100.\n\nThis finishes (23), including the small indices handled separately.\nThe cancellation in (25) retains the additive1 in d_k and the actual\nepsilon dependence. Bounding the two product factors in (24)\nindependently would lose that cancellation.\n\n## 11. The constant-load inequalities hold uniformly in the bulk\n\nThe following is a new analytic conclusion, still inside this unverified\njoint proof draft. For every original integer m>=128, with its SAME\nepsilon=1/m, the required constant-load inequalities hold at every\n\n                         17<=k<=m-1:\n\n                    2d_k<ellhat_k<(11/4)d_k.               (27)\n\nThe proof below does not assume monotonicity of the oscillating clock\nweight tau_i*omega_i. It bounds the actual exit moments on both sides.\nTogether with (11b), it leaves only rows3,...,16 of the load condition\nunproved. It does not prove b_k or u_k.\n\n### 11.1 Mass in the next state before a record step\n\nLet chi_j=B_(2^j-1),(j+1), immediately before the first d_j. We prove\n\n                         chi_j>9/20 for j>=8.             (28)\n\nWrite q_c=1/c and D=d_(j+1). The Newton expansion at z=1/D, where all\nstates below j have died and factors above j+1 vanish, gives\n\n U_j:=P_(H_j)(1/D)/phi_j(1/D)\n     =pi_j+(1-d_j/D)chi_j\n     =product_(r=2)^(j+1)(1-d_(j+1-r)/D)^(2^(r-2)-1).\n\nDefine the positive convergent product\n\n Psi(q)=product_(r>=2)(1-q^r)^(2^(r-2)-1),\n C(q)=sum_(r>=2)(2^(r-2)-1)q^r\n     =q^2/(1-2q)-q^2/(1-q).\n\nThe same finite-tail cancellation as in (25), now with half the leading\nexponent, can be checked directly as follows. The exponent sum in U_j is\n2^j-1-j. Since\n1-d_(j+1-r)/D=(1-1/D)(1-q_c^r), the elementary logarithm inequalities\nused in Section10 yield\n\n log U_j-log Psi(q_c)\n >=-[gamma/(2a(c-2))](2q_c)^(j+1)\n       +[(j+1)/a-1/(c-1)]q_c^(j+1)\n >-(325/32)epsilon*(5/6)^(j+1).                            (29)\n\nFor completeness, the first term is the sum of\n-(2^j-1-j)/(a*c^(j+1)) and the tail\nsum_(r>=j+2)(2^(r-2)-1)q_c^r. Summing its two geometric series gives\nexactly the displayed first line. The second bracket is positive.\n\nAlong q_c=q_0/(1+epsilon), where q_0=sqrt(2)-1, differentiation gives\n\n (d/d epsilon)log Psi(q_c)\n >=[q_c/(1+epsilon)]C'(q_c)>5.\n\nHere q_c/(1+epsilon)>2/5, and q_c>41/100, q_c<5/12 give\n\n C'(q_c)\n >41/9+1681/162-10/7-25/49>25/2.\n\nUniform convergence follows from sum r(2q_c)^r, as in Section10.\nFor j>=4, (325/32)(5/6)^(j+1)<5, so integrating the derivative and\nusing (29) proves U_j>Psi(q_0).\n\nWe now give an explicit rational lower bound on this last product.\nThe first nonzero exponent is at r=3, q_0^3<3/40, and q_0^2<7/40.\nConsequently\n\n -log Psi(q_0)\n <=C(q_0)+[20/37]C(q_0^2)\n <sqrt(2)/2+1/185<713/1000.\n\nIndeed C(q_0)=sqrt(2)/2 and\nC(q_0^2)=q_0^6/[(1-2q_0^2)(1-q_0^2)]\n <343/34320<1/100. The penultimate rational comparison uses\nsqrt(2)<99/70. To compare with log(100/49), integrate\n2/(1-t^2)>2(1+t^2+t^4) from0 to51/149; this gives\n\n log(100/49)>2[51/149+(51/149)^3/3+(51/149)^5/5]\n             >713/1000.\n\nIt follows that U_j>49/100 for j>=4.\n\nFor the other term, log(1-x)<=-x in the exact product for pi_j gives\n\n -log pi_j>=(S_j-L_(j-1))/d_j\n =1/[(c-2)(c-1)]\n   -[j+1/(c-2)+gamma/(c-1)-gamma*2^j/(c-2)]/d_j.\n\nFor j>=8, use c<39/16, 1/(c-2)<5/2, gamma/(c-1)<1/50 and\nd_j>(2/5)(12/5)^j. The expression (j+63/25)/d_j is less than1/40:\nat j=8 it is bounded by (263/10)(5/12)^8<1/40, and this upper\nbound decreases thereafter. Therefore\n\n -log pi_j>256/161-1/40>31/20.\n\nThe finite exponential sum through degree4 at31/20 exceeds50/11.\nThus pi_j<11/50. Since 1-d_j/d_(j+1)<1-1/c<23/39,\n\n chi_j>(49/100-11/50)/(23/39)=1053/2300>9/20.\n\nThis proves (28). All exponent counts are those of the original H_j.\n\n### 11.2 Elementary scale and block-moment bounds\n\nLet T_k=S_k+d_k denote the clock just after the first d_k, and retain\nL_k,M_k,N_k,H2_k,Z_k,W_k from Section5.2. The following bounds will be\nused only at the indicated indices:\n\n L_k<=2d_k for every k;\n L_k<(171/100)d_k, d_k>10000, L_k/d_k^2<1/1000  (k>=12);\n 82/25<T_k/d_k<69/20                               (k>=12);\n 16/39<d_(k-1)/d_k<5/12                            (k>=8). (30)\n\nFor the first assertion, L_0,L_1<2d_0,2d_1 respectively, and\nd_2-d_0-d_1=epsilon+2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]-lambda*epsilon^2>0.\nInduction then uses d_(k+1)>2d_k for k>=2. For the second, the exact\nformula L_k=c*d_k/(c-1)+k-1+gamma/(c-1) applies. For k>=12,\nk/d_k<(5k/2)(5/12)^k<1/1000 and d_k>10000; also\nc/(c-1)<=1+1/sqrt(2)<1+99/140. These imply the stated bounds.\n\nThe clock formula from (3a) is\n\n T_k/d_k=[(c-1)/(c-2)](1-1/d_k)\n                  +gamma*2^k/[(c-2)d_k].\n\nIts lower bound exceeds (23/7)(1-1/d_k)>82/25. For its upper bound,\nuse (c-1)/(c-2)<=2+sqrt(2)<683/200 and\ngamma*2^k/[(c-2)d_k]<(325/2048)(5/6)^k<325/16384\nfor k>=12. Their sum is less than69/20.\n\nFinally d_k<c*d_(k-1) gives the lower ratio. For the upper ratio,\nd_7>198, c>241/100 and c-1<23/16 give\nd_k/d_(k-1)>241/100-23/3168>12/5 for k>=8.\n\nFor k>=12, the sum of squared physical steps in H_(k-1) obeys\n\n                  sum_(h in H_(k-1))h^2<d_k^2/20.         (31)\n\nTo verify it, expand the exact finite sum\nsum_(r=0)^(k-2)2^(k-2-r)(1+a*c^r)^2. Its three terms, divided by\nd_k^2, are bounded above by\n\n (25/8)(25/72)^k,\n (25/2)(5/12)^(k+1),\n 625/13536,\n\nrespectively. The first two are each below1/1000 at k=12 and decrease;\ntheir sum with625/13536 is below1/20. This proves (31) without\nevaluating a kernel or an individual long word.\n\n### 11.3 Two-sided clock-slack moments\n\nWe first prove the lower bound\n\n                         W_k>d_k^2/8  (k>=13).            (32)\n\nConsider the last copy of H_(k-1) in H_k, after the first d_(k-1).\nIt starts at clock T_(k-1) and ends at S_k. Throughout this block,\nstate k-1 is zero, so B_ik decreases to pi_k, and is at least17/100\nby the explicit product proof in Section10. Also tau_i*omega_i>=0.\nTherefore, replacing B_ik by17/100 in this block only decreases W_k.\nDrop the positive term sum h_i*tau_i from omega_i=alpha_clock*tau_i+1-h_i.\nThe right endpoint rule for tau_i^2, together with (31), gives\n\n W_k >=(17/[100d_k])\n   { [alpha_clock/3](S_k^3-T_(k-1)^3)\n                                  -S_k*sum_(h in H_(k-1))h^2 }.\n\nHere alpha_clock>29/100, S_k/d_k>57/25,\nT_(k-1)/d_k<(69/20)(5/12)=23/16, and S_k/d_k<49/20.\nConsequently\n\n W_k/d_k^2\n >(17/100){(29/300)[(57/25)^3-(23/16)^3]-49/400}\n =1/8+257488429/1920000000000>1/8.\n\nThe proof used the actual final H_(k-1) block, not monotonicity of\nthe clock weight across its record jumps.\n\nThe square-clock identity in Section5.2 gives\n\n H2_k=sum_(r=0)^k d_r(2L_r-M_r).\n\nThe exit at the first d_r has probability pi_r>17/100, so\nM_r>(17/100)d_r. For r>=12, (30) bounds its summand by\n(13/4)d_r^2; for all smaller r it is bounded by(383/100)d_r^2.\nAlso sum_(r=0)^j d_r^2<(7/5)d_j^2 for j>=3: at j=2 the sum is\nless than(8/5)d_2^2, and the ratios d_(r+1)>2d_r then prove the claim\ninductively. Using the stronger ratios after index8, for k>=16 we get\n\n H2_k/d_k^2\n <(13/4)(144/119)+(383/100)(7/5)(5/12)^10<4.                (33)\n\nTwo particular ORIGINAL exit steps give quantitative lower bounds on\nM_k and N_k. At the first d_k, the exit probability is pi_k. At the\nfirst d_(k-1), the probability of being in state k just before that\nstep is chi_(k-1)>9/20. Thus, putting q_k=d_(k-1)/d_k,\n\n M_k/d_k>17/100+(9/20)q_k^2,\n N_k/d_k^2>(17/100)(T_k/d_k)\n                   +(9/20)(T_(k-1)/d_(k-1))*q_k^3.       (34)\n\nFor k>=16, use W_k=alpha_clock*H2_k+L_k-N_k, (30), (33), (34), and\nalpha_clock<7/23. This gives\n\n W_k/d_k^2\n <28/23+1/1000-(17/100)(82/25)\n                     -(9/20)(82/25)(16/39)^3\n =9/16-5498383/1515930000<9/16.                           (35)\n\nIn particular, for k>=17, (32) and (35) imply an actual positive\nsigned difference, with no pointwise clock-monotonicity claim:\n\n W_k-W_(k-1)\n >[1/8-(9/16)(5/12)^2]d_k^2=(7/256)d_k^2>0.             (36)\n\n### 11.4 Completion of the bulk load bounds\n\nIn (11c), Z_k>=0, and the explicit baseline (11d) exceeds2d_k at\nk>=2. Combining this with (36) proves ellhat_k>2d_k for k>=17.\n\nFor the upper bound, use the ORIGINAL formula (11), rather than bounding\nboth signs in (11c) independently. Equations (32)-(33) and\nN_(k-1)=alpha_clock*H2_(k-1)+L_(k-1)-W_(k-1) imply\n\n N_(k-1)/d_(k-1)^2<28/23+1/1000-1/8  (k>=17).\n\nPut q_*=16/39 and q^*=5/12. Equations (30), (34) now give\n\n ellhat_k/d_k\n <342/100+1/1000\n    -17/100-(9/20)q_*^2\n    -(17/100)(82/25)-(9/20)(82/25)q_*^3\n    +(28/23+1/1000-1/8)(q^*)^2\n =11/4-404145173/9095580000<11/4.                        (37)\n\nThe positive1/d_k in (11) was bounded by1/1000. Every index used here\nis at most the required k; every contributing physical step occurs by\nthe first d_k, inside the original queried word for k<=m-1. This proves\n(27) uniformly in BOTH k and the enclosing m.\n\nThe rational comparisons in this section were checked by nine elementary\nFraction operations. These compare the displayed proof constants only;\nthey do not evaluate any kernel, recurrence grid, or horizon sample.\nNo kernel case, optimization, installation, or fact_submit was used.\n\n## 12. Scope and remaining work\n\nThe new proved-in-draft part of the stated interface is e_i>=0 for\nevery required index. The stronger entrywise result (7), the identities\n(9), (11), and (13), the uniform decimation estimates (17), and the\ntime localization and stable finite inverse (18)-(22), the row2 load\nbound (11b), and the uniform surviving-mass bound (23) have explicit\nproofs above.\n\nFor a fixed original m, the inherited-boundary identity only requires\ne_i>=0, u_i>=0 and b_i>=2/15 at 0<=i<=m-1, the stronger e_i>=1/15\nand u_i>=1/16 at the SINGLE index i=m-1, and the load bounds at\n2<=i<=m-1. The early formulas already handle b_0,b_1,b_2 and\nu_0,u_1,u_2; they are recorded in the preceding boundary draft and\nare not replaced here by a positive margin independent of epsilon.\nUniform claims at all i>=127 with fixed epsilon would be stronger\nthan the quantitative terminal bounds actually used at i=m-1.\n\nAfter (8), (11b), and (27), the unresolved claims remain b_i>=2/15 and\nu_i>=0 for 3<=i<=m-1, the stronger e_(m-1)>=1/15 and\nu_(m-1)>=1/16, and the load bounds for 3<=i<=16.\nThis document does not prove the full\nkernel positive semidefinite, and it is not a counterexample to those\nremaining actual-word claims. Even their proof would only concern the\nspecified reciprocal-level temporal quotient, not arbitrary legal GD.\n\nThe existing same-multiset foil is still a useful warning: its first\nfew steps also satisfy h_i<s_i+2, and entrywise off-diagonal positivity\nalone does not distinguish its negative diagonal-margin behavior.\nThus (7) must not be promoted into a substitute for the original-order\nestimates. UNVERIFIED; no fact_submit allocated.", "evidence": "# A supercritical whole-word obstruction from ruler products\n\nStatus: complete joint proof candidate, **UNVERIFIED**. Ninth max4 launch,\nsecond and last allocated round (cumulative21). This work uses no numerical\nevaluation, parameter sweep, installation, optimizer, or fact submission.\nThe only correctness predecessors used below are accepted facts\n`1252b8823bb5a290` (original interpolation and maximal terminal extension)\nand `b98bb807d514e80d` (exact benchmark and its elementary growth bound).\nEarlier unverified drafts are not proof premises: the movement, return,\nhierarchy and terminal arguments are all supplied here.\n\nThe new point is a whole-word estimate that permits strictly expanding\nhigh returns. Their gains are interleaved with actual level2 contractions.\nBoth the total multiplier and the accumulated additive perturbations are\ncontrolled. Thus the zero of the earlier four-ray return coefficient is\nnot a boundary of this obstruction for the full physical word.\n\n## 1. Definitions and quantitative claim\n\nFix an integer m>=1024. All subwords below use these SAME enclosing-m\nparameters:\n\n    sigma=1+sqrt(2), delta=1-1/m, c_chr=sigma(1+1/m),\n    a_chr=delta/c_chr, N=2^m, n=N-1,\n    epsilon=1/N, eta=1/N^2, l=1+a_chr, k=1+delta.\n\nThe original word is H_chr,0=() and\n\n    H_chr,j+1=(H_chr,j,1+a_chr*c_chr^j,H_chr,j), 0<=j<m.\n\nEquivalently h_i=1+a_chr*c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)) for0<=i<n, where nu_2\nis the exponent of2 dividing a positive integer. In particular a levelq\nbridge has excess u=a_chr*c_chr^q. No reindexing or parameter substitution\nis made inside a subword.\n\nFor s=(s_0,...,s_(m-1)) in{0,1}^m, define\n\n    chi_s(i)=(-1)^(sum_j s_j i_j),\n    B(s)=(-1)^s_0, C_j(s)=(-1)^(s_1+...+s_j), 1<=j<m,\n\nwhere i_j are the binary digits of the original indexi. The map froms to\n(B,C_1,...,C_(m-1)) is a bijection. Set gamma=9/10 and choose beta in(0,1).\nFor b in{1,-1} write mu_1=-beta,mu_(-1)=gamma, and define probability laws\n\n    A_b(c)=(1+mu_b)/2 * 1_(all c_j=1)\n                +(1-mu_b)/2 * 1_(all c_j=-1),\n    P_b(c)=product_(j=1)^(m-1) (1+mu_b c_j)/2,\n    w_s=(1+(1-epsilon)B(s))/2\n          *[(1-eta)A_(B(s))(C(s))+eta P_(B(s))(C(s))].          (1)\n\nEvery w_s is positive and their sum is1. Prescribe only queried gradients\n\n    g_i=(Gamma_i sqrt(w_s) chi_s(i))_s, 0<=i<n,                (2)\n\nwith arbitrary positive amplitudes Gamma_i. All isometric realizations of\ntheir Gram matrix are included.\n\nHere are exact finite-m quantities, before beta is selected:\n\n    rho=1-epsilon/2, p=1-epsilon, d_eps=gamma epsilon/2,\n    r_leaf=[(2-l)p+sqrt((2-l)^2 p^2+4(l-1))]/2,\n    L_leaf=l/r_leaf, tau_ang=2^-12,\n    beta_S=[k d_eps+sqrt((k+L_leaf)(1+L_leaf p+tau_ang)\n                                     +L_leaf^2 d_eps^2)]\n                         /[(k+L_leaf)rho].                   (3)\n\nThe claim is uniform over\n\n    7/10 <= beta <= beta_S.                                 (4)\n\nSuppose a finite-dimensional convex differentiable1-smooth function F\nwith a minimizer realizes(2) on its actual GD trajectory\nX_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i. Its initial anchor, all values, all positive queried\namplitudes, and its unqueried terminal gradient are unrestricted. Let\nX_star be a closest minimizer to X_0 and set\n\n    R=||X_0-X_star||, F_star=min F,\n    T_path=sum_(i<n)h_i Gamma_i,\n    V_chr=2(F(X_n)-F_star)/R^2.\n\nThen the candidate bounds are\n\n    R>0, T_path<8192R, ||X_i-X_star||<512R for0<=i<=n,         (5)\n    ||grad F(X_n)||<=R[2(3/4)^(N/8)+2^26*m/N^2],             (6)\n    V_chr<=2048(3/4)^(N/8)+2^36*m/N^2.                      (7)\n\nLet beta_R denote(3) with tau_ang replaced by0. We will prove\n\n    4/5<beta_R<beta_S<6/7,\n    beta_S -> sqrt((1+sqrt(2)+tau_ang)/(2+sqrt(2)))\n                  >2^(-1/4).                               (8)\n\nThus(7) extends by a fixed positive angular interval past beta_R; it is\nnot just a shrinking finite-epsilon correction. At beta=beta_S the exact\nreturn coefficient defined below equals -tau_ang, strictly negative.\n\nFor the EXACT benchmark B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, accepted\n`b98bb807d514e80d` gives B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3). Therefore\n\n    B_n V_chr <=(1+4n^(log_2 3))\n           [2048(3/4)^((n+1)/8)+2^36*log_2(n+1)/(n+1)^2]\n          ->0, n=2^m-1.                                    (9)\n\nThe same estimate holds for the maximal queried-prefix interpolant and\nall reduced-feasible terminal values in the precise sense of section8.\nThis is an upper bound on the strength of the specified character law,\nnot on the unrestricted W_n(H_chr,m). The original all-schedule minimax\ntarget remains open.\n\n## 2. Original inequalities and finite parameter estimates\n\nThe minimizer set is nonempty and closed. In finite dimension, a bounded\ndistance-minimizing sequence has a convergent subsequence, so a closest\nminimizer exists. A positive initial gradient implies R>0. Translate\nX_star to0, and subtract F_star from F.\n\nAccepted `1252b8823bb5a290` supplies the necessary original inequalities\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0,\n    f_i=F(X_i),\n\nincluding the optimum jet(X_star,g_star,f_star)=(0,0,0). Adding the two\norientations yields\n\n    ||g_i-g_j||^2<=<g_i-g_j,X_i-X_j>.                        (10)\n\nIn particular <g_i,X_i>>=||g_i||^2 and ||g_i||<=||X_i||.\nAn actual step X'=X-hg satisfies\n\n    ||X'||^2<=||X||^2-h(2-h)||g||^2.\n\nIt follows that distances do not increase for0<=h<=2; for h>=2 they\nincrease by a factor at most h-1. The same statements hold for gradient\nnorms: put g'=grad F(X'), A=||g||, D=||g'|| in(10) to get\n\n    D^2+(h-2)<g',g><=(h-1)A^2.\n\nFor h<=2 use <g',g><=AD; for h>=2 use <g',g>>=-AD. Factoring the\nresulting quadratic gives\n\n    ||g'||<=max(1,h-1)||g||.                                (11)\n\nIf A=0 then X'=X and the conclusion still holds. These inequalities are\nvalid for the actual terminal gradient without prescribing its direction.\n\nThe enclosing parameters obey\n\n    7/5<l<3/2, 199/100<k<2, c_chr<5/2,\n    1<delta*c_chr<5/2, a_chr<1/2.                            (12)\n\nFor example sqrt(2)<99/70 and m>=1024 give c_chr<49/20, while\ndelta>99/100 yields a_chr>(99/100)/(49/20)>2/5. Also c_chr>2,\nso a_chr<1/2. Finally delta*c_chr=sigma(1-1/m^2) lies between1 and5/2.\n\nSet, for the movement calculation,\n\n    lambda=rho(1-eta), omega=1-lambda, e_mix=omega/lambda,\n    d_mass=(l+k)/(l+1+(1-l)e_mix),\n    r_0=sqrt(omega)R/lambda.\n\nThe powers of2 give omega<=epsilon/2+eta and\n\n    e_mix<1/1000, 1/sqrt(lambda)<1001/1000,\n    sqrt(omega)/lambda<1/255, p>999/1000,\n    11/8<d_mass<3/2.                                       (13)\n\nFor the last pair of bounds, the denominator of d_mass is at mostl+1,\nso d_mass>=1+(k-1)/(l+1)>349/250>11/8. Subtract l+2 from\n(3/2)[l+1-(l-1)e_mix]; the result is\n(l-1)(1/2-3e_mix/2)>0, proving the upper bound. All the mixture bounds\nfollow already from omega<1/65536, strengthened if necessary by m>=1024.\n\nThe EXACT coarse correlations are\n\n    z=-rho beta+d_eps, w=-rho beta-d_eps,\n    alpha_ret=1-kz^2+L_leaf(p-zw),\n    alpha_0=(l+1)-(l+k)beta^2.                               (14)\n\nAt an original leaf, the full-law correlation of its endpoints isp.\nWriting r for the positive amplitude ratio, (10) becomes\n\n    r^2+(l-2)p r-(l-1)<=0.\n\nThe unique positive root is r_leaf in(3). Evaluating this polynomial atp\nand1 gives p<=r_leaf<1. Hence l<=L_leaf<=l+3epsilon. For beta in[0,1],\nz^2>=beta^2-2epsilon and p-zw<=1-beta^2+epsilon, whence\n\n    alpha_ret<=alpha_0+9epsilon.                            (15)\n\nTo check the last constant, the extra terms are bounded by\n2k epsilon+3epsilon(1-beta^2)+l epsilon+3epsilon^2<9epsilon.\nDirect substitution in(14) gives the complete quadratic\n\n    alpha_ret=1+L_leaf p-(k+L_leaf)rho^2 beta^2\n                    +2k rho d_eps beta+(L_leaf-k)d_eps^2.   (16)\n\nIts constant term is positive, its leading coefficient is negative,\nand it has exactly one positive zero. Solving alpha_ret=-tau_ang gives\nprecisely beta_S. Solving alpha_ret=0 gives beta_R. At beta=6/7,\nthe bounds l<3/2,k>199/100 in(15) give\n\n    alpha_ret<-157/2450+9epsilon<-tau_ang.\n\nFor the second strict inequality use epsilon<2^-20 and\n9*2^-20+2^-12<1/1000<157/2450. At beta=4/5, z^2,zw<=beta^2,\np-zw>0 and L_leaf>=l give\nalpha_ret>=alpha_0-l epsilon>28/125-(3/2)epsilon>0.\nThis proves the finite bounds in(8) and, throughout(4),\n\n    alpha_ret>=-tau_ang,\n    (l+k)beta^2<=l+1+tau_ang+9epsilon,\n    -z>=2/3, p-zw>0.                                      (17)\n\nThe last two claims follow from beta>=7/10,beta<6/7 and(13).\nAs m tends to infinity, epsilon->0, r_leaf->1, l->sqrt(2), k->2.\nTaking limits in the explicit positive root proves(8).\n\n## 3. Uniform movement BEFORE estimating perturbations\n\nSeparate from(1) its common-prefix component with B=1, of masslambda.\nWithin this component the directions repeat a_0,a_0,b_0,b_0, where\n||a_0||=||b_0||=1 and <a_0,b_0>=-beta. Consequently the full gradients\nhave an isometric direct-sum realization\n\n    g_i=Gamma_i(sqrt(lambda)u_i,sqrt(omega)v_i),\n    u_i in{a_0,b_0}, ||v_i||=1.                              (18)\n\nThis follows by realizing the two mixture Gram matrices in orthogonal\nspaces. The isometry on the gradient span can be extended to the initial\ndisplacement, adding orthogonal space when needed; it preservesR. Write\nX_0=(x^c,x^r).\n\nAt each block start t=4r,0<=t<=N-4, let P_a,P_b denote the total\nprevious physical step masses h_i Gamma_i on a_0,b_0, and put T=P_a+P_b.\nDefine\n\n    A_x=<a_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)-P_a+beta P_b,\n    B_x=<b_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)-P_b+beta P_a.\n\nWrite A=Gamma_t,C=Gamma_(t+1). The first leaf gives A>=C by(11).\nThe remainder displacement before t has norm at most sqrt(omega)T.\nAt the QUERIED sample t+1, its optimum inequality in(10) therefore gives\n\n    A_x>=l(1-e_mix)A+(1+e_mix)C-r_0-e_mix T.                 (19)\n\nIndeed the remainder contribution after that leaf, divided bylambda C,\nis at most r_0+e_mix(T+lA), and the squared gradient term divided by\nlambda C is C/lambda=(1+e_mix)C.\n\nLet D_a=lA+kC be the a-mass added in the first two steps of this block.\nFor0<=r<=1, the ratio\n(l+kr)/[l(1-e_mix)+(1+e_mix)r] increases, because\nk(1-e_mix)-(1+e_mix)>0 by(12),(13). Its value at1 is d_mass. Thus\n\n    D_a<=d_mass(A_x+r_0+e_mix T).                            (20)\n\nAt the QUERIED sample t+2, the direction is b_0. Its optimum inequality,\nwith its positive squared gradient term dropped, gives\n\n    B_x+(beta+e_mix)D_a+r_0+e_mix T>=0.                     (21)\n\nSubstitute(20) and expand A_x,B_x,T. The resulting inequality is\n\n    c_a P_a+c_b P_b\n      <=<b_0+d_mass(beta+e_mix)a_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)\n                                 +[1+d_mass(beta+e_mix)]r_0,\n    c_a=(beta+e_mix)[d_mass(1-e_mix)-1],\n    c_b=1-e_mix-d_mass(beta+e_mix)^2.                        (22)\n\nHere c_a>1/4. Since beta+e_mix<1, the right side is<4R by(13).\nPut xi=max(0,-c_b). The exact numerator of -c_b, on its positive\ndenominator D_mass=l+1+(1-l)e_mix>9/4, is\n\n    (l+k)beta^2-(l+1)+[2(l+k)beta+2l]e_mix+(k+1)e_mix^2.\n\nThe last two coefficients are<10 and<3. Also epsilon<=2e_mix, because\nomega>=epsilon/2. Hence(17) implies\n\n    xi<tau_ang/2+13e_mix,\n    c_a-5xi>1/4-(5/2)tau_ang-65/1000>1/8. The resulting conditional theorem is\nstated last in Section 13. Section 14 records validation and unresolved work.\n\n## 1. Definitions and exact scope\n\nUse the exact recurrences in accepted facts 0be8b54e0a33381c and\nb98bb807d514e80d. Set\n\n    P_n = 1+S_circle(n),  B_n = 1+2S_bullet(n),  P_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(a,b) = (a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,\n    G(a,d) = 2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2,\n    P_n = max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(P_k,P_l),\n    B_n = max_(k+l=n-1) G(P_k,B_l).\n\nAll maxima run over nonnegative integer k,l, with EVERY original split\nretained. Write lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda), s=sqrt(lambda), and\nq_n=sqrt(B_n). In this document q_n is a square-root mass, not an objective\ncoefficient. To discuss leaf counts, also write U_N=P_(N-1) and V_N=B_(N-1)\nfor N>=1, and U_0=0. The source paper's W_N equals V_N, not the project's\nworst-case functional W_n(h).\n\nThe target scalar claim is\n\n    Z_b = lim_(j->infinity) lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1),\n    Z_15 < (773/100) Z_3.                                  (E5)\n\nThe proof below derives existence of these limits as well as their enclosure.\nIt does not need the source's qualitative objective phase theorem.\n\nThe stronger conjecture B_(2n)<=lambda B_n for all n is NOT used. The\nargument allows a rigorously bounded positive defect beyond the finite base.\nThe source's global one-pivot support envelope and the zero-prefix loophole\nin C75 are not used.\n\n## 2. Precisely matched literature input\n\nThe only non-elementary external input is balanced optimality of the\nSYMMETRIC scalar recurrence, as proved in Corollary 4.2 of:\n\nYu Liu, Kang Chen, Rujun Jiang, Tianyu Wang (2026), *Optimal Recursive\nComposition and Dyadic Phase Laws for Gradient Descent with Predetermined\nStepsizes*, arXiv:2609.11788v1, https://arxiv.org/html/2609.11788v1 .\n\nIts complete statement used here is: for U_0=0, U_1=1 and\nU_N=max_(1<=m<N) Omega(U_m,U_(N-m)), the sequence is increasing and\n\n    U_(2a)=lambda U_a,\n    U_(2a+1)=Omega(U_a,U_(a+1)),  a>=0.                    (2.1)\n\nThe second identity includes U_1=Omega(U_0,U_1). The source defines its\nkernel K by exactly our Omega. Proposition 2.5 and identical initial values\nmatch its U_N to P_(N-1), by induction. No assertion about equality of ambient\nprimitive and s-composable certificate classes is imported. In particular,\nbalanced splitting is NEVER assumed for the asymmetric B recurrence.\n\nThe exact paper was downloaded in the prior round; its local text is\ndownloads/liu_chen_jiang_wang_2609.11788v1.txt. Section 2, Theorem 3.1 and\nits proof, Theorem 4.1/Corollary 4.2 and their proofs, and the adjacent-ratio\nproof in Appendix C.2 were read. Matlas returned unrelated statements;\nthis reference is grounded directly in the primary arXiv paper, not falsely\nrepresented as a Matlas hit. No external_refs submission is made because no\nfact_submit is authorized. The subsequent scalar estimates are proved here.\n\n## 3. Elementary growth and adjacent-value bounds\n\nFirst, 1<rho<4/3: lambda>2 and lambda^3=7+5sqrt(2)<16. Thus s>3/2\nand lambda^(3/2)<4.\n\nFor completeness, Omega(x,y)<=(x^(1/rho)+y^(1/rho))^rho for x,y>=0.\nFor positive x,y, put A=x^(1/rho), C=y^(1/rho), and T=(A+C)^rho.\nLet D(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. For g(z)=log D(exp z), differentiation gives\n\n    g''(z) = rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-2)\n             *((1+t)^rho-1-rho*t)/D(t)^2 > 0,  t=exp z.\n\nThe last bracket has value zero at t=0 and strictly positive derivative for\nt>0. Hence D(t)D(1/t)>=D(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. It follows that\n(T-x)(T-y)>=2xy, or T^2-(x+y)T-xy>=0. Since T>=x+y and Omega is the\npositive root of that polynomial, T>=Omega(x,y). Zero arguments follow by\ncontinuity. Induction in the full primitive recurrence gives U_N<=N^rho.\nBalanced dyadic joins give U_(2^j)=lambda^j; monotonicity then yields\n\n    N^rho/lambda <= U_N <= N^rho,  N>=1.                  (3.1)\n\nAlso G(a,d)>=Omega(a,d) for a,d>=0. Indeed, after multiplying their\ndifference by two, the required inequality is\n3a+sqrt(d^2+8ad)>=sqrt(a^2+6ad+d^2); squaring the nonnegative sides\nleaves 8a^2+2ad+6a sqrt(d^2+8ad)>=0. Induction gives V_N>=U_N.\n\nHere is the adjacent primitive estimate derived from (2.1). Let\nR_N=U_(N+1)/U_N, Delta(r)=sqrt(r^2+6r+1), and\n\n    T0(r)=(1+r+Delta(r))/(2lambda),\n    T1(r)=lambda*(Delta(r)-r-1)/2.\n\nThen R_(2N)=T0(R_N), R_(2N+1)=T1(R_N). Both maps fix 1 and have positive\nderivative at most 1/2 on [1,lambda]: their derivatives are\n(1+(r+3)/Delta(r))/(2lambda) and lambda*((r+3)/Delta(r)-1)/2;\n(r+3)/Delta(r) has derivative -8/Delta(r)^3. Starting at R_1=lambda,\nbinary induction gives\n\n    0<R_N-1 <= (lambda-1)/2^floor(log_2 N) <= 2sqrt(2)/N.\n\nUsing (3.1), U_(N+1)-U_N<=2sqrt(2) N^(rho-1). With U_0=0 this implies\nU_m-U_(m-1)<=2sqrt(2) m^(rho-1) for every m>=1.\n\nThe full dominant recurrence now gives\n\n    0 < V_(N+1)-V_N <= 8sqrt(2) N^(rho-1).                (3.2)\n\nFor the upper bound, take a maximizing prefix m for V_(N+1). If m>=2,\ncompare it to the admissible split (m-1,N+1-m) for V_N. If m=1, use\nG(U_0,V_N)=V_N. The derivative of G with respect to its first variable is\n2+2d/sqrt(d^2+8ad)<=4. Since m<=N, the bound follows. Strict increase\nfollows by choosing prefix 1 in the recurrence.\n\nConsequently, with Delta_n=q_(n+1)-q_n and barq_n=(q_n+q_(n+1))/2,\n\n    Delta_n <= 4sqrt(2lambda) (n+1)^(rho/2-1),\n    delta_n := Delta_n^2/(4barq_n)\n             <= 8lambda^(3/2) (n+1)^(rho/2-2)\n             < 32 n^(-4/3),  n>=1.                       (3.3)\n\nThe denominator bound used here is q_n>=sqrt(U_(n+1))\n>=(n+1)^(rho/2)/sqrt(lambda). No concavity of q_n is asserted.\n\n## 4. Exact merge inequality and contraction along maximizing trees\n\nLet a,b,d>0, write Q=sqrt(d), u=T_b(Q), t=T_a(u), where\n\n    T_a(v)=(v+sqrt(v^2+8a))/2,  T_a(v)^2=G(a,v^2).\n\nIf a<=d, then\n\n    G(a,G(b,d)) < G(Omega(a,b),d).                        (4.1)\n\nTo prove this, u>Q and Q(u+Q)>2Q^2>=2a. Since t is the positive\nsolution of t^2-ut=2a, this implies t<u+Q. Set r=t/u and c=a+br.\nThe polynomial defining Omega gives\n\n    c^2-(a+b)c-ab = ab*(t-u-Q)/u < 0.\n\nHere r(r-1)=2a/u^2 and 2b/u^2=1-Q/u were substituted to obtain the\nidentity. Thus c<Omega(a,b). Finally,\nt^2-Qt=2a+(u-Q)t=2(a+bt/u)=2c<2Omega(a,b), so T_Omega(a,b)(Q)>t.\nSquaring proves (4.1).\n\nConsider two successive right-continuation joins in ANY maximizing B tree.\nLet their primitive leaf counts be m1,m2 and the remaining leaf count be R.\nTheir values are a=U_m1, b=U_m2, d=V_R. The same total leaf count permits\nmerging the two primitive blocks: Omega(a,b)<=U_(m1+m2). Therefore (4.1)\nwould strictly improve the maximizing tree if a<=d. Necessarily a>d.\nBy (3.1) and V_R>=U_R,\n\n    m1^rho >= a > d >= R^rho/lambda,\n    R < 2m1,\n    R < (2/3)(m1+m2+R).                                  (4.2)\n\nThus the remaining leaf count decreases by a factor less than 2/3 every\nTWO edges along a maximizing right spine. This is an optimality argument\nfor the exact full DP, not a restriction imposed on allowed splits.\n\n## 5. A one-step rounding defect inequality\n\nExtend P_-1=0. Let v(x) be linear interpolation of q_n on nonnegative\nintegers: v(n+theta)=(1-theta)q_n+theta q_(n+1), 0<=theta<=1. Define\n\n    e_t = max(0, q_t/s - v(t/2)),  t>=0.                  (5.1)\n\nFor a maximizing split k+l=t-1, the claim is\n\n    e_t <= e_l                              if t is even,\n    e_t <= e_l+delta_floor(t/2)              if t is odd. (5.2)\n\nOnly t>4095 will require the odd formula, so delta_0 need not be bounded.\n\nThe primitive bounds from (2.1) are\n\n    P_(2a+1)/lambda=P_a,\n    P_(2a)/lambda<= (P_(a-1)+P_a)/2.                      (5.3)\n\nFor a>=1 the second follows from\nOmega(x,y)<=lambda(x+y)/2, obtained using 4xy<=(x+y)^2. For a=0,\nP_0/lambda<=1/2=(P_-1+P_0)/2 because lambda>2.\n\nThe exact dominant split inequality can be written without a radical:\n\n    2P_i+q_j q_m <= q_m^2,  i+j=m-1.                    (5.4)\n\nIt is equivalent to T_(P_i)(q_j)<=q_m. We additionally permit the formal\nboundary i=-1,j=m, where (5.4) is equality.\n\nIf t=2n and k=2a+1,l=2b, then a+b=n-1. Use (5.4) directly. If\nk=2a,l=2b+1, then a+b=n-1; average (5.4) at the two splits\n(a-1,b+1) and (a,b), both with parent n. Using (5.3) gives a root at\nmost q_n in either case before adding the continuation defect e_l.\n\nFor t=2n+1, set Qbar=(q_n+q_(n+1))/2. If k=2a+1,l=2b+1,\nthen a+b=n-1. Average the inequalities with parent/split\n(n;a,b) and (n+1;a,b+1). For u=P_a and w=(q_b+q_(b+1))/2, this gives\n\n    2u+Qbar*w-Qbar^2 <= (Delta_n^2-Delta_n*Delta_b)/4\n                       <= Delta_n^2/4.\n\nIf k=2a,l=2b, then a+b=n. Average parent/split\n(n;a-1,b) and (n+1;a,b). With u=(P_(a-1)+P_a)/2 and w=q_b,\nthe corresponding upper bound is Delta_n^2/4. Boundary a=0 uses the\nformal equality in (5.4). In both cases w<=Qbar. The derivative of\ny^2-wy-2u for y>=Qbar is at least Qbar. Its positive root is therefore\nat most Qbar+Delta_n^2/(4Qbar)=Qbar+delta_n.\n\nFinally, T_a is increasing and 1-Lipschitz in its nonnegative argument:\nT_a'(v)=(1+v/sqrt(v^2+8a))/2<=1. Homogeneity and\nq_l/s<=v(l/2)+e_l imply\n\n    q_t/s = T_(P_k/lambda)(q_l/s)\n           <= T_(P_k/lambda)(v(l/2))+e_l.\n\nCombining this with the preceding parity cases proves (5.2).\n\n## 6. The exact finite base and the resulting uniform error\n\nThe saved interval calculation checks the following FINITE certificate:\n\n    e_t=0 for every integer 0<=t<=4095.                  (6.1)\n\nThe integer tests and reproducibility information are given in section 8.\nAssuming this explicit finite certificate, follow a maximizing right spine\nin (5.2) until its index is at most 4095. For visited indices t_i>4095,\nput N_i=t_i+1 and n_i=floor(t_i/2)>=2048. By (4.2), whenever i and i+2\nare both visited, N_i>(3/2)N_(i+2). Consequently\n\n    n_i >= (N_i-2)/2 > (3/4)N_(i+2)-1\n        >= (3/2)n_(i+2)-1/4 > (4/3)n_(i+2).\n\nThe last step uses n_(i+2)>=2048. Split the visited nodes into the two\nparities of their spine position. Applying (3.3) backward from the last\nnode of each parity yields\n\n    e_t <= sum_i delta_(n_i)\n        < 64*2048^(-4/3)/(1-(3/4)^(4/3))\n        < 256*2048^(-4/3)\n        < 1/100.                                        (6.2)\n\nFor the final exact comparison, 2048^4=17592186044416 exceeds\n25600^3=16777216000000. Thus (6.2) holds at every t, with (6.1) handling\nthe finite base. In particular,\n\n    q_(2n) <= s*(q_n+1/100) for every n>=0.              (6.3)\n\nThis is the required uniform estimate. The unproved zero-defect shifted\ndoubling conjecture has been replaced by an explicit permitted defect.\n\n## 7. Uniform ray enclosure and the oriented comparison\n\nDirectly from the original recurrences,\n\n    P_(2n+1)>=lambda P_n,  B_(2n+1)>=lambda B_n.         (7.1)\n\nThe primitive bound follows from the balanced join of two copies. For the\ndominant bound, B_1=4>=lambda B_0. Induct on n and double both child leaf\ncounts in any maximizing split of B_n; homogeneity of G and the previous\nprimitive/dominant lower bounds give (7.1). Therefore the sequence\nlambda^(-j)B_(b2^j-1) is increasing for every integer b>=1.\n\nIterating (6.3) gives\n\n    s^(-r)q_(2^r N)\n      <= q_N+(1/100)*sum_(i=0)^(r-1)s^(-i)\n      < q_N+3/100,                                     (7.2)\n\nbecause s>3/2. Monotonicity B_(2^r N-1)<=B_(2^r N) bounds each increasing\nray, proving existence of Z_b without invoking a qualitative phase limit.\nApplying (7.2) at N=b2^j yields the uniform enclosure\n\n    lambda^(-j)B_(b2^j-1)\n      <= Z_b\n      <= lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(b2^j))+3/100)^2             (7.3)\n\nfor EVERY integer b>=1 and j>=0.\n\nChoose upper ray b=15,j=8 (so the upper finite index is 3840) and lower\nray b=3,j=10 (finite index 3071). These two depths are used for the proved\nenclosure; the discovery screen separately compared SAME-j ray ratios.\nThe finite certificate shows B_3840<90000, hence\n\n    (sqrt(B_3840)+3/100)^2 < B_3840+19.\n\nIt also gives the exact strict inequality\n\n    lambda^2*(B_3840+19) < (773/100)*B_3071.             (7.4)\n\nSince 10-8=2, (7.3) and (7.4) establish the proposed E5 inequality,\n\n    Z_15/Z_3 < lambda^2*(B_3840+19)/B_3071 < 773/100.    (7.5)\n\nThe computed rational upper quotient in (7.5) is approximately\n7.719573399222938; the displayed exact integer slack in section 8 is the\ncertificate, not this decimal. In fact the same endpoints certify the\nslightly stronger comparison\n\n    Z_15/Z_3 < 193/25 = 7.72.                            (7.6)\n\n## 8. Finite computation, integer certificate, and source preservation\n\nPublic ledger: research/max3_phase_ratio_discovery_budget.json.\n\n1. Run 1: n<=31, binary64, all original F/G splits, small calibration.\n2. Run 2: n<=4095, binary64, all original F/G splits, same-j and adjacent rays.\n3. Run 3: n<=63, Decimal with 80 significant digits, independently evaluated\n   using the ORIGINAL phi/psi and S_circle/S_bullet transformations.\n4. Run 4: n<=4095, EXACT integer interval endpoints on denominator D=2^80,\n   all original splits, integer square roots rounded outward.\n\nThe source for runs 1--3 is research/c76_phase_dp.py. The source for run 4\nis research/c77_interval_dp.py. No NumPy was present and none was installed.\nOnly the existing Python standard library was used, in one process.\n\nHere is the enclosing arithmetic for run 4. If a,b are endpoint numerators\non denominator D, let\n\n    R_Omega=(a+b)^2+4ab, c_Omega=a+b,\n    R_G=b^2+8ab,         c_G=4a+b.\n\nFor a lower endpoint, return floor((c+floor(sqrt(R)))/2); for an upper\nendpoint, return ceil((c+ceil(sqrt(R)))/2). The result is again an integer\nnumerator on denominator D. Starting with exact D for P_0 and B_0, apply\nthese formulas to lower endpoints and upper endpoints separately, and\nmaximize each over ALL original k=0,...,n-1. Positivity and monotonicity\nof Omega,G prove by induction that each stored rational interval encloses\nthe exact recurrence value. Integer sqrt is checked by its defining\nr^2<=R<(r+1)^2 condition. No original split is pruned and no computed\nargmax is assumed exact. All possible maximizing splits are retained as\nthose whose upper bound is at least the largest lower bound.\n\nThe complete table is research/c77_dp_run04.csv, SHA256\n2a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.\nIts largest interval widths for P and B are below 2.768e-20 and 5.920e-20,\nrespectively. These widths are diagnostics, not additional hypotheses.\n\nThe exact saved-table postprocessor research/c77_postprocess_certificate.py\ndoes NOT evaluate the recurrence again. It encloses q_i by the floor/ceiling\ninteger square roots of D times the saved B endpoint numerators. With\n\n    D = 1208925819614629174706176,\n    s_lower_numerator = 1878397475050142397719609,\n    lambda_squared_upper_numerator = 7046136038847924384392699,\n\nit checks, for all fine indices t=0,...,4095, positivity of\n\n    s_lower_numerator*q_lower_(t/2) - D*q_upper_t\n                                                    (t even),\n    s_lower_numerator*(q_lower_floor(t/2)+q_lower_(floor(t/2)+1))\n                          -2D*q_upper_t             (t odd).\n\nThe minimum positive slack is\n33709536743225496543599832619768805273186776891, at t=4094.\nAll 4096 slacks, not just this minimum, are retained in\nresearch/c77_finite_base_slacks.csv, SHA256\ndfeb3c318e08fba6a889ff78154cf7b762029b86ffa73b64d20fd6f99aa9fb73.\nThis is the finite certificate (6.1).\n\nFor (7.4), the saved endpoint numerators are\n\n    B_3071 lower = 77954524439662110401113475160,\n    B_3840 upper = 103225412920462918384305104511.\n\nWriting these as L and H, and the lambda-squared upper numerator as A,\nthe integer cross-multiplication gives\n\n    773*L*D - 100*A*(H+19D)\n      = 98261575859406148591490057041393620527049740462983180 > 0.\n\nFor (7.6), replace 773 by 772 in that expression. Its exact positive value is\n4020338508518990670593580566018450094572713188395020. The separate saved\naddendum research/c77_stronger_threshold.json retains this comparison and\nthe P_4 upper endpoint, which also proves P_4<77/10. Thus the new sufficient\ncapacity threshold 193/25 exceeds the finite primitive baseline by more\nthan 1/50. These deductions use the existing table, with zero recurrence\nevaluations; their negligible measured postprocessing is charged in the ledger.\n\nThe certificate JSON is research/c77_finite_certificate.json. Every run\nand postprocessing source/output hash is in the public ledger. Numerical\nfloating-point ties were retained in the earlier runs; mathematical ties\nare not inferred from them. The independent 80-digit original recurrence\nagreed with binary64 to below 4e-16 relative error on its prefix. The exact\ninterval run independently encloses the full original recurrence and is\nthe only finite arithmetic used in (6.1) and (7.4).\n\n## 9. A sharper rational endpoint comparison and a direct convergence rate\n\nContinue the scalar derivation in Sections 1--8. All arguments in those\nsections are reproduced in this joint candidate; C77 is not a predecessor\nfact. Define the following fixed positive integers from its saved certificate:\n\n    D80 = 1208925819614629174706176,\n    L3071 = 77954524439662110401113475160,\n    H3840 = 103225412920462918384305104511,\n    Lambda2up = 7046136038847924384392699.\n\nThey have the enclosing meanings\n\n    L3071/D80 <= B_3071,\n    B_3840 <= H3840/D80,\n    lambda^2 <= Lambda2up/D80.\n\nSet\n\n    Theta = Lambda2up*(H3840+19D80)/(D80*L3071).\n\nThe two exact integer comparisons needed below are\n\n    19299*L3071*D80-2500*Lambda2up*(H3840+19D80)\n      = 6267225362087608843943037675086081931840802435287340 > 0,\n    L3071-64000D80 = 583271984325843219918211160 > 0.\n\nThus\n\n    Theta < 19299/2500 = 193/25-1/2500,\n    B_3071 > 64000.                                      (9.1)\n\nThese are two fixed integer checks using the saved endpoints, not another\nrecurrence computation. Their output is in the public file\nresearch/c78_fixed_endpoint_audit.json. Section 7 now gives\n\n    Z_15/Z_3 < lambda^2*(B_3840+19)/B_3071\n              <= Theta < 19299/2500.                     (9.2)\n\nHere the initial strict inequality follows from B_3840<90000 and the\nstrict bound (sqrt(B_3840)+3/100)^2<B_3840+19. Both positive ray limits\nexist by Section 7. No theorem about a qualitative objective phase or its\nnonconstancy enters this argument.\n\nAn elementary global upper bound makes the convergence error explicit:\n\n    B_n <= 4P_n <= 4(n+1)^rho,  n>=0.                    (9.3)\n\nIndeed, for a,b>=0,\n\n    G(a,4b)=2a+2b+2sqrt(b^2+2ab)\n           <=2a+2b+2sqrt(a^2+6ab+b^2)=4Omega(a,b).\n\nThe initial bound B_0<=4P_0 holds. If it holds at every smaller index,\nthen each ORIGINAL dominant split obeys\n\n    G(P_k,B_l)<=G(P_k,4P_l)<=4Omega(P_k,P_l)<=4P_n\n\nwhen k+l=n-1. Maximizing gives the first assertion of (9.3). The second\nwas proved in Section 3. Balanced splitting is not assumed for B.\n\nFor every positive integer b and integer j>=0, put M=b*2^j and\n\n    E(b,j)=8sqrt(2)*b^(rho-1)*2^(-j)\n           +(3/25)*(b+1)^(rho/2)*lambda^(-j/2)\n           +(9/10000)*lambda^(-j).\n\nThen the enclosure in (7.3) implies the explicit bound\n\n    0 <= Z_b-lambda^(-j)B_(M-1) <= E(b,j).               (9.4)\n\nTo prove it, subtract the lower endpoint in (7.3) from its upper endpoint:\n\n    Z_b-lambda^(-j)B_(M-1)\n      <=lambda^(-j)[B_M-B_(M-1)+(3/50)sqrt(B_M)+9/10000].\n\nThe adjacent bound (3.2) gives B_M-B_(M-1)<=8sqrt(2)M^(rho-1).\nBy (9.3), sqrt(B_M)<=2(M+1)^(rho/2). Substitution, lambda=2^rho,\nand b+2^(-j)<=b+1 give exactly (9.4).\n\nIf d is a fixed nonnegative integer and M-1-d>=0, monotonicity and the\nsame adjacent bound give\n\n    0 <= B_(M-1)-B_(M-1-d) <= 8sqrt(2)*d*M^(rho-1).\n\nEvery increment on the left has its index at most M-1, which justifies\nusing the displayed common upper bound. Consequently\n\n    0 <= Z_b-lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1-d)\n      <= E(b,j)+8sqrt(2)*d*b^(rho-1)*2^(-j).             (9.5)\n\nIn particular this shifted sequence converges to the SAME Z_b. This\nsupplies the precise bounded-offset step needed for actual substitution\nwithout importing the source objective-phase theorem. For b ranging over\na finite set and d bounded by a fixed integer, the explicit bound is\nuniform. No conclusion that the error is zero at a finite index is made.\n\nThe next three sections reproduce and audit the full original-function\nseed and composition identities. They are part of this one candidate,\nnot uses of the unverified C75 draft as a mathematical building block.\n\n## 10. Full original-space seed condition\n\nFor a nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) denotes the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over every finite-dimensional\nEuclidean space, every convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient\nand a minimizer x_star, and every x_0!=x_star, under\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Here L>0, and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nNormalize L=1, translate a selected minimizer to zero, and subtract f_star\nso that its value is also zero. For a finite\nnonnegative word h of length r, let s=sum h_i, A=1+s, and retain every\nformal gradient g_0,...,g_r, including gradients at zero steps. Put\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)\n          +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_r||^2.\n\nEvery actual smooth convex trajectory has Q_ij<=0. We call the seed\nprimitive when V_h>=0 on every such trajectory in every finite dimension.\nA concrete sufficient certificate retains ALL ordered finite multipliers\nLambda_ij>=0, i!=j, and Lambda_ii=0. Let e be the all-ones column, and set\n\n    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,\n    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,\n    D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)).\n\nThe feasibility conditions are\n\n    L_flow^T e=omega,\n    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0.            (4)\n\nIndeed substituting the GD equations and collecting function coefficients\nand gradient inner products gives the exact identity\n\n    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.\n\nTo audit the matrix formula, the position terms of sum Lambda Q contribute\nsym(L_flow(T-I)); the squared-difference terms contribute\nsym(L_flow)-diag(omega)/2. Adding V_h's diagonal yields (4).\nThus (4) proves the required actual primitive inequality. It has no support,\nrank, symmetry, quotient, or terminal-edge restriction.\n\nAdding optimum multipliers cannot supply a missing positive x_0 diagonal:\nthat diagonal is zero. Its mixed coefficient with g_i is\n-Lambda_(i,star), so PSD forces each Lambda_(i,star)=0. Summing the finite\nfunction-flow equations then forces sum_i Lambda_(star,i)=0, and their\nnonnegativity makes all these multipliers zero. Therefore the original\nformal space (x_0,g_0,...,g_r) is retained, with its x_0 row zero.\nNo equality between formal gradients at duplicated query positions is\nimposed. For the implication used here, sufficiency of (4) is enough;\nno unproved dual attainment is invoked.\n\n## 11. Complete primitive and dominant composition identities\n\nPrimitive join: let a,b be primitive, with capacities A=1+sum a and\nC=1+sum b. Choose d>0 by d(A+C+d)=AC, put T_mass=A+C+d and\nkappa=(T_mass/C)^2, and use the physical word h=(a,1+d,b).\nLet p be its bridge query, z its terminal query, and j range over the\nnonterminal queries of b. Direct expansion gives\n\n    V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1) E_PP,\n    E_PP=sum_j b_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)+d Q_(p,z)\n                                      +sum_j b_j Q_(j,z).       (5)\n\nFor a coefficient audit, the value part of E_PP is\n2sum b_j f_j+(d-C)f_p+(2-C-d)f_z. Its gradient part is\n\n    sum_j b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2\n       +[(C-d)/2-d^2]||g_p||^2+(C+d)||g_z||^2/2.\n\nThe remaining coefficient identities are\n(kappa-1)(C-d)=2(A+d), kappa d^2=A^2, and kappa C^2=T_mass^2.\nThey follow from d T_mass=AC. Also 0<d<C and kappa>1, so all\nadded multipliers are nonnegative. If the child certificates have arbitrary\nPSD remainders, the full parent remainder is their original-coordinate sum\nM_a+kappa M_b, embedded on the actual child-gradient indices. The resulting\ncapacity is exactly Omega(A,C).\nThe physical bridge obeys 1+d=phi(A-1,C-1), as required by the recurrences in Section 1.\n\nDominant join: for a word h with terminal z, put D=2sum h+1. For\nnonnegative weights u_i summing to D define\n\n    J_(h,u)=||x_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2D f_z\n                                  -||x_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.    (6)\n\nThe empty word has u=1 and J=0. A primitive word is dominant with\nu=(h,s+1), since direct expansion gives J=V_h. In general J>=0 implies\n2f_z<=||x_0||^2/D on every actual trajectory.\n\nLet a be a primitive left child of capacity A and b a dominant right child\nwith mass D and weights v. Define\n\n    mu=2A/D,  zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2,\n    gamma=A+1,  beta=1+A/zeta.\n\nUse the physical parent word (a,beta,b), and weights u=(a,gamma,zeta v).\nTheir mass is G(A,D)=D zeta^2=2A+zeta D=2sum(parent)+1. With p the\nbridge query, all right-child queries i>p, including its terminal, give\n\n    J_parent=V_a+zeta^2 J_right\n                         -2mu sum_(i>p)v_i Q_(i,p).             (7)\n\nThe physical bridge is exactly beta=psi(A-1,(D-1)/2); multiply\nzeta^2-zeta=2A/D by D/zeta to check this against the radical in psi.\n\nThis is an equality on the full original formal space. Function\ncoefficients use mu D=2A. Expanding all squares and the GD bridge uses\n\n    (A-1)A+mu D+gamma=gamma^2,\n    zeta^2=zeta+mu,  zeta^2 beta=mu+gamma zeta;\n\nthese cancel the mixed terms and give (7). The child remainder is pulled\nback from the actual right-child starting position x_p-beta g_p; hence the\noriginal x_0 and all inherited gradients remain in the certificate.\nFor an additional coefficient check, put y=x_p-beta g_p,\nxi_i=x_i-y on the right child, and R_v=sum_(i>p)v_i g_i. Expanding\nJ_parent-V_a-zeta^2 J_right gives precisely\n\n    -2mu sum_i v_i(f_i-f_p)\n    -mu sum_i v_i||g_i||^2-mu D||g_p||^2\n    +2mu sum_i v_i<g_i,xi_i>-2mu(beta-1)<R_v,g_p>.\n\nIndeed the left-prefix displacement cancels between the position terms\nand the final square; its remaining diagonal is V_a's diagonal. The\nbridge diagonal difference is -2A=-mu D. The right-child local quadratic\nhas coefficient zeta-zeta^2=-mu, and the remaining bridge cross coefficient\nis 2zeta(beta-gamma)=-2mu(beta-1). As\n\n    Q_(i,p)=f_i-f_p+(beta-1)<g_i,g_p>-<g_i,xi_i>\n                         +(||g_i||^2+||g_p||^2)/2,\n\nthe displayed expansion is exactly the last term in (7). This also checks\nthe endpoint i=z and the empty-right-child case without deleting either.\nAll extra interpolation coefficients are nonnegative. Its PSD\nremainder is the retained final square plus the embedded child remainders,\nall divided by D. This is an all-function objective bound, not a bound on\na quadratic, projection or temporal subclass.\n\n## 12. The exact physical substitution and the untouched dominant leaf\n\nFix an ACTUAL seed a satisfying Section 10, of length r=m-1 and capacity\nA=1+sum a>=1.
